# Daily Summary: 2025-03-23

### SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMIRe -- Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15358v1)
- **Authors**: Thomas Pickard, Aline Villavicencio, Maggie Mi, Wei He, Dylan Phelps, Carolina Scarton, Marco Idiart
- **Abstract**: Idiomatic expressions present a unique challenge in NLP, as their meanings are often not directly inferable from their constituent words. Despite recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs), idiomaticity remains a significant obstacle to robust semantic representation. We present datasets and tasks for SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMiRe (Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation), which challenges the community to assess and improve models' ability to interpret idiomatic expressions in multimodal contexts and in multiple languages. Participants competed in two subtasks: ranking images based on their alignment with idiomatic or literal meanings, and predicting the next image in a sequence. The most effective methods achieved human-level performance by leveraging pretrained LLMs and vision-language models in mixture-of-experts settings, with multiple queries used to smooth over the weaknesses in these models' representations of idiomaticity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SemEval-2025 Task 1 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMiRe (Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation), which focuses on evaluating and improving NLP models' ability to understand idiomatic expressions in multimodal contexts and across multiple languages (English and Brazilian Portuguese). The task involves two subtasks: ranking images based on their alignment with idiomatic or literal meanings of a target noun compound, and predicting the next image in a sequence depicting an idiomatic expression.  The paper details the dataset construction process, involving selection of idiomatic expressions, sentence and image prompt creation (via crowdsourcing and the Midjourney model), and evaluation metrics (Top Image Accuracy, Discounted Cumulative Gain (DCG), Image Completion Accuracy, and Sentence Type Accuracy). The paper also provides a summary of the participating systems and their results. Finally, the authors also discuss the insights gained from the shared task competition, including the reliance on large language models (LLMs) and the challenges in handling the unique characteristics of idiomatic language.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its multimodal approach to idiomaticity understanding and evaluation. While previous work has explored idiomaticity in text, AdMiRe incorporates visual modalities (static images and image sequences) to provide a more holistic challenge to NLP models.  The inclusion of Brazilian Portuguese alongside English also adds to the novelty, albeit modestly, expanding the task beyond primarily English-centric research. The use of diffusion models for image generation, coupled with human expert verification is a good choice. The careful filtering to ensure dual meanings and imageability of items for use in static and sequential contexts is also a strong point. However, the core methodology for constructing the tasks is not entirely new, building upon established SemEval paradigms.  The task itself is interesting, but not revolutionary.

**Significance:**

The significance of AdMiRe lies in its potential to advance the state-of-the-art in idiomaticity understanding, a longstanding challenge in NLP.  By introducing visual modalities, the task pushes models beyond simply memorizing textual patterns and towards a deeper semantic comprehension of idioms. The task facilitates research into how LLMs and VLMs can be adapted to handle the intricacies of figurative language. The insights derived from the task (e.g., challenges in prompt engineering, biases in LLM outputs) provide valuable guidance for future research directions.  However, the dataset size is modest which may limit the generalizability of the findings. Also, the use of a single image generation model potentially introduces bias. Finally, while the study identifies challenges with LLMs (e.g., context-insensitive idiomatic interpretations), it doesn't offer fundamentally new theoretical or practical insights into *why* these problems occur or how to best address them besides refining prompts and using MoEs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Multimodal Focus:**  The incorporation of visual modalities is a key strength.
*   **Multilingual Aspect:**  The inclusion of Brazilian Portuguese broadens the scope of the task.
*   **Detailed Dataset Creation:** The paper provides a detailed description of the dataset construction process, ensuring reproducibility.
*   **Insightful Analysis:** The discussion of challenges faced by LLMs and participating systems offers valuable insights.
*   **Solid Methodology**: The framework for task creation is sound and aligns well with the goals.
*   **Well-motivated**: The need for this task is clearly articulated.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dataset Size:** The modest size of the dataset may limit the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Image Generation Model Bias:** Reliance on a single image generation model could introduce biases.
*   **Limited Novelty in Approach:** builds on past SemEval tasks, does not reinvent the wheel.
*   **Limited Scope**: The chosen nominal compounds may limit the scope of idiomatic understanding.
*   **Lacks Deeper Theoretical Insights**: The observed challenges of LLMs are not analyzed deeply theoretically.

**Overall:**

The AdMiRe task is a valuable contribution to the field of idiomaticity understanding in NLP. Its multimodal focus and insights into LLM behavior are significant. While the dataset size is a limitation and its novelty is somewhat incremental, it provides a solid framework for future research in this area.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**  The paper presents a worthwhile, well-executed, and reasonably novel task with solid experimental methodology. The strengths lie in the multimodal and multilingual aspects, as well as the careful dataset construction. The limitations include modest dataset size, the absence of new theoretical insights and a single image generation model that creates the images.  It advances the field but does not constitute a breakthrough innovation.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EfficientLLaVA:Generalizable Auto-Pruning for Large Vision-language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15369v1)
- **Authors**: Yinan Liang, Ziwei Wang, Xiuwei Xu, Jie Zhou, Jiwen Lu
- **Abstract**: While multimodal large language models demonstrate strong performance in complex reasoning tasks, they pose significant challenges related to model complexity during deployment, especially for resource-limited devices. In this paper, we propose an automatic pruning method for large vision-language models to enhance the efficiency of multimodal reasoning. Conventional methods rely on the training data of the original model to select the proper pruning ratio for different network components. However, these methods are impractical for large vision-language models due to the unaffordable search costs caused by web-scale training corpus. In contrast, our approach only leverages a small number of samples to search for the desired pruning policy by maximizing its generalization ability on unknown training data while maintaining the model accuracy, which enables the achievement of an optimal trade-off between accuracy and efficiency for large visual language models. Specifically, we formulate the generalization gap of the pruning strategy using the structural risk minimization principle. Based on both task performance and generalization capability, we iteratively search for the optimal pruning policy within a given search space and optimize the vision projector to evolve the search space with higher upper bound of performance. We conduct extensive experiments on the ScienceQA, Vizwiz, MM-vet, and LLaVA-Bench datasets for the task of visual question answering. Using only 64 samples for pruning policy search, EfficientLLaVA achieves an accuracy of 83.05% on ScienceQA, along with a $\times$ 1.8 speedup compared to the dense LLaVA-v1.5-7B model.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EfficientLLaVA: Generalizable Auto-Pruning for Large Vision-language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EfficientLLaVA, a novel automatic pruning method designed to reduce the complexity of large vision-language models (LVLMs) for deployment on resource-constrained devices. Unlike traditional pruning methods that rely heavily on large-scale training data for policy search (which is computationally expensive for LVLMs), EfficientLLaVA uses only a small set of proxy samples. It maximizes the generalization ability of the pruning policy to unseen data using the structural risk minimization (SRM) principle. The method iteratively searches for an optimal pruning policy and optimizes the vision projector to enhance the performance of evolved search space.  Experiments on ScienceQA, Vizwiz, MM-vet, and LLaVA-Bench datasets demonstrate that EfficientLLaVA achieves significant speedup while maintaining competitive accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its approach to addressing the computationally expensive pruning of large VLMs.  The combination of proxy data, the structural risk minimization (SRM) principle for generalization, iterative policy search, and vision projector optimization is a reasonably novel contribution. Previous methods either required large training datasets or did not sufficiently address the generalization issue when using limited data. The approach of evolving search space with the projector is also a new concept in this context.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical practical problem – deploying large VLMs on resource-limited devices. Reducing the model size and computational cost without significantly impacting accuracy is crucial for making these models accessible in real-world applications. Achieving good results with a small proxy dataset directly tackles the cost barrier associated with current pruning methods. The improvements shown on the diverse question-answering datasets are promising.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Relevance:**  The paper targets a real and increasingly important challenge in the field of VLMs.
    *   **Technical Approach:** The combination of techniques (SRM, proxy data, iterative search, vision projector optimization) is well-motivated and reasonably executed.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper provides empirical results on several datasets and compares against baselines, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method. Ablation studies highlight the contribution of key components.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and explains the approach clearly.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset for Pruning:** While the small size of the proxy set is a selling point, the paper could provide more analysis of the sensitivity of the pruning policy to the composition of the proxy set. What types of data in the proxy data lead to better generalization? It would be a great improvement if the proxy sample selection process could be improved.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of Generalization:** While the paper uses unseen data, more detailed analysis of the types of generalization achieved would improve the paper. For instance, is the model only generalizing within the same domain but using a different dataset, or can it generalize to drastically different data distributions and/or new modalities?
    *   **Baselines:**  While the paper uses LLM-Pruner and SparseGPT as baselines, comparing with other model compression method could improve the justification.
    *   **Inference Speed:** The paper provides some inference speedup result, but a more thorough benchmark would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and deployable VLMs. The use of SRM and proxy data for pruning could be adopted by other researchers in the field.
*   **Rigorousness:**  The paper shows sufficient empirical results, but there are still some gaps in the experimental design and explanation. A more thorough analysis of hyperparameter settings is required.

**Justification of Score:**

EfficientLLaVA introduces a practical and relevant solution to a critical problem in the field of VLMs.  The approach combines several existing techniques into a novel method tailored for large models with limited training data availability. While the paper has some limitations, such as the dataset and baselines mentioned above, it represents a valuable contribution with the potential to inspire further research.

### CCDP: Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies with Guided Sampling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15386v1)
- **Authors**: Amirreza Razmjoo, Sylvain Calinon, Michael Gienger, Fan Zhang
- **Abstract**: Imitation Learning offers a promising approach to learn directly from data without requiring explicit models, simulations, or detailed task definitions. During inference, actions are sampled from the learned distribution and executed on the robot. However, sampled actions may fail for various reasons, and simply repeating the sampling step until a successful action is obtained can be inefficient. In this work, we propose an enhanced sampling strategy that refines the sampling distribution to avoid previously unsuccessful actions. We demonstrate that by solely utilizing data from successful demonstrations, our method can infer recovery actions without the need for additional exploratory behavior or a high-level controller. Furthermore, we leverage the concept of diffusion model decomposition to break down the primary problem (which may require long-horizon history to manage failures) into multiple smaller, more manageable sub-problems in learning, data collection, and inference, thereby enabling the system to adapt to variable failure counts. Our approach yields a low-level controller that dynamically adjusts its sampling space to improve efficiency when prior samples fall short. We validate our method across several tasks, including door opening with unknown directions, object manipulation, and button-searching scenarios, demonstrating that our approach outperforms traditional baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CCDP: Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies with Guided Sampling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies (CCDP), a novel method designed to improve the robustness of imitation learning policies in the face of execution failures. It enhances a standard diffusion policy framework by conditioning it on key failure cases identified by a failure detection system.  The core idea is to guide the policy away from actions similar to previous failures.  Instead of repeatedly sampling from the same distribution after a failed attempt, CCDP dynamically adjusts the sampling space by composing multiple diffusion models, each specialized to address individual failure events.  This enables the robot to explore alternative actions observed in successful demonstrations, including infrequent ones, without requiring additional exploration data or a high-level controller. The approach is validated across various tasks, demonstrating improved success rates and performance relative to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the specific application of compositional diffusion models to address failure recovery in imitation learning.  While diffusion policies are not new, and compositional models exist, the combination and specific conditioning strategy presented here offer a practical and intuitive approach to a well-known challenge. The idea of using failure cases as "negative guidance" to steer the policy away from unproductive areas is a clever adaptation. The assumption that recovery actions exist within the successful demonstration set simplifies the problem and makes it tractable. The proposed recovery mechanism by synthesising failed actions via the auxiliary dataset is an interesting idea.
* **Significance:** The problem of handling failures in imitation learning is highly relevant.  The proposed method offers a practical solution that does not require complex environment modeling, extensive exploration, or explicit cost function design.  The potential impact is significant for real-world robotic applications where these resources are often limited.  The modular decomposition of the problem also makes the learning process more manageable and scalable. It also addresses long action histories, making the approach more viable for more complex tasks.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Practicality:** The method is designed for real-world scenarios where only successful demonstrations are available.
    * **Efficiency:** CCDP avoids the inefficiencies of repeated sampling and rejection sampling.
    * **Modularity:**  The compositional approach allows for independent learning of recovery strategies for individual failure events.
    * **Adaptability:**  The system can adapt to variable failure counts without retraining the entire model.
    * **Strong Experimental Validation:**  The experiments are well-designed, cover a range of robotic tasks, and include comparisons to relevant baselines. The tasks also go beyond pure failure recovery, showing the usefulness of the approach.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Assumption of Static Failure Causes:** The method assumes that the underlying cause of failure remains constant over time, limiting its applicability in dynamic environments.
    * **Manual Definition of Failure Key Features:** The process of defining the function z(.) to extract key features of failed actions is currently manual and could benefit from automation.
    * **Dependence on Successful Demonstrations:** The method still relies on a set of successful demonstrations, which may not always be available in all scenarios.
    * **Limited Exploration of Weight Optimization:** While the paper explores the use of combination weights, a more systematic approach to optimizing these weights could further improve performance.
    * **Lack of theoretical analysis:** While the empirical results are strong, the work lacks theoretical guarantees regarding its convergence and stability.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-validated approach to addressing a significant challenge in imitation learning. While the assumptions of static failure causes and manual feature definition are limiting factors, the practical benefits of CCDP, coupled with its modularity and adaptability, make it a valuable contribution. The clear improvements demonstrated in the experimental results further support this assessment. The weaknesses are acknowledged and future work is outlined, showing a clear path for further improvement.

### Improving Adversarial Transferability on Vision Transformers via Forward Propagation Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15404v1)
- **Authors**: Yuchen Ren, Zhengyu Zhao, Chenhao Lin, Bo Yang, Lu Zhou, Zhe Liu, Chao Shen
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) have been widely applied in various computer vision and vision-language tasks. To gain insights into their robustness in practical scenarios, transferable adversarial examples on ViTs have been extensively studied. A typical approach to improving adversarial transferability is by refining the surrogate model. However, existing work on ViTs has restricted their surrogate refinement to backward propagation. In this work, we instead focus on Forward Propagation Refinement (FPR) and specifically refine two key modules of ViTs: attention maps and token embeddings. For attention maps, we propose Attention Map Diversification (AMD), which diversifies certain attention maps and also implicitly imposes beneficial gradient vanishing during backward propagation. For token embeddings, we propose Momentum Token Embedding (MTE), which accumulates historical token embeddings to stabilize the forward updates in both the Attention and MLP blocks. We conduct extensive experiments with adversarial examples transferred from ViTs to various CNNs and ViTs, demonstrating that our FPR outperforms the current best (backward) surrogate refinement by up to 7.0\% on average. We also validate its superiority against popular defenses and its compatibility with other transfer methods. Codes and appendix are available at https://github.com/RYC-98/FPR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach, Forward Propagation Refinement (FPR), to improve the transferability of adversarial examples generated for Vision Transformers (ViTs).  Existing methods primarily focus on refining the backward propagation (gradient information) of the surrogate model used to generate adversarial examples. In contrast, FPR focuses on refining the *forward* propagation by modifying attention maps and token embeddings. Specifically, FPR comprises two key modules: Attention Map Diversification (AMD), which introduces randomness into attention maps to prevent overfitting, and Momentum Token Embedding (MTE), which stabilizes token embeddings during adversarial example generation by accumulating historical information.  Experimental results demonstrate that FPR outperforms existing backward propagation refinement methods and enhances the transferability of adversarial examples from ViTs to various CNNs and ViTs, even against defenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of refining the *forward* propagation instead of just the backward propagation for improving adversarial transferability is relatively novel, especially within the context of ViTs. Prior work has largely concentrated on manipulating gradients. The two specific modules proposed (AMD and MTE) are also novel in their application to this problem. AMD's approach of injecting moderate randomness contrasts with more aggressive techniques like dropout. MTE's inspiration from momentum distillation offers a unique perspective.

*   **Significance:** Improving the transferability of adversarial examples is significant because it highlights vulnerabilities in models even when attacks are generated on different (unseen) architectures. Demonstrating that FPR achieves higher transferability rates compared to the state-of-the-art (SOTA) is valuable for the field of adversarial robustness and security. The findings can help in developing more robust models and understanding the underlying mechanisms of adversarial transferability. The demonstrated compatibility with other transfer-based attacks indicates the potential to create even more effective attacks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly identifies a gap in the existing research (i.e., limited focus on forward propagation refinement).
    *   The proposed FPR method is well-motivated and explained, with clear descriptions of AMD and MTE.
    *   The paper includes a theoretical justification for AMD's impact on backward propagation.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, including comparisons with SOTA methods, evaluations against different defenses, and compatibility studies.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of individual components (AMD and MTE).
    *   The parameter sensitivity analysis justifies the parameter choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The theoretical justification for AMD, while present, could be more rigorous and in-depth. The connection between diversified attention maps and gradient vanishing is implied rather than explicitly proven.
    *   While the paper mentions that dropout is too aggressive, a more detailed analysis or controlled comparison between AMD and different dropout rates would strengthen this claim.
    *   The sensitivity of index selection can be better explored. A more systematic approach, beyond simply removing and adding, could improve the selection process.
    *   Visualizations of how AMD and MTE change feature representations or gradients would provide a stronger understanding.

*   **Potential Impact:** This paper has the potential to influence the direction of future research on adversarial transferability. By highlighting the importance of forward propagation, it encourages researchers to explore new techniques for refining model representations during adversarial example generation.  The specific techniques (AMD and MTE) may also be adopted or adapted by others in the field. Ultimately, these developments could lead to a deeper understanding of adversarial vulnerabilities and more robust models.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper introduces a clearly novel approach to the problem of adversarial transferability, and the experimental results support the efficacy of their proposed method. While the theoretical justification could be stronger and additional analyses would strengthen the arguments, the paper presents a solid and valuable contribution to the field.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Visual Persona: Foundation Model for Full-Body Human Customization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15406v1)
- **Authors**: Jisu Nam, Soowon Son, Zhan Xu, Jing Shi, Difan Liu, Feng Liu, Aashish Misraa, Seungryong Kim, Yang Zhou
- **Abstract**: We introduce Visual Persona, a foundation model for text-to-image full-body human customization that, given a single in-the-wild human image, generates diverse images of the individual guided by text descriptions. Unlike prior methods that focus solely on preserving facial identity, our approach captures detailed full-body appearance, aligning with text descriptions for body structure and scene variations. Training this model requires large-scale paired human data, consisting of multiple images per individual with consistent full-body identities, which is notoriously difficult to obtain. To address this, we propose a data curation pipeline leveraging vision-language models to evaluate full-body appearance consistency, resulting in Visual Persona-500K, a dataset of 580k paired human images across 100k unique identities. For precise appearance transfer, we introduce a transformer encoder-decoder architecture adapted to a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model, which augments the input image into distinct body regions, encodes these regions as local appearance features, and projects them into dense identity embeddings independently to condition the diffusion model for synthesizing customized images. Visual Persona consistently surpasses existing approaches, generating high-quality, customized images from in-the-wild inputs. Extensive ablation studies validate design choices, and we demonstrate the versatility of Visual Persona across various downstream tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Visual Persona: Foundation Model for Full-Body Human Customization":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Visual Persona, a foundation model for text-to-image generation specifically designed for full-body human customization. Given a single in-the-wild image of a person, the model can generate diverse variations of that person guided by text descriptions while preserving their unique full-body appearance. This is achieved through a novel architecture that decomposes the input image into body regions, encodes them with a transformer encoder, and then uses a body-partitioned transformer decoder to project these features into identity embeddings, which then guides the pre-trained diffusion model. A key contribution is a new, large-scale paired dataset, Visual Persona-500K, of human images with consistent full-body identities, created through a curation pipeline leveraging vision-language models (VLMs). The paper demonstrates that Visual Persona outperforms existing methods in generating high-quality, customized images with accurate appearance transfer and text alignment, and its versatility across various downstream tasks such as text-guided virtual try-on, human stylization, and character customization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several aspects:

    *   **Full-Body Focus:** It extends the scope of personalized image generation beyond just faces to the full body, an area that hasn't been extensively explored.
    *   **Visual Persona-500K Dataset:** The creation of a large-scale paired dataset of diverse human images with consistent identities addresses a critical limitation in the field. The use of VLMs for data curation is an innovative approach to building such a dataset.
    *   **Transformer Encoder-Decoder Architecture:** The body-partitioned transformer encoder-decoder, specifically adapted for a pre-trained T2I diffusion model, shows a strong design for transferring each body part into the complex body structure of the customized images.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of personalized image generation with potential impact:

    *   **Improved Identity Preservation:** The model's ability to accurately preserve full-body appearance while manipulating other attributes is a significant advancement, opening possibilities for applications where consistent identity is crucial.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The showcased applications, such as virtual try-on, stylization, and character customization, demonstrate the practicality of the model and its potential to be applied in various fields including e-commerce, entertainment, and social media.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The Visual Persona-500K dataset acts as a valuable resource for future research in full-body customization and personalized image generation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **High-Quality Results:** The qualitative and quantitative results clearly demonstrate the superiority of Visual Persona over existing methods in preserving identity and aligning with text descriptions.
    *   **Extensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component of the architecture, validating the design choices.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is clearly written and well-structured, making it easy to understand the proposed method and its contributions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The model relies on pre-trained models (SDXL, DINOv2, LLAVA, face recognition model, body parsing model), which may limit its flexibility and introduce biases.
    *   **Complexity:** The architecture is relatively complex, involving a body parsing step and a transformer encoder-decoder, which may be computationally expensive.
    *   **Failure Cases:** The current model has issues like generating inaccurate body proportions and being influenced by unwanted identity-unrelated attributes in the input.
*   **Limitations discussion:** The limitations are openly discussed and the authors have suggested possible ways to address these limitations such as refined foreground masks for separating each part of the human body for more accurate isolations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in personalized image generation, particularly in extending personalization to the full body. The Visual Persona-500K dataset is likely to become a benchmark for evaluating full-body customization methods. The architecture and training approach could inspire new approaches to appearance transfer and text alignment.

**Justification of Score:**

The Visual Persona paper is a significant contribution to personalized image generation. The extension to full-body personalization, the introduction of a large-scale paired dataset, and the effective use of VLMs in the curation process represent substantial advancements. The model's performance, demonstrated through rigorous experiments, is impressive. While the reliance on pre-trained models and the complexity of the architecture are valid concerns, the paper's overall impact and potential influence warrant a high score.

### Visual Position Prompt for MLLM based Visual Grounding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15426v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Tang, Yanpeng Sun, Qinying Gu, Zechao Li
- **Abstract**: Although Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) excel at various image-related tasks, they encounter challenges in precisely aligning coordinates with spatial information within images, particularly in position-aware tasks such as visual grounding. This limitation arises from two key factors. First, MLLMs lack explicit spatial references, making it difficult to associate textual descriptions with precise image locations. Second, their feature extraction processes prioritize global context over fine-grained spatial details, leading to weak localization capability. To address this issue, we introduce VPP-LLaVA, an MLLM equipped with Visual Position Prompt (VPP) to improve its grounding capability. VPP-LLaVA integrates two complementary mechanisms. The global VPP overlays learnable, axis-like embeddings onto the input image to provide structured spatial cues. The local VPP focuses on fine-grained localization by incorporating position-aware queries, which suggests probable object locations. We also introduce a VPP-SFT dataset with 0.6M samples, consolidating high-quality visual grounding data into a compact format for efficient model training. Training on this dataset with VPP enhances the model's performance, achieving state-of-the-art results on standard grounding benchmarks despite using fewer training samples compared to other MLLMs like MiniGPT-v2, which rely on much larger datasets ($\sim$21M samples). The code and VPP-SFT dataset will be available at https://github.com/WayneTomas/VPP-LLaVA upon acceptance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces VPP-LLaVA, a Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) designed to improve visual grounding performance, particularly in tasks requiring precise spatial understanding. The key innovation is the Visual Position Prompt (VPP), comprising: 1) a global VPP that overlays learnable, axis-like embeddings onto the input image to provide structured spatial cues, and 2) a local VPP that incorporates position-aware queries to suggest probable object locations. To train and evaluate the model, the authors also create a VPP-SFT dataset with 0.6M samples. Experiments demonstrate that VPP-LLaVA achieves state-of-the-art results on standard grounding benchmarks, despite using fewer training samples compared to other MLLMs like MiniGPT-v2, which relies on much larger datasets.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating positional references as prompts within MLLMs to enhance visual grounding is a valuable contribution. The decomposition of the prompt into global (axis-like) and local (object-specific) components is also innovative. The creation of the VPP-SFT dataset, while leveraging existing data, contributes to making the training process more efficient and focused. However, the paper draws inspiration from other related works for visual prompting. For instance, it mentioned that this work is inspired by "[26], [58], [27], introduce a learnable global Visual Position Prompt" which may reduce its novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical limitation of MLLMs in accurately aligning visual coordinates with spatial information. The improved grounding capabilities can benefit various applications like image segmentation, remote sensing, and human-robot interaction. The fact that the proposed approach achieves strong results with a smaller dataset is highly significant, making it more practical and resource-efficient. The ablation studies provide insights into the individual contributions of the global and local VPPs, along with other architectural components.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed architecture and training strategy.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation with multiple benchmarks.
    *   Detailed ablation studies.
    *   Strong performance with a relatively small training dataset.
    *   Qualitative results that provide useful insights.
    *   Ablation study of variants of Global VPP Initialization to evaluate how different initial axes images affect the grounding performance of the model

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper relies heavily on existing components like CLIP and DETR.
    *   The method is not without failure, as some scenarios are shown in the paper, such as queries related to complex relationships or negation.
    *   The reliance on LLaVA's coordinate representation is dataset-specific and might not easily generalize.
    *   The paper claims it is a pure MLLM, but the DETR generator still generates bounding box proposals.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in visual grounding by:

    *   Providing a strong baseline for position-aware grounding with MLLMs.
    *   Encouraging the development of more efficient training strategies for MLLMs.
    *   Inspiring new techniques for integrating spatial information into MLLMs.
    *   The findings of the study, particularly with respect to the impact of various initialization strategies for the VPP and how masking affects performance, could inform the design of future models that leverage spatial prompting.
    *   Highlighting the importance of the quality of the training data and making the code/data public.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of visual grounding by introducing the VPP-LLaVA model. The proposed VPP, particularly the combination of global and local prompts, offers a significant improvement over existing MLLMs in terms of spatial awareness and grounding accuracy. The emphasis on data efficiency and the detailed ablation studies make this work practically relevant and insightful. However, the reliance on existing components, the inability to handle complex prompts, and the somewhat limited generalization capabilities to coordinate representation reduce the impact score compared to works with truly radical and groundbreaking innovations. The strong performance with a relatively small dataset elevates the score, especially in the current landscape of ever-increasing dataset sizes for MLLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15451v1)
- **Authors**: Lixing Xiao, Shunlin Lu, Huaijin Pi, Ke Fan, Liang Pan, Yueer Zhou, Ziyong Feng, Xiaowei Zhou, Sida Peng, Jingbo Wang
- **Abstract**: This paper addresses the challenge of text-conditioned streaming motion generation, which requires us to predict the next-step human pose based on variable-length historical motions and incoming texts. Existing methods struggle to achieve streaming motion generation, e.g., diffusion models are constrained by pre-defined motion lengths, while GPT-based methods suffer from delayed response and error accumulation problem due to discretized non-causal tokenization. To solve these problems, we propose MotionStreamer, a novel framework that incorporates a continuous causal latent space into a probabilistic autoregressive model. The continuous latents mitigate information loss caused by discretization and effectively reduce error accumulation during long-term autoregressive generation. In addition, by establishing temporal causal dependencies between current and historical motion latents, our model fully utilizes the available information to achieve accurate online motion decoding. Experiments show that our method outperforms existing approaches while offering more applications, including multi-round generation, long-term generation, and dynamic motion composition. Project Page: https://zju3dv.github.io/MotionStreamer/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MotionStreamer, a novel framework for streaming text-conditioned motion generation. It addresses the limitations of existing diffusion and GPT-based approaches by incorporating a continuous causal latent space within a probabilistic autoregressive model.  The key components include: (1) an autoregressive model predicting motion latents conditioned on text and historical motions; (2) a causal temporal autoencoder (Causal TAE) for continuous motion compression and online decoding; and (3) strategies to mitigate error accumulation in streaming generation. They show improved performance on text-to-motion and long-term motion synthesis tasks, as well as demonstrate downstream applications like multi-round generation, long-term generation, and dynamic motion composition.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty and Significance:*

The paper addresses a practical and important problem: streaming motion generation. The novelty lies primarily in the architecture and training strategies:

*   **Continuous Causal Latent Space:**  Moving away from discrete tokens (VQ-VAE) to a continuous latent space is a reasonable improvement, potentially reducing information loss and error accumulation. However, this is not a radical departure, as other continuous latent space methods exist. The Causal TAE is a good design choice, but the benefits may not be extremely significant compared to standard VAEs with similar capacity.
*   **Two-Forward Training and Mixed Training:** These training strategies aim to address the exposure bias inherent in autoregressive models. While potentially effective, such techniques are common in sequence generation and don't represent a groundbreaking contribution.
*   **Streaming Motion Generation:** While there are other autoregressive motion generation frameworks available, this does address limitations of prior methods.

The significance is in improved results and demonstrating practical applications. The multi-round generation and dynamic motion composition are compelling use cases, showcasing the potential of the framework. However, the quantitative gains over baselines, while present, are not overwhelmingly large, implying that other factors might be at play.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of streaming motion generation and the shortcomings of existing methods.
*   **Well-Structured Architecture:**  The individual components of MotionStreamer (autoregressive model, Causal TAE, training strategies) are logically connected and well-explained.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are thorough, covering various benchmarks and ablation studies, providing evidence for the effectiveness of different components.
*   **Practical Applications:** Demonstrating the use of the framework in multi-round generation, long-term generation, and dynamic motion composition adds practical value.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the approach is technically sound, the individual components (VAE, autoregressive models, specific training strategies) have been explored in other contexts.  The novelty lies in the integration, rather than a fundamentally new concept.
*   **Quantitative Gains:** The improvements over baselines, while present, are not dramatic.  This suggests that the benefits of the proposed techniques might be marginal.
*   **Limited Discussion on Latency:** While first-frame latency is reported, a deeper discussion on the trade-offs between generation quality, computational complexity, and real-time performance would be valuable.
*   **Lack of comparison to state of the art interactive methods:** Need to compare more head to head with methods like CAMDM, ready-to-react, and other streaming motion generation work.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper could influence future research in streaming motion generation and related areas. The Causal TAE and the proposed training strategies may inspire other researchers working with autoregressive models and continuous latent spaces. However, its impact may be limited if the quantitative gains do not translate into substantially better real-world applications.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of text-conditioned motion generation by addressing the practical problem of streaming generation. It presents a well-designed framework with clear improvements over existing approaches. However, the novelty is primarily in the integration of existing techniques, and the quantitative gains, while present, are not overwhelming. Also, limitations related to comparison with other relevant works reduces the overall quality of this paper. Therefore, a score of 6 is assigned.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O: Distilling Masked Diffusion Models into One-step Generator
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15457v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanzhi Zhu, Xi Wang, Stéphane Lathuilière, Vicky Kalogeiton
- **Abstract**: Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) have emerged as a powerful generative modeling technique. Despite their remarkable results, they typically suffer from slow inference with several steps. In this paper, we propose Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O, a novel approach that distills masked diffusion models into a one-step generator. Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O addresses two key challenges: (1) the intractability of using intermediate-step information for one-step generation, which we solve through token-level distribution matching that optimizes model output logits by an 'on-policy framework' with the help of an auxiliary model; and (2) the lack of entropy in the initial distribution, which we address through a token initialization strategy that injects randomness while maintaining similarity to teacher training distribution. We show Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O's effectiveness on both class-conditional and text-conditional image generation, impressively achieving performance competitive to multi-step teacher outputs while drastically reducing inference time. To our knowledge, we are the first to successfully achieve one-step distillation of masked diffusion models and the first to apply discrete distillation to text-to-image generation, opening new paths for efficient generative modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Di[M]O: Distilling Masked Diffusion Models into One-step Generator":

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the challenge of slow inference speeds in Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) by proposing Di[M]O, a novel approach for distilling MDMs into a one-step generator. The core technical contributions are: (1) a method to approximate the training objective by using pseudo-intermediate states with an auxiliary model to surrogate gradients, thereby tackling the intractability of using intermediate-step information for one-step generation; and (2) a token initialization strategy that injects randomness into the initial masked state while maintaining similarity to the teacher training distribution, effectively addressing the problem of low entropy in MDMs and mode collapse.  The authors demonstrate Di[M]O's effectiveness on both class-conditional and text-conditional image generation tasks, achieving performance comparable to the multi-step teacher models but with significantly reduced inference time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in being the first to successfully achieve one-step distillation of masked diffusion models. Previous distillation efforts have focused on continuous diffusion models or required multiple distillation rounds, which are computationally expensive. The proposed Di[M]O tackles MDM's unique challenges, namely using intermediate step information for one-step generation and lack of entropy in the initialization process. The token-level distribution matching and token initialization strategies are also novel contributions within the context of MDM distillation. They build upon, but significantly adapt and extend existing distillation techniques (e.g., on-policy distillation, variational score distillation) for a new type of generative model.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the improved efficiency it brings to MDMs. Faster inference times unlock the potential for wider adoption of MDMs in applications like real-time image generation, content creation, and other areas where rapid generation is critical. Moreover, the ability to distill MDMs into one-step generators opens doors for further research into efficient generative modeling using discrete data representations, which is particularly relevant in multimodal contexts.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing MDMs and the challenges in applying standard distillation techniques.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The proposed Di[M]O approach is well-motivated and technically sound, with clear explanations of the proposed token-level distribution matching and the initialization strategies.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on both class-conditional image generation (MaskGit) and text-conditional image generation (Meissonic), demonstrating the effectiveness of Di[M]O. The ablation studies provide insights into the key components and hyperparameter choices.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors have released the code and results for their experiments and mention many hyperparameter settings, increasing the liklihood of reproduciblity.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Teacher Models:** While the results are promising, the experiments are primarily focused on MaskGit and Meissonic. Expanding the experiments to include more diverse and larger MDM architectures would further strengthen the claims.
    *   **Complexity of the auxiliary model**: There is a significant overhead added through requiring the auxiliary model in addition to the student.
    *   **Performance Gap**: there still remains a small performance gap in image quality as measured by FIDs and other metrics between multi-step teachers and one-step distillations.

*   **Potential Impact:** Di[M]O has the potential to significantly impact the field of generative modeling by enabling efficient inference with MDMs. It provides a solid foundation for future research in this area, potentially leading to more efficient distillation methods, better initialization strategies, and wider applications of MDMs. It can be extended for faster image editing or video generation techniques.

*   **Justification of Score:**
    The paper presents a novel and significant contribution by introducing the first successful one-step distillation method for masked diffusion models. While there are some limitations in the scope of the experiments and potential complexity (overhead of the auxiliary model) and performance gap it remains a valuable contribution to the generation and the acceleration of existing techniques.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### From 1,000,000 Users to Every User: Scaling Up Personalized Preference for User-level Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15463v1)
- **Authors**: Jia-Nan Li, Jian Guan, Songhao Wu, Wei Wu, Rui Yan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have traditionally been aligned through one-size-fits-all approaches that assume uniform human preferences, fundamentally overlooking the diversity in user values and needs. This paper introduces a comprehensive framework for scalable personalized alignment of LLMs. We establish a systematic preference space characterizing psychological and behavioral dimensions, alongside diverse persona representations for robust preference inference in real-world scenarios. Building upon this foundation, we introduce \textsc{AlignX}, a large-scale dataset of over 1.3 million personalized preference examples, and develop two complementary alignment approaches: \textit{in-context alignment} directly conditioning on persona representations and \textit{preference-bridged alignment} modeling intermediate preference distributions. Extensive experiments demonstrate substantial improvements over existing methods, with an average 17.06\% accuracy gain across four benchmarks while exhibiting a strong adaptation capability to novel preferences, robustness to limited user data, and precise preference controllability. These results validate our framework's effectiveness, advancing toward truly user-adaptive AI systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper tackles the problem of aligning Large Language Models (LLMs) with diverse human preferences, moving beyond the traditional "one-size-fits-all" alignment approach. It introduces a framework for personalized LLM alignment, based on a comprehensive preference space that incorporates psychological and behavioral dimensions. The key components are:

1.  **Preference Space:** A structured space capturing individual preference variations, grounded in psychological theories and reflecting both fundamental needs and topical interests.
2.  **ALIGNX Dataset:** A large-scale dataset of over 1.3 million personalized preference examples derived from forum interactions, capturing persona-preference relationships. Personas are constructed from both behavioral and descriptive data.
3.  **ALIGNXPERT Models:** LLMs trained using ALIGNX, employing two alignment approaches:
    *   *In-Context Alignment (ICA)*: Directly conditions on persona representations within the context window.
    *   *Preference-Bridged Alignment (PBA)*: Maps personas to explicit preference distributions to guide response generation.

The paper demonstrates that ALIGNXPERT models significantly outperform existing methods on personalized alignment benchmarks, exhibiting strong adaptation to unseen preferences, robustness to limited user data, and precise preference controllability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM alignment by addressing the critical gap of personalized preferences. Previous methods have largely focused on a universal set of values, neglecting the diversity of human needs and desires. The systematic approach to constructing a preference space and the creation of the ALIGNX dataset is a significant step forward. The ICA and PBA models are sensible applications of the data, offering complementary approaches to personalize generation.
*   **Significance:** The shift towards personalized alignment is crucial for LLMs to effectively serve diverse user populations. Addressing this diversity mitigates biases and improves user satisfaction. The results demonstrating superior adaptation, robustness, and controllability highlight the practical benefits of the proposed framework. Furthermore, the interpretability afforded by the Preference-Bridged Alignment approach is a notable contribution.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The comprehensive preference space grounded in psychological theories adds depth and richness to the alignment process.
    *   The large-scale ALIGNX dataset addresses the scarcity of personalized preference data, enabling effective training of personalized LLMs.
    *   The strong empirical results demonstrate significant improvements in preference alignment accuracy compared to existing methods.
    *   The exploration of two complementary alignment approaches (ICA and PBA) provides valuable insights into personalized LLM training.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper relies on forum data for constructing the ALIGNX dataset, which might introduce biases inherent to online forums. This aspect requires further scrutiny and mitigation strategies.
    *   The definition of "ground truth" preferences based on forum interactions could be noisy, as the user's true preferences might not be accurately reflected in their online behavior. The authors acknowledge that the step of inferring directions from user behavior is itself a bottleneck. While clever, deriving "true" preference directions from online behaviors (or a combination of stated and observed) is a limitation.
    *   The paper acknowledges a trade-off in that in-context alignment has the problem of vulnerability to overfitting, suggesting limitations in generalizability.
    *   The method for converting preference directions to natural language for PВА, while systematic, is somewhat simplistic. More sophisticated natural language generation techniques could potentially improve the effectiveness of preference-guided generation.

*   **Potential Impact:** This paper has the potential to significantly influence the direction of LLM alignment research, shifting the focus towards personalized models that better cater to individual user preferences. The proposed framework and dataset can serve as valuable resources for the community, enabling further exploration and development of personalized AI systems. The ethical considerations raised in the Impact Statement are crucial for responsible deployment of such technologies.

*   **Room for Improvement:** Future work could explore more sophisticated methods for inferring user preferences, incorporating diverse data sources beyond forum interactions and addressing the biases inherent to those sources. Furthermore, developing more adaptive and generalizable alignment approaches that overcome the overfitting limitations of ICA is important. The development of metrics that better measure nuanced alignment is also needed.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper makes a significant, practical contribution by tackling personalized LLM alignment, an area of increasing importance. The strengths of its methodology and the empirical results are compelling. However, the limitations regarding data sources, "ground truth" inference, and the potential for bias prevent a higher score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FP4DiT: Towards Effective Floating Point Quantization for Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15465v1)
- **Authors**: Ruichen Chen, Keith G. Mills, Di Niu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Models (DM) have revolutionized the text-to-image visual generation process. However, the large computational cost and model footprint of DMs hinders practical deployment, especially on edge devices. Post-training quantization (PTQ) is a lightweight method to alleviate these burdens without the need for training or fine-tuning. While recent DM PTQ methods achieve W4A8 on integer-based PTQ, two key limitations remain: First, while most existing DM PTQ methods evaluate on classical DMs like Stable Diffusion XL, 1.5 or earlier, which use convolutional U-Nets, newer Diffusion Transformer (DiT) models like the PixArt series, Hunyuan and others adopt fundamentally different transformer backbones to achieve superior image synthesis. Second, integer (INT) quantization is prevailing in DM PTQ but doesn't align well with the network weight and activation distribution, while Floating-Point Quantization (FPQ) is still under-investigated, yet it holds the potential to better align the weight and activation distributions in low-bit settings for DiT. In response, we introduce FP4DiT, a PTQ method that leverages FPQ to achieve W4A6 quantization. Specifically, we extend and generalize the Adaptive Rounding PTQ technique to adequately calibrate weight quantization for FPQ and demonstrate that DiT activations depend on input patch data, necessitating robust online activation quantization techniques. Experimental results demonstrate that FP4DiT outperforms integer-based PTQ at W4A6 and W4A8 precision and generates convincing visual content on PixArt-$\alpha$, PixArt-$\Sigma$ and Hunyuan in terms of several T2I metrics such as HPSv2 and CLIP.
- **Summary**: **Concise Summary:** The paper presents FP4DiT, a post-training quantization (PTQ) method aimed at optimizing floating-point quantization for Diffusion Transformers (DiT), which are currently underrepresented in existing quantization frameworks primarily focused on traditional convolutional diffusion models. Recognizing the limitations of integer-based quantization methods, FP4DiT employs floating-point quantization (FPQ) to achieve effective weight and activation distribution alignment for low-bit settings, specifically targeting W4A6 quantization. By extending Adaptive Rounding PTQ, the proposed method successfully calibrates weight quantization and addresses the need for robust online activation quantization techniques based on input patch data. The experimental findings indicate that FP4DiT surpasses integer-based PTQ methods in terms of image synthesis quality across several Diffusion Transformers, affirming its effectiveness and potential for practical deployment in resource-constrained environments. --- **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty and Contribution:** The paper’s contribution lies in its novel approach to diffusion model quantization, specifically targeting the newer Diffusion Transformers rather than sticking solely to traditional methods meant for U-Net based models. The use of floating-point quantization represents a significant shift in the methodology of applying PTQ, suggesting an understanding that integer quantization may not always be optimal, especially for complex models like DiTs.  **Strengths:** 1. **Original Approach**: The shift to FPQ broadens the scope of quantization techniques available for cutting-edge models in text-to-image generation. 2. **Experimental Validation**: The results demonstrate clear advantages of FP4DiT over previous integer-based methods, suggesting practical applicability and effectiveness. 3. **Addressing Key Limitations**: By recognizing the discrepancies in activation and weight distributions, the paper successfully addresses an important challenge in quantization approaches. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope of Evaluation**: While results are promising, the paper primarily focuses on a limited number of models (e.g., PixArt-$\alpha$, PixArt-$\Sigma$, Hunyuan). A broader set of comparisons across various diffusion model architectures would strengthen the arguments. 2. **Potential Overlook of Training Complexity**: While the method aims to keep the quantization post-training, any reliance on adaptive techniques could arguably complicate deployment even further in certain scenarios, particularly if extensive calibration is required during implementation. 3. **Quantitative Comparisons**: The paper could benefit from more robust comparative analyses, establishing how FP4DiT may stack against not just existing integer PTQ methods, but also its runtime and resource efficiency during inference. **Overall Impact:** The paper proposes a relevant improvement in the burgeoning field of diffusion models and brings attention to the need for advanced quantization techniques tailored to contemporary architectures. Considering the importance of deploying efficient models in real-world applications, FP4DiT has the potential to influence further research in adaptive quantization strategies. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the paper's significant contribution to the field, but also acknowledges some limitations in scope and depth of evaluation that prevent it from being an outstanding, groundbreaking work.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Cube: A Roblox View of 3D Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15475v1)
- **Authors**: Foundation AI Team, Kiran Bhat, Nishchaie Khanna, Karun Channa, Tinghui Zhou, Yiheng Zhu, Xiaoxia Sun, Charles Shang, Anirudh Sudarshan, Maurice Chu, Daiqing Li, Kangle Deng, Jean-Philippe Fauconnier, Tijmen Verhulsdonck, Maneesh Agrawala, Kayvon Fatahalian, Alexander Weiss, Christian Reiser, Ravi Kiran Chirravuri, Ravali Kandur, Alejandro Pelaez, Akash Garg, Michael Palleschi, Jessica Wang, Skylar Litz, Leon Liu, Anying Li, David Harmon, Derek Liu, Liangjun Feng, Denis Goupil, Lukas Kuczynski, Jihyun Yoon, Naveen Marri, Peiye Zhuang, Yinan Zhang, Brian Yin, Haomiao Jiang, Marcel van Workum, Thomas Lane, Bryce Erickson, Salil Pathare, Kyle Price, Anupam Singh, David Baszucki
- **Abstract**: Foundation models trained on vast amounts of data have demonstrated remarkable reasoning and generation capabilities in the domains of text, images, audio and video. Our goal at Roblox is to build such a foundation model for 3D intelligence, a model that can support developers in producing all aspects of a Roblox experience, from generating 3D objects and scenes to rigging characters for animation to producing programmatic scripts describing object behaviors. We discuss three key design requirements for such a 3D foundation model and then present our first step towards building such a model. We expect that 3D geometric shapes will be a core data type and describe our solution for 3D shape tokenizer. We show how our tokenization scheme can be used in applications for text-to-shape generation, shape-to-text generation and text-to-scene generation. We demonstrate how these applications can collaborate with existing large language models (LLMs) to perform scene analysis and reasoning. We conclude with a discussion outlining our path to building a fully unified foundation model for 3D intelligence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Cube: A Roblox View of 3D Intelligence" presents an initial step towards building a foundation model for 3D intelligence at Roblox. The authors focus on 3D shape tokenization as a key component, describing a method to convert 3D shapes into discrete tokens. They then demonstrate the use of this tokenization scheme in applications such as text-to-shape generation, shape-to-text generation, and text-to-scene generation. The system leverages existing large language models (LLMs) for scene analysis and reasoning.  The paper emphasizes the importance of jointly learning from multi-modal data, handling unbounded input/output sizes with autoregressive models, and collaborating with humans and other AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the specific implementation and application of 3D shape tokenization within the Roblox context. While shape tokenization itself is not entirely novel (VQ-VAEs have been used for this in other domains), the authors introduce specific architectural modifications (Phase-Modulated Positional Encoding, Stochastic Gradient Shortcut, and Self-Supervised Latent Space Regularization) tailored to 3D shapes. The application of this tokenization to build a scene generation pipeline that connects to LLMs adds to the paper's innovation.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential impact on 3D content creation. The Roblox platform, with its vast user base and developer community, provides a strong motivation for automating 3D content creation. This research could pave the way for more accessible and efficient 3D modeling and scene building tools, ultimately benefiting Roblox developers.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear description of the shape tokenization pipeline, including the architectural modifications.
    *   The demonstrated applications (text-to-shape, shape-to-text, text-to-scene) showcase the versatility of the tokenization scheme.
    *   The integration with LLMs for scene analysis and reasoning is a promising direction.
    *   The open-source release of code and model weights increases the accessibility of the research.
    *   The experiments are convincing and show that the methods presented perform well.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could benefit from a more thorough comparison to existing shape tokenization methods. While the paper mentions some relevant prior works, a detailed quantitative comparison would strengthen the argument for the proposed modifications.
    *   The evaluation is limited to qualitative results. More rigorous quantitative metrics (e.g. FID score) would be beneficial.
    *   The reliance on proprietary LLMs (e.g., GPT-4) could limit the reproducibility of the research and potentially introduce bias.
    *   The paper does not adequately address the challenges posed by scalability to large and complex scenes, which are typical in Roblox experiences. The scene layouts still have several limitations as well as some obvious errors which have been corrected in the newer models.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of 3D content generation, particularly within gaming platforms like Roblox. The shape tokenization and scene generation techniques could be adopted by other researchers and developers, leading to new tools and workflows for 3D modeling.
    *   The paper's greatest strength comes from the fact that it showcases a viable method to integrate several models to achieve the goal of scene creation and editing, while being backed up with solid methodology and experimentation. This means the paper has high utility.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:** While the paper demonstrates a promising approach to 3D intelligence, it lacks a depth of comparison, quantifiable results, and detailed scalability discussions that would warrant a higher score. The reliance on proprietary models and limited evaluation weaken the overall impact of the work. Therefore, a score of 6 is justified.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### CAM-Seg: A Continuous-valued Embedding Approach for Semantic Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15617v1)
- **Authors**: Masud Ahmed, Zahid Hasan, Syed Arefinul Haque, Abu Zaher Md Faridee, Sanjay Purushotham, Suya You, Nirmalya Roy
- **Abstract**: Traditional transformer-based semantic segmentation relies on quantized embeddings. However, our analysis reveals that autoencoder accuracy on segmentation mask using quantized embeddings (e.g. VQ-VAE) is 8% lower than continuous-valued embeddings (e.g. KL-VAE). Motivated by this, we propose a continuous-valued embedding framework for semantic segmentation. By reformulating semantic mask generation as a continuous image-to-embedding diffusion process, our approach eliminates the need for discrete latent representations while preserving fine-grained spatial and semantic details. Our key contribution includes a diffusion-guided autoregressive transformer that learns a continuous semantic embedding space by modeling long-range dependencies in image features. Our framework contains a unified architecture combining a VAE encoder for continuous feature extraction, a diffusion-guided transformer for conditioned embedding generation, and a VAE decoder for semantic mask reconstruction. Our setting facilitates zero-shot domain adaptation capabilities enabled by the continuity of the embedding space. Experiments across diverse datasets (e.g., Cityscapes and domain-shifted variants) demonstrate state-of-the-art robustness to distribution shifts, including adverse weather (e.g., fog, snow) and viewpoint variations. Our model also exhibits strong noise resilience, achieving robust performance ($\approx$ 95% AP compared to baseline) under gaussian noise, moderate motion blur, and moderate brightness/contrast variations, while experiencing only a moderate impact ($\approx$ 90% AP compared to baseline) from 50% salt and pepper noise, saturation and hue shifts. Code available: https://github.com/mahmed10/CAMSS.git
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CAM-Seg paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CAM-Seg, a novel approach to semantic image segmentation that utilizes a continuous-valued embedding framework.  Unlike traditional transformer-based methods that rely on quantized embeddings (like VQ-VAE), CAM-Seg employs a continuous embedding space (similar to KL-VAE) to preserve fine-grained details.  The architecture consists of a VAE encoder for feature extraction, a diffusion-guided transformer for embedding generation, and a VAE decoder for mask reconstruction. A key innovation is the use of a diffusion model to generate the continuous semantic embeddings, eliminating the need for discrete latent representations. The authors demonstrate the method's robustness to various image degradations (noise, blur, color variations) and its ability to perform zero-shot domain adaptation on datasets with different weather conditions and camera viewpoints.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** While the architecture combines existing components, the specific combination and application to semantic segmentation is relatively novel. The main contribution lies in the use of a diffusion model *specifically* within the transformer architecture for generating *continuous* semantic embeddings *conditioned on* an RGB image. This combination aims to address limitations of quantized embeddings in capturing fine-grained details. The use of bidirectional attention inspired by MAE within the transformer is a reasonable but not exceptionally groundbreaking improvement. Using pretrained autoencoders is efficient, but also reduces architectural novelty. The idea of using continuous-valued embeddings isn't entirely new, with prior work exploring CAMs for generative modeling, but adapting it specifically to semantic segmentation with this particular architecture is a clear contribution.

**Significance:** The claimed robustness and zero-shot domain adaptation capabilities are significant if the claims hold.  Semantic segmentation is a crucial task in computer vision, and improvements in robustness and adaptability are highly valuable. The experiments, covering a range of datasets and noise types, strengthen these claims. Demonstrating superior performance compared to quantized embedding approaches (KL-VAE vs. VQ-VAE) is important, providing justification for the chosen embedding space.  The ablation studies, though basic, help understand the impact of design choices.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper identifies a limitation of quantized embeddings in semantic segmentation and proposes a solution.
*   **Reasonable Architecture:**  The architecture is well-structured and builds upon established components (VAE, transformer, diffusion model).
*   **Solid Experimental Validation:** Experiments across multiple datasets with various noise types and domain shifts are comprehensive.
*   **Well-Written:** The paper is generally clear and easy to follow.
*   **Addresses relevant problems**: The paper addresses challenges in real-world application of semantic segmentation, such as robustness and domain adaptation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Advance:** The approach isn't revolutionary. It combines existing techniques in a specific configuration. The individual components are well-established.
*   **Limited Comparison:** The absence of a direct comparison with current semantic segmentation benchmarks (aside from the component analysis) makes it harder to fully assess the magnitude of the performance gain. Comparing only to autoencoder baseline performance isn't sufficient.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** The paper lacks deeper theoretical justification as to why continuous-valued embeddings are fundamentally better for preserving fine-grained semantic detail *in this specific context*.
*   **Limited Ablation:**  The ablation studies are useful but could be more extensive (e.g., varying transformer architecture, diffusion steps).
*   **Potential for Overselling**: Claims of "state-of-the-art robustness" are only partially true if it is referring to direct component comparison rather than the semantic segmentation task at large.

**Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research by encouraging more exploration of continuous-valued embeddings in semantic segmentation. It provides a practical framework that other researchers can build upon or compare against. The robustness and adaptation findings could inspire further work on domain-invariant semantic segmentation.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a valid and well-executed approach to semantic segmentation. However, its incremental nature and limited comparison to established state-of-the-art methods restrain its overall impact. It is a good engineering contribution rather than a theoretical breakthrough. While the empirical results are encouraging, more rigorous benchmarking is necessary to fully establish its advantages.
