- **Score**: 7/10

### Uncertainty Quantification and Confidence Calibration in Large Language Models: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15850v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoou Liu, Tiejin Chen, Longchao Da, Chacha Chen, Zhen Lin, Hua Wei
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in text generation, reasoning, and decision-making, enabling their adoption in high-stakes domains such as healthcare, law, and transportation. However, their reliability is a major concern, as they often produce plausible but incorrect responses. Uncertainty quantification (UQ) enhances trustworthiness by estimating confidence in outputs, enabling risk mitigation and selective prediction. However, traditional UQ methods struggle with LLMs due to computational constraints and decoding inconsistencies. Moreover, LLMs introduce unique uncertainty sources, such as input ambiguity, reasoning path divergence, and decoding stochasticity, that extend beyond classical aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty. To address this, we introduce a new taxonomy that categorizes UQ methods based on computational efficiency and uncertainty dimensions (input, reasoning, parameter, and prediction uncertainty). We evaluate existing techniques, assess their real-world applicability, and identify open challenges, emphasizing the need for scalable, interpretable, and robust UQ approaches to enhance LLM reliability.
- **Summary**: Okay, I have carefully reviewed the provided survey paper titled "Uncertainty Quantification and Confidence Calibration in Large Language Models: A Survey." Here's a summary and a critical evaluation, including a novelty/significance score and justification:

**Summary:**

The survey paper addresses the critical issue of uncertainty quantification (UQ) and confidence calibration in large language models (LLMs).  It highlights the challenges posed by LLMs in terms of reliability, especially in high-stakes domains.  The authors introduce a new taxonomy for categorizing UQ methods tailored to LLMs, focusing on computational efficiency and different dimensions of uncertainty (input, reasoning, parameter, and prediction).  The paper evaluates existing techniques, assesses their real-world applicability, identifies open challenges, and emphasizes the need for scalable, interpretable, and robust UQ approaches to enhance LLM reliability. It differentiates the different uncertainty types from one another. It reviews a good number of the top papers to offer this as a starting point for anyone interested in UQ in LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The taxonomy based on computational efficiency *and* uncertainty dimensions is a valuable contribution. Prior works have often focused on hallucination detection or adapting classical UQ methods without specifically addressing the unique characteristics of LLMs (especially prompt-driven uncertainties). The decoupling of uncertainty sources and focusing on the dimensions of uncertainty in response to LLM offers a fresh perspective. However, the individual components are not entirely novel.  The concept of input, reasoning, parameter, and prediction uncertainty, while well-organized, could be viewed as a refined categorization of existing notions rather than a completely new invention. The individual techniques discussed are also existing techniques.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a very important and timely problem: the lack of trust and reliability in LLMs. Given the increasing deployment of LLMs in critical applications, UQ and confidence calibration are essential for ensuring responsible AI. The survey provides a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners seeking to understand the landscape of UQ methods for LLMs and to identify promising research directions. The emphasis on reasoning uncertainty, which is often overlooked, is also significant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a broad range of UQ methods, providing a good overview of the field.
    *   **Clear Taxonomy:**  The proposed taxonomy helps to structure the complex landscape of UQ methods for LLMs.
    *   **Focus on LLM-Specific Challenges:**  The paper explicitly addresses the challenges posed by the scale, generation capabilities, and uncertainty dynamics of LLMs, differentiating them from traditional ML tasks.
    *   **Identifies Open Challenges:** The section on open challenges and future directions is valuable for guiding future research efforts.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Evaluation:** The survey primarily focuses on reviewing existing methods.  A comparative empirical evaluation of different UQ methods on specific LLM tasks would significantly strengthen the paper. (It is a survey, so perhaps not expected).
    *   **Depth of Analysis:** While comprehensive, the analysis of individual methods could be deeper, providing more critical insights into their strengths and limitations.
    *   **Practical Guidance:** While identifying challenges, the paper could benefit from more concrete guidance on how to select and apply appropriate UQ methods for specific LLM applications.
    *   **The paper could use some editing for grammar and clarity. It also lacks depth in some areas in the applications section, which feels like a laundry list of potential uses.**

*   **Potential Influence:** This survey is well-timed and addresses a critical need. It will likely be a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners working on UQ for LLMs. By providing a clear taxonomy and identifying key challenges, it can help to focus future research efforts and accelerate progress in the field. The influence will be further amplified if the authors maintain and update the survey as the field evolves.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers valuable insights and organization of the field of uncertainty quantification in LLMs, which addresses a critical gap in the literature. However, the novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary, and the lack of original empirical analysis limits its immediate impact. The high significance in addressing such a rapidly developing area and creating an organization with a taxonomy balances the need for improvement.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Zero-1-to-A: Zero-Shot One Image to Animatable Head Avatars Using Video Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15851v1)
- **Authors**: Zhou Zhenglin, Ma Fan, Fan Hehe, Chua Tat-Seng
- **Abstract**: Animatable head avatar generation typically requires extensive data for training. To reduce the data requirements, a natural solution is to leverage existing data-free static avatar generation methods, such as pre-trained diffusion models with score distillation sampling (SDS), which align avatars with pseudo ground-truth outputs from the diffusion model. However, directly distilling 4D avatars from video diffusion often leads to over-smooth results due to spatial and temporal inconsistencies in the generated video. To address this issue, we propose Zero-1-to-A, a robust method that synthesizes a spatial and temporal consistency dataset for 4D avatar reconstruction using the video diffusion model. Specifically, Zero-1-to-A iteratively constructs video datasets and optimizes animatable avatars in a progressive manner, ensuring that avatar quality increases smoothly and consistently throughout the learning process. This progressive learning involves two stages: (1) Spatial Consistency Learning fixes expressions and learns from front-to-side views, and (2) Temporal Consistency Learning fixes views and learns from relaxed to exaggerated expressions, generating 4D avatars in a simple-to-complex manner. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Zero-1-to-A improves fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed compared to existing diffusion-based methods, providing a solution for lifelike avatar creation. Code is publicly available at: https://github.com/ZhenglinZhou/Zero-1-to-A.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Zero-1-to-A: Zero-Shot One Image to Animatable Head Avatars Using Video Diffusion":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Zero-1-to-A, a novel method for generating animatable 4D head avatars from a single input image using video diffusion models.  The key idea is to address the spatial and temporal inconsistencies that arise when directly distilling 4D avatars from video diffusion models using Score Distillation Sampling (SDS).  Zero-1-to-A proposes a "symbiotic generation" approach, where an updatable dataset is iteratively constructed to cache video diffusion results, fostering a mutually beneficial relationship between avatar generation and dataset construction.  The method also employs a progressive learning strategy, decoupling video diffusion generation into Spatial Consistency Learning (front-to-side views, fixed expression) and Temporal Consistency Learning (relaxed to exaggerated expressions, fixed camera). The authors claim that Zero-1-to-A improves fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed compared to existing diffusion-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper has some notable novelty, primarily in the combination of existing techniques in a specific way to address a well-defined problem. The "symbiotic generation" concept, where the dataset and avatar are refined iteratively, is a reasonable contribution. The progressive learning strategy, while not entirely new in the broader machine learning landscape, is a good application to the specific challenge of 4D avatar generation using video diffusion. The splitting into spatial and temporal consistency is helpful in breaking down the problem.

* **Significance:** The significance hinges on whether the method demonstrably solves the inconsistency problem in video diffusion for avatar generation and provides practical benefits.  The paper claims improvements in fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed. While the qualitative results appear promising, a more rigorous quantitative evaluation is needed.  The limitations section, particularly the difficulty in modeling elements beyond the head due to the Gaussian head representation, highlights a constraint that future work could address. A more detailed comparison between various related approaches would enhance the contribution of the paper.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Addresses a relevant problem:** The inconsistency issues in video diffusion for 4D avatar generation are well-articulated.
    * **Clear methodology:** The symbiotic generation and progressive learning strategies are described in sufficient detail.
    * **Qualitatively appealing results:** The generated avatars look visually impressive.
    * The progressive training approach seems to stabilize and improve the overall quality, which is well justified.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Quantitative Evaluation:** While the paper reports quantitative metrics (CLIP score, ID Consistency, Temporal Smoothness, Rendering Speed), a deeper analysis of these metrics and a broader comparison to other state-of-the-art methods would strengthen the claims of superiority.
    * **Lack of Comparison:** The paper does not show an ablation that specifically removes "SymGEN" - showing how the "Progressive Learning" strategy performs *without* the updatable dataset. This is critical because the main claim is about the "mutually beneficial relationship" of avatar generation and dataset construction. Without an experiment showing that PL without updatable datasets, the claims around SymGEN are hard to believe.
    * **Overclaims:** The claim of "Zero-Shot" is somewhat misleading. The method relies on pre-trained image/video diffusion models, so it's not truly zero-shot in the sense of learning a completely new task from scratch without any prior knowledge.
    * **Dependence on the Gaussian Head Representation:** The method's reliance on the Gaussian head representation limits its ability to handle complex hairstyles and other elements beyond the head, as acknowledged by the authors.
    * The dependence on FLAME limits the generality of the approach.
    * Figure 8's legend is inconsistent: SDS and Video Diffusion are listed separately, even though video diffusion uses SDS loss. It is confusing and weakens the credibility of the authors' comparison.

* **Potential Impact:** The work could potentially influence the direction of research in data-efficient avatar generation.  If the method proves robust and generalizable, it could be valuable for applications in VR/AR, gaming, and digital content creation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a contribution to the field by proposing a method that alleviates inconsistencies in generating 4D head avatars from a single image using diffusion models. The combination of symbiotic generation and progressive learning is a reasonable approach. However, the lack of rigorous quantitative comparisons, overclaims of a zero-shot approach, and dependence on the Gaussian head representation limit its significance.

- **Score**: 6/10

### DroidTTP: Mapping Android Applications with TTP for Cyber Threat Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15866v1)
- **Authors**: Dincy R Arikkat, Vinod P., Rafidha Rehiman K. A., Serena Nicolazzo, Marco Arazzi, Antonino Nocera, Mauro Conti
- **Abstract**: The widespread adoption of Android devices for sensitive operations like banking and communication has made them prime targets for cyber threats, particularly Advanced Persistent Threats (APT) and sophisticated malware attacks. Traditional malware detection methods rely on binary classification, failing to provide insights into adversarial Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures (TTPs). Understanding malware behavior is crucial for enhancing cybersecurity defenses. To address this gap, we introduce DroidTTP, a framework mapping Android malware behaviors to TTPs based on the MITRE ATT&CK framework. Our curated dataset explicitly links MITRE TTPs to Android applications. We developed an automated solution leveraging the Problem Transformation Approach (PTA) and Large Language Models (LLMs) to map applications to both Tactics and Techniques. Additionally, we employed Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) with prompt engineering and LLM fine-tuning for TTP predictions. Our structured pipeline includes dataset creation, hyperparameter tuning, data augmentation, feature selection, model development, and SHAP-based model interpretability. Among LLMs, Llama achieved the highest performance in Tactic classification with a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9583 and Hamming Loss of 0.0182, and in Technique classification with a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9348 and Hamming Loss of 0.0127. However, the Label Powerset XGBoost model outperformed LLMs, achieving a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9893 for Tactic classification and 0.9753 for Technique classification, with a Hamming Loss of 0.0054 and 0.0050, respectively. While XGBoost showed superior performance, the narrow margin highlights the potential of LLM-based approaches in TTP classification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DroidTTP: Mapping Android Applications with TTP for Cyber Threat Intelligence":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DroidTTP, a system for mapping Android malware behavior to Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures (TTPs) as defined by the MITRE ATT&CK framework.  The authors created a novel dataset linking MITRE TTPs to Android applications. DroidTTP uses Problem Transformation Approaches (PTA) and Large Language Models (LLMs) for tactic and technique prediction. They explored Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) with prompt engineering and LLM fine-tuning strategies. The system includes a pipeline for dataset creation, hyperparameter tuning, data augmentation, feature selection using an enhanced SelectKBest method, model development, and SHAP for model interpretation. Experimental results showed Label Powerset XGBoost outperforming LLMs in some tasks, but fine-tuned LLMs exhibit promising performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the dataset it creates and the application of LLMs to map Android malware to the MITRE ATT&CK framework.  Prior work has focused mainly on malware detection and classification, not mapping to attacker behaviors. The enhanced SelectKBest feature selection is a potentially useful refinement of an existing technique.
*   **Significance:**  Mapping malware to TTPs is significant because it moves beyond simple classification, giving security analysts deeper insights into attacker strategies and helping them to build more effective defenses. This level of understanding is critical for robust threat intelligence and proactive cybersecurity measures.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The creation of a dedicated dataset is a valuable contribution to the field.  The lack of such datasets has hindered research in this area.
    *   The thorough experimental evaluation comparing different PTAs, LLMs, and feature selection techniques provides a good benchmark.
    *   The use of SHAP analysis helps in understanding the model's decision-making process, adding a layer of interpretability.
    *   The integration of both classical ML and LLM approaches offers a comprehensive examination of potential solutions.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper lacks in a rigorous comparative analysis with recent and more sophisticated threat intelligence frameworks.
    *   While LLMs show promise, the performance gap between them and the XGBoost-based models is relatively small.  This raises questions about the cost-benefit ratio of using LLMs, given their computational demands.
    *   The description of the prompt engineering strategies could be more detailed, including examples of how the prompts were optimized and what specific challenges were encountered.
    *   The study uses a specific set of applications. The results might not generalize to the entire population of Android apps.
    *   The reliance on static analysis limits the approach to identifying known malware characteristics. Dynamic analysis might be required to detect more sophisticated or obfuscated malware.

*   **Impact:** The DroidTTP system has the potential to influence future research by providing a dataset and a baseline for mapping Android malware to TTPs. It could lead to the development of more intelligent and proactive cybersecurity tools for Android devices.
*   **Justification of Score:** While the paper presents a solid and well-executed study with a novel dataset and a potentially useful system, the incremental improvement over existing methods warrants a score that reflects this. The primary weakness is the relatively small performance difference between LLMs and classical ML models, which weakens the case for adopting the more complex LLM-based approach.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### TruthLens: Explainable DeepFake Detection for Face Manipulated and Fully Synthetic Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15867v1)
- **Authors**: Rohit Kundu, Athula Balachandran, Amit K. Roy-Chowdhury
- **Abstract**: Detecting DeepFakes has become a crucial research area as the widespread use of AI image generators enables the effortless creation of face-manipulated and fully synthetic content, yet existing methods are often limited to binary classification (real vs. fake) and lack interpretability. To address these challenges, we propose TruthLens, a novel and highly generalizable framework for DeepFake detection that not only determines whether an image is real or fake but also provides detailed textual reasoning for its predictions. Unlike traditional methods, TruthLens effectively handles both face-manipulated DeepFakes and fully AI-generated content while addressing fine-grained queries such as "Does the eyes/nose/mouth look real or fake?" The architecture of TruthLens combines the global contextual understanding of multimodal large language models like PaliGemma2 with the localized feature extraction capabilities of vision-only models like DINOv2. This hybrid design leverages the complementary strengths of both models, enabling robust detection of subtle manipulations while maintaining interpretability. Extensive experiments on diverse datasets demonstrate that TruthLens outperforms state-of-the-art methods in detection accuracy (by 2-14%) and explainability, in both in-domain and cross-data settings, generalizing effectively across traditional and emerging manipulation techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TruthLens: Explainable DeepFake Detection for Face Manipulated and Fully Synthetic Data":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TruthLens, a novel framework for DeepFake detection that aims to provide not only binary real/fake classification but also detailed, textual explanations for its predictions. TruthLens is designed to handle both face-manipulated DeepFakes and fully AI-generated content, addressing fine-grained queries about specific facial features. The framework combines the global contextual understanding of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) like PaliGemma2 with the localized feature extraction capabilities of vision-only models (VOMs) like DINOv2. This hybrid architecture is intended to leverage the strengths of both model types, enabling robust detection of subtle manipulations and maintaining interpretability. The authors conduct experiments on diverse datasets, demonstrating that TruthLens outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both detection accuracy and explainability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach by integrating global context from MLLMs and local feature extraction from VOMs for DeepFake detection, enhancing explainability. The hybrid architecture, incorporating both PaliGemma2 and DINOv2 is original within this specific application domain. The ability to address nuanced queries and provide textual reasoning sets it apart from existing binary classification methods. The authors' decision to pre-train the adapter with LCS-558K demonstrates a conscious effort to avoid overfitting on specific DeepFake features.

*   **Significance:** The problem of detecting DeepFakes, especially in the face of increasingly sophisticated AI-generated content, is highly relevant. The addition of explainability is a significant contribution, promoting transparency and trust in automated detection systems. The framework's ability to handle both face-manipulated and fully AI-generated content extends its applicability and makes it valuable as the field of synthetic media advances. The reported improvements in detection accuracy and explainability over existing methods validate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.  However, it is crucial to note some caveats. The improvements, although present, may not be uniformly significant across all datasets, and further analysis is required to determine the specific scenarios where TruthLens excels most. Moreover, the dependence on Gemini-1.0 as a judge raises concerns about subjectivity and potential bias.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The hybrid architecture effectively integrates complementary strengths of MLLMs and VOMs.
    *   The detailed textual explanations enhance interpretability and promote transparency.
    *   The framework is designed to handle both face-manipulated and fully AI-generated content.
    *   Extensive experiments demonstrate improved detection accuracy and explainability.
    *   It is a unified solution, unlike many existing solutions tailored to one type of deepfake.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The quantitative improvements, while positive, may not be drastic compared to existing methods. A more in-depth analysis of the performance gains on specific types of manipulations would be beneficial.
    *   The reliance on Gemini-1.0 for evaluation introduces potential bias and subjectivity. A more objective evaluation metric would strengthen the results.
    *   The complexity of the model and its reliance on multiple large pre-trained models may present challenges in terms of computational resources and deployment.
    *   The authors may need to discuss more extensively the computational requirements for training and inference, especially when dealing with high-resolution images or video data.

*   **Potential Influence:** TruthLens has the potential to significantly influence the field of DeepFake detection by promoting more transparent, interpretable, and generalizable solutions. The hybrid architecture could inspire new approaches that combine global and local feature extraction techniques. The framework's ability to handle both face-manipulated and fully AI-generated content makes it relevant to the evolving landscape of synthetic media. The code and trained models are to be released and that would definitely allow the community to improve and adapt the model.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper introduces a novel approach to DeepFake detection with a significant emphasis on explainability, addressing a crucial need in the field. The integration of MLLMs and VOMs is innovative, and the reported results demonstrate its effectiveness. However, the reliance on a subjective evaluation metric, potential complexity, and moderate performance gains relative to existing methods temper the assessment. A score of 7 reflects the paper's promising contributions, while acknowledging areas for improvement and further investigation.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### UniCoRN: Latent Diffusion-based Unified Controllable Image Restoration Network across Multiple Degradations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15868v1)
- **Authors**: Debabrata Mandal, Soumitri Chattopadhyay, Guansen Tong, Praneeth Chakravarthula
- **Abstract**: Image restoration is essential for enhancing degraded images across computer vision tasks. However, most existing methods address only a single type of degradation (e.g., blur, noise, or haze) at a time, limiting their real-world applicability where multiple degradations often occur simultaneously. In this paper, we propose UniCoRN, a unified image restoration approach capable of handling multiple degradation types simultaneously using a multi-head diffusion model. Specifically, we uncover the potential of low-level visual cues extracted from images in guiding a controllable diffusion model for real-world image restoration and we design a multi-head control network adaptable via a mixture-of-experts strategy. We train our model without any prior assumption of specific degradations, through a smartly designed curriculum learning recipe. Additionally, we also introduce MetaRestore, a metalens imaging benchmark containing images with multiple degradations and artifacts. Extensive evaluations on several challenging datasets, including our benchmark, demonstrate that our method achieves significant performance gains and can robustly restore images with severe degradations. Project page: https://codejaeger.github.io/unicorn-gh
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "UNICORN: Latent Diffusion-based Unified Controllable Image Restoration Network across Multiple Degradations" proposes a unified image restoration approach using a latent diffusion model to handle multiple degradations simultaneously.  It uses low-level visual cues extracted from images to guide a controllable diffusion model. The architecture features a multi-head control network, adapted via a mixture-of-experts strategy, and is trained without explicit knowledge of specific degradations through curriculum learning. The authors also introduce a new benchmark dataset, METARESTORE, consisting of metalens-captured images with multiple real-world degradations.  Experiments demonstrate improved performance on existing benchmarks and METARESTORE, suggesting robustness in restoring images with severe degradations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper has several aspects of novelty, although not all groundbreaking.
    * The use of a latent diffusion model for unified multi-degradation image restoration is a good direction.  While diffusion models are gaining traction, the specific application to unified restoration with low-level cue guidance provides a specific contribution.  It is important to consider that there have been other papers that address multi-degradation image restoration. The contribution, thus, relies on the specific approach and improved results over other methods. The claim of unifying existing approaches and being agnostic is somewhat overstated, as careful design is still involved in deciding how to combine cues.
    * The multi-head control network architecture, especially the adaptive mixture-of-experts, is a significant design choice that contributes to the paper's novelty.  The approach allows learning individual visual cue restoration heads, allowing greater flexibility.
    * The METARESTORE dataset is a valuable contribution to the community, providing a challenging benchmark for real-world, multiple-degradation scenarios.  Many existing datasets focus on individual degradation, making the new dataset critical.

* **Significance:**
    * The ability to handle multiple degradations simultaneously has considerable practical significance.  Real-world imaging systems commonly suffer from multiple artifacts.
    * The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods, particularly on the METARESTORE dataset. This highlights the practical advantage of the proposed approach.
    * The curriculum learning strategy aids in avoiding catastrophic forgetting and enables the model to handle complexity, which is an important consideration.

* **Strengths:**
    * The paper is well-written and clearly presents the proposed methodology.
    * The introduction of the METARESTORE dataset addresses a critical gap in the field.
    * The ablation studies provide insights into the effectiveness of individual components of the UNICORN architecture.
    * The results demonstrate strong performance compared to state-of-the-art methods.

* **Weaknesses:**
   *  The description of the "low-level visual cues" feels somewhat hand-engineered. A more automated way to extract the right cues would be a stronger approach.
    *  The dependence on a pretrained Stable Diffusion model implies that the results depend on the quality of this foundational element.
    *  The claim about UNICORN being corruption-agnostic is arguable, as the model architecture and training data are designed with specific degradations in mind, limiting applicability to totally unseen corruptions.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the direction of research in image restoration by encouraging more focus on unified multi-degradation models and leveraging low-level cues to control generative models. The METARESTORE dataset is poised to be a useful resource for evaluating future image restoration approaches.

**Justification for the score:**

The UNICORN paper provides a valuable contribution to the field of image restoration through its unified approach to handling multiple degradations. The novel architecture, adaptive learning strategy, and especially the introduction of the challenging METARESTORE dataset represent a step forward in tackling more realistic image restoration scenarios. While the reliance on a pre-trained diffusion model and some limitations in corruption agnosticism do not make the paper transformative, it certainly pushes forward the boundaries and will be used as reference in future multi-degradation research. For that reason, I would award a:

**Score: 7.5**

### MASH-VLM: Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through Disentangled Spatial-Temporal Representations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15871v1)
- **Authors**: Kyungho Bae, Jinhyung Kim, Sihaeng Lee, Soonyoung Lee, Gunhee Lee, Jinwoo Choi
- **Abstract**: In this work, we tackle action-scene hallucination in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs), where models incorrectly predict actions based on the scene context or scenes based on observed actions. We observe that existing Video-LLMs often suffer from action-scene hallucination due to two main factors. First, existing Video-LLMs intermingle spatial and temporal features by applying an attention operation across all tokens. Second, they use the standard Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE), which causes the text tokens to overemphasize certain types of tokens depending on their sequential orders. To address these issues, we introduce MASH-VLM, Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through disentangled spatial-temporal representations. Our approach includes two key innovations: (1) DST-attention, a novel attention mechanism that disentangles the spatial and temporal tokens within the LLM by using masked attention to restrict direct interactions between the spatial and temporal tokens; (2) Harmonic-RoPE, which extends the dimensionality of the positional IDs, allowing the spatial and temporal tokens to maintain balanced positions relative to the text tokens. To evaluate the action-scene hallucination in Video-LLMs, we introduce the UNSCENE benchmark with 1,320 videos and 4,078 QA pairs. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MASH-VLM achieves state-of-the-art results on the UNSCENE benchmark, as well as on existing video understanding benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MASH-VLM: Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through Disentangled Spatial-Temporal Representations."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of "action-scene hallucination" in Video-LLMs, where models incorrectly predict actions based on scene context or vice-versa. The authors identify two primary causes: the intermingling of spatial and temporal features within the LLM's attention mechanism and the limitations of standard Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE) in multimodal contexts.  They propose MASH-VLM, which introduces two key innovations: (1) DST-attention, a masked attention mechanism to disentangle spatial and temporal tokens within the LLM, and (2) Harmonic-RoPE, an extension of RoPE that provides balanced positional IDs for spatial and temporal tokens.  The paper also introduces UNSCENE, a new benchmark designed specifically to evaluate action-scene hallucination. Experiments demonstrate that MASH-VLM achieves state-of-the-art performance on UNSCENE and other video understanding benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a clear advancement in addressing the action-scene hallucination problem. DST-attention, which is a form of structured attention specifically masked, and Harmonic-ROPE are new and intriguing solutions. The idea of using masking in the attention mechanism to disentangle visual tokens and modulating RoPE to better balance the positions of different modalities addresses a core limitation of existing models. Though structured attention/masked attention has been utilized previously, its application here is novel. The introduction of the UNSCENE benchmark is also significant, as it provides a targeted evaluation framework for this specific type of hallucination.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because hallucination is a major obstacle to the widespread adoption of MLLMs in real-world applications. By specifically addressing action-scene hallucination, the paper contributes to improving the reliability and trustworthiness of these models. The performance gains on UNSCENE and existing benchmarks are compelling and suggest that the proposed techniques are effective. The insights into the limitations of standard attention and RoPE in multimodal contexts are also valuable for the broader research community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clearly identifies and articulates the problem of action-scene hallucination.
    *   Proposes a well-motivated and technically sound solution, MASH-VLM.
    *   Introduces a new benchmark, UNSCENE, tailored to the problem.
    *   Provides strong empirical evidence to support the effectiveness of MASH-VLM.
    *   Includes ablation studies to analyze the contribution of different components.
    *   Qualitative results provide further insights into the model's behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The implementation details, though provided, could be more transparent.  For instance, details on hyperparameters or specific implementation tricks during instruction tuning.
    *   While the results are impressive, more analysis can be done on what failure modes still exist and how to further address them.
    *   The analysis of attention scores, though insightful, can be elaborated by including visualizations for different layers of the model, in order to provide a more detailed picture of how the attention mechanism operates at different processing stages.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in Video-LLMs by highlighting the importance of disentangled representations and providing effective techniques for mitigating hallucination. The UNSCENE benchmark will likely be used by other researchers to evaluate their models. The concepts presented in the paper can potentially be extended to address other types of hallucination in MLLMs.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper presents a novel solution to a well-defined problem. The approach is technically sound, empirically validated, and has the potential to significantly impact the field of video understanding. Though there are some minor limitations to the paper, like the need for additional analysis and detail, MASH-VLM shows significant progress and warrants a high score.

### DeepPsy-Agent: A Stage-Aware and Deep-Thinking Emotional Support Agent System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15876v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Chen, Zebing Sun
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces DeepPsy-Agent, an innovative psychological support system that combines the three-stage helping theory in psychology with deep learning techniques. The system consists of two core components: (1) a multi-stage response-capable dialogue model (\textit{deeppsy-chat}), which enhances reasoning capabilities through stage-awareness and deep-thinking analysis to generate high-quality responses; and (2) a real-time stage transition detection model that identifies contextual shifts to guide the dialogue towards more effective intervention stages. Based on 30,000 real psychological hotline conversations, we employ AI-simulated dialogues and expert re-annotation strategies to construct a high-quality multi-turn dialogue dataset. Experimental results demonstrate that DeepPsy-Agent outperforms general-purpose large language models (LLMs) in key metrics such as problem exposure completeness, cognitive restructuring success rate, and action adoption rate. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of stage-awareness and deep-thinking modules, showing that stage information contributes 42.3\% to performance, while the deep-thinking module increases root-cause identification by 58.3\% and reduces ineffective suggestions by 72.1\%. This system addresses critical challenges in AI-based psychological support through dynamic dialogue management and deep reasoning, advancing intelligent mental health services.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepPsy-Agent, an AI system designed for emotional support conversations (ESC). It combines a three-stage psychological helping theory (Exploration, Insight, Action) with deep learning techniques.  DeepPsy-Agent consists of a multi-stage response-capable dialogue model (deeppsy-chat) enhanced with stage-awareness and deep-thinking analysis, and a real-time stage transition detection model. The system was trained on a large dataset of psychological hotline conversations enhanced through AI simulation and expert re-annotation.  Experiments demonstrate that DeepPsy-Agent outperforms general-purpose LLMs in areas like problem exposure, cognitive restructuring, and action adoption. Ablation studies highlight the contributions of stage-awareness and deep-thinking modules.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The paper's primary novelty lies in the **integration of a specific psychological theory (Hill's three-stage model) with deep learning** in the context of emotional support. Existing ESC systems often rely on more generic approaches or lack a clear theoretical grounding. The explicit stage-awareness in the dialogue model and the dynamic stage transition detection are also novel components. The use of "slow thinking" (test-time scaling) to enhance reasoning is another interesting technique applied in this specific context.

*Significance:* ESC is a growing area, and improvements to the quality and effectiveness of these systems are important. If the claims of the paper hold, DeepPsy-Agent represents a step forward in building more helpful and nuanced emotional support AI. The improved performance metrics (problem exposure, cognitive restructuring, action adoption) directly address key challenges in ESC. Moreover, the integration of psychological theory provides a more structured and potentially explainable framework, which is important for building trust and user acceptance.

*Strengths:*

*   **Strong Theoretical Basis:**  The use of Hill's three-stage model provides a solid foundation and helps structure the dialogue in a meaningful way.
*   **Clear Architecture:**  The system architecture is well-defined, with distinct modules for dialogue generation, stage transition detection, and state information update.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes comparative experiments, ablation studies, and case studies, providing a good level of validation.
*   **Large Dataset:** Training on a large, specialized dataset (30,000 conversations) is crucial for performance.
*   **Addressing Explainability:** By explicitly outputting reasoning chains and stage information, the system aims to enhance explainability, a critical aspect for trust in AI-based mental health applications.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Details on "Slow Thinking":** The paper refers to "slow thinking" enabled by Test-time scaling but doesn't elaborate on how it is implemented or adapted in the context of dialogues.
*   **Limited External Validation:** While comparisons with other LLMs are included, a user study evaluating the perceived helpfulness and satisfaction of DeepPsy-Agent is missing. This would provide more direct evidence of its impact.
*   **Reliance on Simulated Dialogues:**  While re-annotation is done by experts, the use of AI-simulated dialogues in training could introduce biases or limitations.  A breakdown of real versus simulated data in the training set is lacking.
*   **Ethical considerations:** The paper doesn't explicitly address the ethical implications of deploying such a system, including issues of privacy, potential harm, and the importance of human oversight.

*Influence:*

DeepPsy-Agent could influence the field by demonstrating the benefits of integrating psychological theories into AI-based ESC systems.  The stage-aware architecture and the emphasis on deep reasoning may inspire new approaches to dialogue management and response generation in this domain. The results could encourage further research into using similar techniques for other mental health applications.

**Justification of Score:**

DeepPsy-Agent presents a novel and promising approach to building emotional support AI. The integration of psychological theory with deep learning, the clear architecture, and the strong evaluation all contribute to its significance. However, the lack of user validation, limited details on the "slow thinking" mechanism, and reliance on simulated dialogues in training weaken the claims somewhat. While a user study would further validate the impact. The missing ethical considerations further pull down the score. Considering these points, a score of 7 is justified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Repurposing 2D Diffusion Models with Gaussian Atlas for 3D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15877v1)
- **Authors**: Tiange Xiang, Kai Li, Chengjiang Long, Christian Häne, Peihong Guo, Scott Delp, Ehsan Adeli, Li Fei-Fei
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-to-image diffusion models have been driven by the increasing availability of paired 2D data. However, the development of 3D diffusion models has been hindered by the scarcity of high-quality 3D data, resulting in less competitive performance compared to their 2D counterparts. To address this challenge, we propose repurposing pre-trained 2D diffusion models for 3D object generation. We introduce Gaussian Atlas, a novel representation that utilizes dense 2D grids, enabling the fine-tuning of 2D diffusion models to generate 3D Gaussians. Our approach demonstrates successful transfer learning from a pre-trained 2D diffusion model to a 2D manifold flattened from 3D structures. To support model training, we compile GaussianVerse, a large-scale dataset comprising 205K high-quality 3D Gaussian fittings of various 3D objects. Our experimental results show that text-to-image diffusion models can be effectively adapted for 3D content generation, bridging the gap between 2D and 3D modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of limited high-quality 3D data for training 3D generative models, which hinders their performance compared to 2D counterparts. The authors propose a novel approach to repurpose pre-trained 2D diffusion models for 3D object generation. They introduce "Gaussian Atlas," a 2D representation of 3D Gaussians, enabling fine-tuning of 2D diffusion models for 3D generation. This involves projecting 3D Gaussians onto a sphere, then flattening them into a 2D grid. They also compile "GaussianVerse," a large-scale dataset of high-quality 3D Gaussian fittings. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach, bridging the gap between 2D and 3D modeling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the "Gaussian Atlas" representation, which effectively transforms 3D Gaussian data into a format suitable for fine-tuning pre-trained 2D diffusion models. This is a conceptually interesting and potentially impactful contribution. Projecting 3D data onto a sphere and then flattening it is not entirely new, but its application to Gaussian Splatting and adaptation to 2D diffusion models is novel. Also, the way the OT is done and applied to multiple samples is clever. While previous works like NFD, CRM, and others explored 2D representations of 3D content, the Gaussian Atlas offers a dense representation directly amenable to fine-tuning existing 2D diffusion models without complex architectures. The GaussianVerse dataset is also a valuable contribution, although other datasets of this kind exist.
*   **Significance:** The potential significance is high. The scarcity of high-quality 3D data is a bottleneck in 3D generative modeling. If the proposed method allows leveraging the vast resources already invested in 2D diffusion models, it can significantly accelerate progress in 3D content creation. The demonstrated results showcase improved alignment with text prompts and user preferences compared to existing 3D Gaussian generators. This approach effectively simplifies 3D generation by directly fine-tuning 2D models, avoiding complex 3D-specific designs.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of limited 3D data and the need for leveraging 2D priors.
    *   **Novel Representation:** The Gaussian Atlas is a well-motivated and innovative representation for 3D Gaussians.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes both qualitative and quantitative evaluations, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method. The user studies strengthen the claims about improved generation quality.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The release of the GaussianVerse dataset is a valuable contribution to the community.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** They provide ablation studies supporting key design choices.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on 2D Diffusion Model Quality:** The performance relies heavily on the quality of the pre-trained 2D diffusion model. Limitations of the base 2D model, like prompt adherence in long prompts, are inherited by the 3D system.
    *   **Computational Costs:** Though the overall approach may be more efficient than training 3D diffusion models from scratch, the initial fitting of 3D Gaussians and the fine-tuning process still involve considerable computational resources.  This needs more highlighting.
    *   **Artifacts:** While the paper mentions the absence of "seam" artifacts, it should acknowledge the presence of other potential artifacts due to the projection and flattening process.
    *   **Comparison to Existing 2D Methods** Though the authors claim direct comparison to PI3D, HexaGen3D, and GIMDiffusion is not possible due to publicly unavailable implementations, the authors should include comparisons with readily available 2D methods or reimplementations.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of 3D generative modeling. The Gaussian Atlas is a compelling idea, and the experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness in leveraging 2D diffusion models for 3D content creation. While limitations exist, such as reliance on the 2D model's performance and computational costs, the proposed approach has the potential to significantly impact the development of 3D generative models. The contribution is meaningful, and the paper is well-written.

### Enhancing Zero-Shot Image Recognition in Vision-Language Models through Human-like Concept Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15886v1)
- **Authors**: Hui Liu, Wenya Wang, Kecheng Chen, Jie Liu, Yibing Liu, Tiexin Qin, Peisong He, Xinghao Jiang, Haoliang Li
- **Abstract**: In zero-shot image recognition tasks, humans demonstrate remarkable flexibility in classifying unseen categories by composing known simpler concepts. However, existing vision-language models (VLMs), despite achieving significant progress through large-scale natural language supervision, often underperform in real-world applications because of sub-optimal prompt engineering and the inability to adapt effectively to target classes. To address these issues, we propose a Concept-guided Human-like Bayesian Reasoning (CHBR) framework. Grounded in Bayes' theorem, CHBR models the concept used in human image recognition as latent variables and formulates this task by summing across potential concepts, weighted by a prior distribution and a likelihood function. To tackle the intractable computation over an infinite concept space, we introduce an importance sampling algorithm that iteratively prompts large language models (LLMs) to generate discriminative concepts, emphasizing inter-class differences. We further propose three heuristic approaches involving Average Likelihood, Confidence Likelihood, and Test Time Augmentation (TTA) Likelihood, which dynamically refine the combination of concepts based on the test image. Extensive evaluations across fifteen datasets demonstrate that CHBR consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art zero-shot generalization methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called Concept-guided Human-like Bayesian Reasoning (CHBR) to enhance zero-shot image recognition in vision-language models (VLMs). Inspired by the human ability to classify unseen categories by composing known concepts, CHBR models concepts as latent variables and formulates the recognition task by marginalizing over a space of potential concepts. To address the computational intractability of this approach, the authors propose an importance sampling algorithm that leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate discriminative concepts for each class. Furthermore, the paper presents three heuristic methods – Average Likelihood, Confidence Likelihood, and Test Time Augmentation (TTA) Likelihood – to refine the combination of concepts based on the input image, enabling dynamic adaptation during inference. The experimental results across fifteen datasets demonstrate that CHBR consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art zero-shot generalization methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The core idea of using LLMs to extract concepts and then use these concepts in a Bayesian framework for image recognition has elements of novelty. The main contribution lies in combining concept extraction with a Bayesian reasoning framework, allowing the model to weigh the importance of different concepts dynamically during inference. Further novelty lies in the use of an importance sampling technique that emphasizes inter-class differences when generating the concepts with the LLM.  The specific implementations of the likelihood functions are incremental improvements upon existing methods (test time augmentation etc), and by themselves don't constitute major breakthroughs.

*Significance:* The significance of the paper is moderate. Zero-shot image recognition is an important problem, and the CHBR framework offers a way to leverage LLMs' knowledge to improve accuracy. The gains reported over existing baselines are consistent and provide evidence that the framework works, especially in fine grained classification tasks. The integration of test-time augmentation, and dynamic weighting of the concepts, addresses a key limitation of previous concept based approaches, which often struggled to adapt in complex real-world situations. The claim of "human-like reasoning" might be an overstatement, as the model is only loosely inspired by human cognitive processes. However, overall the approach is promising, showing a clear benefit from LLM-extracted concepts.

*Strengths:*
*   The paper is well-written and the proposed approach is clearly explained.
*   The framework addresses the computational challenge effectively using importance sampling.
*   The experimental evaluation is extensive, covering fifteen datasets and various visual encoders.
*   The results demonstrate consistent improvements over state-of-the-art baselines.
*   Provides ablation analysis to show the influence of the sampling windows.

*Weaknesses:*

*   The "human-like" claim is somewhat weak and could be misleading. The connection to actual human cognition is somewhat superficial.
*   The dependency on LLMs for concept generation introduces a potential source of bias and computational cost, and a black box to the whole model. While importance sampling improves this cost, other approaches may be needed.
*   The improvement is more significant on fine-grained datasets. The authors acknowledge a reduced performance gap on ImageNet variants, suggesting the framework might be less crucial when classes are more visually distinct.
*   There is a level of complexity involved in implementing the method that might discourage some from trying it, unless the model is made available as a service.

*Potential Influence:* The paper could influence future research in zero-shot image recognition and vision-language models by highlighting the benefits of incorporating external knowledge from LLMs and dynamically adapting concept importance. Other researchers may explore alternative methods for concept generation, or refine the likelihood functions to achieve greater accuracy and robustness. It would be of interest to evaluate on other scenarios that are of practical use.

*Rigorous Rationale:* While the paper presents a solid framework with demonstrable performance gains, it is important to acknowledge the dependency on large language models (LLMs), which introduces both computational costs and potential biases. The reported improvements, although consistent across several datasets, are moderate rather than revolutionary. The lack of a truly novel mathematical or algorithmic component and the overstatement of the “human-like” claim contribute to a lower score.

### Parameters vs. Context: Fine-Grained Control of Knowledge Reliance in Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15888v1)
- **Authors**: Baolong Bi, Shenghua Liu, Yiwei Wang, Yilong Xu, Junfeng Fang, Lingrui Mei, Xueqi Cheng
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) mitigates hallucinations in Large Language Models (LLMs) by integrating external knowledge. However, conflicts between parametric knowledge and retrieved context pose challenges, particularly when retrieved information is unreliable or the model's internal knowledge is outdated. In such cases, LLMs struggle to determine whether to rely more on their own parameters or the conflicted context. To address this, we propose **CK-PLUG**, a plug-and-play method for controlling LLMs' reliance on parametric and contextual knowledge. We introduce a novel knowledge consistency metric, Confidence Gain, which detects knowledge conflicts by measuring entropy shifts in token probability distributions after context insertion. CK-PLUG then enables fine-grained control over knowledge preference by adjusting the probability distribution of tokens with negative confidence gain through a single tuning parameter. Experiments demonstrate CK-PLUG's ability to significantly regulate knowledge reliance in counterfactual RAG scenarios while maintaining generation fluency and knowledge accuracy. For instance, on Llama3-8B, memory recall (MR) of RAG response can be adjusted within a broad range (9.9%-71.9%), compared to the baseline of 42.1%. Moreover, CK-PLUG supports adaptive control based on the model's confidence in both internal and external knowledge, achieving consistent performance improvements across various general RAG tasks. Our code is available at: $\href{https://github.com/byronBBL/CK-PLUG}{\text{this https URL}}$.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of balancing parametric (internal) and contextual (retrieved) knowledge in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems.  It proposes CK-PLUG, a plug-and-play method that controls a Large Language Model's (LLM's) reliance on these knowledge sources.  The core idea is to detect potential knowledge conflicts using a novel "Confidence Gain" metric, which measures entropy shifts in token probability distributions after context insertion.  CK-PLUG then adjusts the probability distribution of tokens with negative confidence gain using a tunable parameter, enabling fine-grained control over knowledge preference.  The paper demonstrates CK-PLUG's ability to regulate knowledge reliance in counterfactual RAG scenarios and achieve performance improvements on general RAG tasks, even without manual tuning by adaptively balancing using model confidence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the "Confidence Gain" metric for detecting knowledge conflicts and the plug-and-play architecture, CK-PLUG, for selectively modulating token probabilities based on this metric. While using entropy to measure uncertainty isn't entirely new, its application to detect *knowledge conflicts* within a RAG setting and use it to guide token probability adjustment is a significant step. Specifically, existing papers mostly focus on editing internal parameters or enhancing the context faithfulness.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practically important problem in RAG: how to handle conflicts between what the model "knows" and what the retrieved context suggests. Current RAG pipelines can suffer when retrieved information is unreliable or the model's internal knowledge is outdated. A robust mechanism to control knowledge reliance could lead to more trustworthy and reliable RAG systems. Its plug-and-play nature makes it easily adaptable across various models without retraining, broadening its applicability. Furthermore, the adaptive control method of parameters makes it even more applicable in real world scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The Confidence Gain metric is well-motivated and empirically demonstrated as effective in identifying knowledge conflicts. The design of algorithm is easy to understand and well reasoned.
    *   The CK-PLUG architecture provides a flexible and controllable way to adjust knowledge reliance, verified by varying the parameter on multiple language models to test the effectiveness.
    *   The experiments showcase the ability to fine-tune performance in challenging counterfactual scenarios and to autonomously balances internal and external knowledge by leveraging model confidence metrics.
    *   The method is plug-and-play, requiring no model retraining, and adaptable across different LLMs. This greatly enhances its potential for practical use.
    *   The ablation studies confirm the importance of the conflict detection component.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation focuses heavily on QA tasks with counterfactual contexts, especially for the experiments in knowledge control. More extensive evaluation on diverse real-world RAG applications is needed.
    *   While the Confidence Gain metric is promising, its effectiveness likely depends on the specific LLM and the nature of the knowledge conflict. The algorithm and its threshold parameters need further optimization for more complex and noisy retrieval contexts.
    *   The interpretability analysis, while helpful, could be more thorough. A more detailed analysis of the token-level modulation would strengthen the understanding of CK-PLUG's behavior.
    *   The framework is relatively lightweight; deeper theoretical analyses may be helpful to clarify its effect on a more rigorous level.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   CK-PLUG's knowledge conflict detection and control mechanism may inspire new RAG architectures that more dynamically adapt to different knowledge scenarios, leading to more reliable and trustworthy LLMs.
    *   The plug-and-play approach could encourage broader research into modular components for RAG systems, making them more customizable and adaptable.
    *   The fine-grained control afforded by CK-PLUG may pave the way for safer deployment of LLMs in contexts where factual accuracy is paramount.

*   **Score:** 7.5

*   **Justification:** CK-PLUG is a valuable contribution to the RAG field because it offers a practical and novel method for controlling knowledge reliance. The design of the model is reasonable, and its evaluation with variations over a set of different language models is sufficient to verify the effectiveness of the model. The Confidence Gain metric is well-motivated and empirically demonstrated. Though there are still limitations in comprehensive demonstration, especially in real-world cases, the work's plug-and-play nature, strong empirical validation, and potential to inspire follow-up research warrant a score of 7.5, indicating a significant contribution with considerable potential impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Time After Time: Deep-Q Effect Estimation for Interventions on When and What to do
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15890v1)
- **Authors**: Yoav Wald, Mark Goldstein, Yonathan Efroni, Wouter A. C. van Amsterdam, Rajesh Ranganath
- **Abstract**: Problems in fields such as healthcare, robotics, and finance requires reasoning about the value both of what decision or action to take and when to take it. The prevailing hope is that artificial intelligence will support such decisions by estimating the causal effect of policies such as how to treat patients or how to allocate resources over time. However, existing methods for estimating the effect of a policy struggle with \emph{irregular time}. They either discretize time, or disregard the effect of timing policies. We present a new deep-Q algorithm that estimates the effect of both when and what to do called Earliest Disagreement Q-Evaluation (EDQ). EDQ makes use of recursion for the Q-function that is compatible with flexible sequence models, such as transformers. EDQ provides accurate estimates under standard assumptions. We validate the approach through experiments on survival time and tumor growth tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of off-policy evaluation (OPE) in sequential decision-making scenarios with irregularly spaced time events. Existing methods often discretize time or ignore the timing of interventions, which can be problematic. The authors introduce Earliest Disagreement Q-Evaluation (EDQ), a novel deep Q-learning-based algorithm that leverages recursion and flexible sequence models (like transformers) to estimate the causal effect of policies, considering both *what* action to take and *when*. The algorithm updates the Q-function by using "counterfactual" trajectories, determined by the target policy until the earliest disagreement with the observed policy.  The paper provides theoretical justification for EDQ under standard causal assumptions and validates the approach through experiments on survival time and tumor growth tasks, demonstrating advantages over baselines relying on discretization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in adapting deep Q-learning to the irregular time setting, focusing on interventions on both treatment type *and* timing, and providing a model-free approach that does not require complex integration, reweighting, or restrictive architectures such as differential equation solvers. While Q-learning and FQE are established techniques, their adaptation to continuous, irregularly sampled data, combined with the concept of earliest disagreement to avoid discretization, contributes to a distinct methodological approach. The theoretical backing, connecting the algorithm to identifiability results in continuous-time causal inference, strengthens the contribution.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly relevant to numerous fields, particularly healthcare, where treatment timing is critical. The scalability offered by EDQ, due to its compatibility with sequence models like transformers, makes it potentially valuable for real-world applications involving high-dimensional data and complex treatment plans. The empirical results, although limited to simulations, do provide a concrete demonstration of the algorithm's effectiveness compared to a discretized baseline.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   The EDQ algorithm appears relatively straightforward to implement, given the use of established deep learning architectures.
    *   Provides a strong connection to theoretical causal inference (using Røysland, et al).
    *   Demonstrates clear practical benefits over discretized baselines.
    *   The reliance on dynamic programming makes the approach conceptually straightforward and easily applicable to existing architectures.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Empirical validation relies solely on simulated data. The transferability of these results to real-world datasets with potentially different noise characteristics, confounding variables, or data distributions remains unproven.
    *   While the paper discusses identifiability conditions, it doesn't address the robustness of EDQ to violations of these assumptions.
    *   The computational complexity analysis is high-level. A more detailed comparison, including wall-clock times and resource requirements, would strengthen the paper.
    *   There are limitations to the method due to the assumptions it has to make, and these should be emphasized.
    *   This method does not handle censoring, which is a significant limitation in the area of survival analysis.

*   **Potential Impact:** EDQ has the potential to influence the design of decision support systems in healthcare and other domains where treatment timing is critical. Its model-free nature and adaptability to sequence models make it accessible to a wider range of practitioners. However, the lack of real-world validation necessitates further research to establish its practical utility and address its limitations. The clear limitations should be outlined in detail, and the future work should clearly address them.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially impactful algorithm for a relevant problem. The theoretical guarantees and the clear presentation of the algorithm's benefits over discretization are significant strengths. The major weaknesses are the reliance on simulations, limited discussion of real-world applicability, and lack of discussion on robustness. Overall, EDQ makes a moderate contribution to the field of causal inference in sequential decision-making, but its impact remains to be fully demonstrated through real-world applications. The score of 6.5 reflects this balance between innovation and remaining open questions. The limitations should be discussed, otherwise the score would be even lower.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### CONTHER: Human-Like Contextual Robot Learning via Hindsight Experience Replay and Transformers without Expert Demonstrations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15895v1)
- **Authors**: Maria Makarova, Qian Liu, Dzmitry Tsetserukou
- **Abstract**: This paper presents CONTHER, a novel reinforcement learning algorithm designed to efficiently and rapidly train robotic agents for goal-oriented manipulation tasks and obstacle avoidance. The algorithm uses a modified replay buffer inspired by the Hindsight Experience Replay (HER) approach to artificially populate experience with successful trajectories, effectively addressing the problem of sparse reward scenarios and eliminating the need to manually collect expert demonstrations. The developed algorithm proposes a Transformer-based architecture to incorporate the context of previous states, allowing the agent to perform a deeper analysis and make decisions in a manner more akin to human learning. The effectiveness of the built-in replay buffer, which acts as an "internal demonstrator", is twofold: it accelerates learning and allows the algorithm to adapt to different tasks. Empirical data confirm the superiority of the algorithm by an average of 38.46% over other considered methods, and the most successful baseline by 28.21%, showing higher success rates and faster convergence in the point-reaching task. Since the control is performed through the robot's joints, the algorithm facilitates potential adaptation to a real robot system and construction of an obstacle avoidance task. Therefore, the algorithm has also been tested on tasks requiring following a complex dynamic trajectory and obstacle avoidance. The design of the algorithm ensures its applicability to a wide range of goal-oriented tasks, making it an easily integrated solution for real-world robotics applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CONTHER, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) algorithm for robotic manipulation tasks. CONTHER combines a Transformer-based architecture for context analysis with Hindsight Experience Replay (HER) to address sparse reward scenarios and eliminate the need for expert demonstrations.  The Transformer enables the agent to learn from sequences of past states and actions, while HER generates artificial successful trajectories to improve learning efficiency.  The paper presents empirical results demonstrating that CONTHER outperforms baseline RL algorithms and achieves faster convergence and higher success rates in point-reaching tasks, as well as more complex dynamic path following and obstacle avoidance scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of a Transformer-based architecture with Hindsight Experience Replay in the context of robotic manipulation is moderately novel.  While Transformers and HER have been independently used in RL, their synergistic application for context-aware manipulation is a valuable contribution. The introduction of goals for obstacles, G-, in addition to goals for targets, G+, also adds a layer of novelty for obstacle avoidance tasks. A strength is the detailed description of the CONTHER algorithm's architecture and buffer sampling method. Also, the comparison of CONTHER-v.0 vs CONTHER-v.1 helps highlight the advantage of using explicit information about the last state with contextual information.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in improving the efficiency and effectiveness of RL for robotic manipulation. Overcoming the sparse reward problem and reducing the reliance on expert demonstrations are crucial steps towards deploying robots in real-world environments. The improvement in success rate compared to baseline methods provides a solid basis for the algorithm's effectiveness. The application to path-following tasks demonstrates the ability of the approach to be applied more widely, beyond a single point-reaching goal.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The detailed explanation of the algorithm's architecture and implementation is a significant strength.
    *   The empirical results demonstrate improved performance compared to baselines.
    *   The experimental setup is well-defined and the selection of baseline algorithms relevant.
    *   The exploration of more complex, dynamic trajectories with obstacle avoidance broadens the scope of the study.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments are limited to a simulated environment, and the transition to a real robot system remains to be demonstrated.
    *   The complexity and hyperparameter tuning required for Transformers can be a barrier to wider adoption. Additional analysis should be done on the effect of different hyperparameter settings and how to best optimize for these parameters.
    *   While the experiments are comprehensive, there is no analysis done with respect to computational time and resources to train the model.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in RL for robotics. The context-aware learning approach and efficient exploration strategy could be adopted and extended in other manipulation tasks. The work provides a solid foundation for further research and development in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and valuable contribution to the field of reinforcement learning for robotics. The combination of Transformers and HER, along with the demonstrated improvement in performance and the application to a broader range of tasks, warrants a positive evaluation. However, the lack of real-world experiments and the potential challenges associated with training Transformers limit the immediate impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### On the Limits of Applying Graph Transformers for Brain Connectome Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15902v1)
- **Authors**: Jose Lara-Rangel, Clare Heinbaugh
- **Abstract**: Brain connectomes offer detailed maps of neural connections within the brain. Recent studies have proposed novel connectome graph datasets and attempted to improve connectome classification by using graph deep learning. With recent advances demonstrating transformers' ability to model intricate relationships and outperform in various domains, this work explores their performance on the novel NeuroGraph benchmark datasets and synthetic variants derived from probabilistically removing edges to simulate noisy data. Our findings suggest that graph transformers offer no major advantage over traditional GNNs on this dataset. Furthermore, both traditional and transformer GNN models maintain accuracy even with all edges removed, suggesting that the dataset's graph structures may not significantly impact predictions. We propose further assessing NeuroGraph as a brain connectome benchmark, emphasizing the need for well-curated datasets and improved preprocessing strategies to obtain meaningful edge connections.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of graph transformers (specifically Exphormer, a sparse graph transformer) to brain connectome classification tasks using the NeuroGraph benchmark datasets.  The authors compare the performance of Exphormer to a baseline ResidualGCN model on gender, activity, and age prediction tasks. They find that Exphormer does not significantly outperform ResidualGCN, and both models are susceptible to overfitting.  Furthermore, the authors conduct experiments where edges are randomly removed from the graphs, finding that the performance of both models remains largely unaffected, suggesting that the graph structure in the NeuroGraph datasets may not be crucial for accurate predictions and may be relying primarily on node features. The authors conclude that the NeuroGraph datasets might lack meaningful edge information and call for further investigation into preprocessing strategies to generate more informative graph representations of brain connectomes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of graph transformers (specifically, Exphormer) to the NeuroGraph dataset isn't groundbreaking in itself, given the increasing popularity of graph transformers. The generation of synthetic datasets by removing edges is a useful exploration, although edge deletion experiments have been done before in the broader graph learning literature. The main novel contribution, and perhaps the most insightful one, lies in the **negative finding**: that graph structure might not be as informative as presumed in NeuroGraph, which is a valuable, albeit unexpected, observation.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems primarily from its critical assessment of the NeuroGraph benchmark. By demonstrating that models perform well even with all edges removed, the authors raise important questions about the dataset's construction and whether it adequately captures relevant structural information for brain connectome analysis. This challenge to the validity of a published benchmark is valuable, and could prompt a re-evaluation of preprocessing steps in fMRI data or lead to the development of improved benchmark datasets. While the non-improvement by graph transformers is not in itself a breakthrough, it motivates the insightful critique of the graph structure.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined experiments with clear comparisons between models.
    *   The edge removal experiment is a straightforward and insightful way to probe the importance of graph structure.
    *   The paper directly challenges the assumption that existing graph datasets in brain connectomics necessarily encode meaningful relationships.
    *   The call for better pre-processing pipelines is a constructive suggestion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper focuses on one specific sparse graph transformer (Exphormer). Exploring other transformer architectures could provide a more complete picture.
