### A Survey on fMRI-based Brain Decoding for Reconstructing Multimodal Stimuli
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15978v1)
- **Authors**: Pengyu Liu, Guohua Dong, Dan Guo, Kun Li, Fengling Li, Xun Yang, Meng Wang, Xiaomin Ying
- **Abstract**: In daily life, we encounter diverse external stimuli, such as images, sounds, and videos. As research in multimodal stimuli and neuroscience advances, fMRI-based brain decoding has become a key tool for understanding brain perception and its complex cognitive processes. Decoding brain signals to reconstruct stimuli not only reveals intricate neural mechanisms but also drives progress in AI, disease treatment, and brain-computer interfaces. Recent advancements in neuroimaging and image generation models have significantly improved fMRI-based decoding. While fMRI offers high spatial resolution for precise brain activity mapping, its low temporal resolution and signal noise pose challenges. Meanwhile, techniques like GANs, VAEs, and Diffusion Models have enhanced reconstructed image quality, and multimodal pre-trained models have boosted cross-modal decoding tasks. This survey systematically reviews recent progress in fMRI-based brain decoding, focusing on stimulus reconstruction from passive brain signals. It summarizes datasets, relevant brain regions, and categorizes existing methods by model structure. Additionally, it evaluates model performance and discusses their effectiveness. Finally, it identifies key challenges and proposes future research directions, offering valuable insights for the field. For more information and resources related to this survey, visit https://github.com/LpyNow/BrainDecodingImage.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper is a survey focusing on fMRI-based brain decoding techniques used to reconstruct multimodal stimuli (images, sounds, videos).  It provides a detailed review of datasets used in this field, categorizes existing brain decoding methods based on model architecture (End-to-End, Large-Scale Pretraining, Encoder Alignment, LLM-centric, and Hybrid), and compares their performance both qualitatively and quantitatively. The survey also highlights challenges and proposes future research directions, encompassing both algorithmic improvements and practical applications in areas like artificial intelligence, brain-computer interfaces, and disease treatment. The authors maintain a dedicated Github repository to accompany the survey.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The survey appears to fill a gap in the literature by focusing specifically on the reconstruction of multimodal stimuli from brain signals, particularly using fMRI.  It also concentrates on recent advancements, offering an updated perspective compared to older reviews. The emphasis on model architecture design and task-oriented approaches represents a somewhat novel angle. However, some of the categorization and high-level overview may overlap with existing surveys. The explicit acknowledgement of limitations is a strength.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its comprehensive organization of the field, providing a structured understanding for researchers entering or already working in fMRI-based brain decoding. It consolidates information from disparate publications, offering a single resource for understanding the current state-of-the-art. Identifying and discussing the challenges, along with proposing future directions, is valuable for guiding future research efforts. The identification of the task-oriented approach as a main focal point is quite useful for those starting in the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a wide range of methods, models and datasets, offering a well-rounded picture of the field.
    *   **Clear Categorization:** The classification of brain decoding methods based on model architecture is helpful for understanding the underlying principles of different approaches.
    *   **Balanced Evaluation:** The inclusion of both qualitative and quantitative performance evaluations provides a nuanced understanding of the strengths and weaknesses of different models.
    *   **Forward-Looking Perspective:** The discussion of challenges and future research directions is insightful and valuable for guiding future research efforts.
    *   **Open Resource:**  The accompanying Github repository enhances the survey's utility by providing access to code and resources.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Potential for Overlap:** Surveys in rapidly developing fields like this one risk some overlap with existing review articles, especially at the higher-level overview. Although the authors address this, a more extensive comparative analysis against earlier reviews could strengthen the justification for this work.
    *   **Depth of Evaluation:** While a broad range of methods are discussed, the depth of analysis for each specific technique is inevitably limited. Some discussions are more descriptive than critically analytical.
    *   **Subjectivity in Selection:**  The selection of papers for inclusion in the survey necessarily involves a degree of subjectivity, which could introduce bias. Explicit discussion on the process is needed.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Conclusion:** Many conclusions and future steps can be considered as "common sense" for experts in the field.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to be a valuable resource for researchers in brain decoding, particularly those focusing on multimodal stimuli reconstruction. It can serve as a starting point for new projects and help guide the development of more advanced techniques. The github repository is a valuable resource that allows reproduction and extensibility of the presented study.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

This is a well-written, structured survey that provides a valuable service to the fMRI brain decoding community. It balances breadth with a decent level of depth, clearly categorizes the landscape, and offers actionable insights for future research.  However, a more focused and comparative analysis against existing reviews would be valuable to highlight its unique contribution, along with increased depth of critical analysis rather than summary of each method. More specific future directions can also strengthen this work.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SpiLiFormer: Enhancing Spiking Transformers with Lateral Inhibition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15986v1)
- **Authors**: Zeqi Zheng, Yanchen Huang, Yingchao Yu, Zizheng Zhu, Junfeng Tang, Zhaofei Yu, Yaochu Jin
- **Abstract**: Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) based on Transformers have garnered significant attention due to their superior performance and high energy efficiency. However, the spiking attention modules of most existing Transformer-based SNNs are adapted from those of analog Transformers, failing to fully address the issue of over-allocating attention to irrelevant contexts. To fix this fundamental yet overlooked issue, we propose a Lateral Inhibition-inspired Spiking Transformer (SpiLiFormer). It emulates the brain's lateral inhibition mechanism, guiding the model to enhance attention to relevant tokens while suppressing attention to irrelevant ones. Our model achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance across multiple datasets, including CIFAR-10 (+0.45%), CIFAR-100 (+0.48%), CIFAR10-DVS (+2.70%), N-Caltech101 (+1.94%), and ImageNet-1K (+1.6%). Notably, on the ImageNet-1K dataset, SpiLiFormer (69.9M parameters, 4 time steps, 384 resolution) outperforms E-SpikeFormer (173.0M parameters, 8 time steps, 384 resolution), a SOTA spiking Transformer, by 0.46% using only 39% of the parameters and half the time steps. Our code and training checkpoints will be released upon acceptance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SpiLiFormer: Enhancing Spiking Transformers with Lateral Inhibition" introduces a novel spiking transformer architecture (SpiLiFormer) designed to address the "attention distraction" issue commonly observed in existing Transformer-based Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs).  The core innovation is the incorporation of lateral inhibition, inspired by biological neural processing, to improve focus on relevant image features and suppress irrelevant background information. SpiLiFormer uses two types of lateral inhibition modules: Feedforward-pathway Lateral Differential Inhibition (FF-LiDiff) in shallow layers and Feedback-pathway Lateral Differential Inhibition (FB-LiDiff) in deeper layers. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across several datasets (CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, CIFAR10-DVS, N-Caltech101, ImageNet-1K) and show enhanced robustness against adversarial attacks and noise.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The concept of applying lateral inhibition to spiking transformers to mitigate attention distraction is reasonably novel.  While lateral inhibition has been used in SNNs before, its specific application within a transformer architecture and its partitioning into feedforward and feedback mechanisms presents a unique contribution. The creation of two specific attention modules, FF-LiDiff and FB-LiDiff, is also a notable engineering contribution.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing a crucial weakness of current SNNs: their tendency to be overly sensitive to irrelevant information.  By improving attention mechanisms, SpiLiFormer not only achieves better accuracy but also enhances robustness and potentially improves energy efficiency (though this is discussed more theoretically than experimentally). The improved performance on ImageNet-1K, a large-scale dataset, is particularly significant, demonstrating the potential of spiking transformers to bridge the gap with ANNs.  The use of the model leads to decreased parameter size in comparison to SOTA Spike-Driven Transformers (i.e E-SpikeFormer), which is of great benefit.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents convincing empirical evidence across multiple datasets, demonstrating consistent performance improvements over existing SNN architectures.
    * **Biologically Inspired Design:** The incorporation of lateral inhibition, a well-established principle in neuroscience, grounds the approach in biological plausibility, aligning with the goals of SNN research.
    * **Robustness Analysis:** The adversarial testing and attention heatmap visualizations provide valuable insights into the model's behavior and demonstrate its improved robustness to noise and adversarial attacks.
    * **Clear and well presented work:** A good amount of detail provided to recreate this work for the use of others.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Theoretical Energy Savings:** While the paper mentions potential energy efficiency benefits, a detailed experimental analysis of energy consumption comparing SpiLiFormer to other SNNs on dedicated hardware is lacking.  Without this, the claim of enhanced energy efficiency remains somewhat speculative.
    * **Ablation Study:** Although the ablation study analyses the FF-LiDiff and FB-LiDiff attention modules, it would be very beneficial to determine how different quantities of layers and parameters in those attention mechanisms would influence the performance to create a more effective model.
    * **Clarity of Implementation:** The architectural descriptions were a little abstract and could have had greater benefits from diagrams and flow-charts to convey information.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has a strong potential to influence the field of spiking neural networks.  It offers a practical solution to a common problem, demonstrates impressive results, and provides a novel architectural design that can be further explored and adapted by other researchers. It also could generate interesting questions on the influence of different attention mechanisms in Spiking Neural Networks.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents significant advancements, the lack of concrete energy efficiency analysis and slight clarity in the architectural description prevents it from being a perfect score.  The strong empirical results and the potential for broad impact warrant a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.NE
- **Score**: 8/10

### ECKGBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models in E-commerce Leveraging Knowledge Graph
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15990v1)
- **Authors**: Langming Liu, Haibin Chen, Yuhao Wang, Yujin Yuan, Shilei Liu, Wenbo Su, Xiangyu Zhao, Bo Zheng
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated their capabilities across various NLP tasks. Their potential in e-commerce is also substantial, evidenced by practical implementations such as platform search, personalized recommendations, and customer service. One primary concern associated with LLMs is their factuality (e.g., hallucination), which is urgent in e-commerce due to its significant impact on user experience and revenue. Despite some methods proposed to evaluate LLMs' factuality, issues such as lack of reliability, high consumption, and lack of domain expertise leave a gap between effective assessment in e-commerce. To bridge the evaluation gap, we propose ECKGBench, a dataset specifically designed to evaluate the capacities of LLMs in e-commerce knowledge. Specifically, we adopt a standardized workflow to automatically generate questions based on a large-scale knowledge graph, guaranteeing sufficient reliability. We employ the simple question-answering paradigm, substantially improving the evaluation efficiency by the least input and output tokens. Furthermore, we inject abundant e-commerce expertise in each evaluation stage, including human annotation, prompt design, negative sampling, and verification. Besides, we explore the LLMs' knowledge boundaries in e-commerce from a novel perspective. Through comprehensive evaluations of several advanced LLMs on ECKGBench, we provide meticulous analysis and insights into leveraging LLMs for e-commerce.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ECKGBench, a new benchmark dataset specifically designed for evaluating the factuality of large language models (LLMs) in the e-commerce domain.  The authors identify shortcomings in existing factuality evaluation methods, particularly their lack of reliability, high computational cost, and insufficient domain expertise. ECKGBench addresses these issues by leveraging a large-scale e-commerce knowledge graph (ECKG) to automatically generate multiple-choice questions with guaranteed ground truths.  The dataset incorporates e-commerce domain expertise in various stages, including relation templating, negative sampling, and question verification. The authors explore different types of e-commerce knowledge (common vs. abstract) and propose metrics to analyze LLMs' knowledge boundaries in this domain. Experimental results on several LLMs, including both closed-source and open-source models, are presented, along with ablation studies to demonstrate the contribution of each component of the dataset generation process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Gap:** The paper clearly identifies a crucial gap in the evaluation of LLMs, specifically the lack of specialized benchmarks for e-commerce applications.
    *   **Knowledge Graph Foundation:**  Using an e-commerce knowledge graph as the data source provides a reliable and structured basis for question generation, addressing the factuality concerns often associated with LLMs.
    *   **Automated Generation:** The automated question generation process with negative sampling and multiple-choice format significantly improves efficiency and scalability compared to methods relying on human judgment or retrieval models.
    *   **Incorporation of Domain Expertise:**  The careful design of relation templates, the use of LLMs for fine-grained negative sampling with explicit requirements, and human verification steps demonstrate a strong commitment to incorporating domain expertise, resulting in a high-quality dataset.
    *   **Knowledge Boundary Analysis:** The novel approach to exploring LLMs' knowledge boundaries in e-commerce is insightful and provides a valuable method for understanding model capabilities and guiding future research.
    *   **Thorough Experiments and Analysis:** The comprehensive evaluation of various LLMs, including both closed-source and open-source models, and the ablation studies provide a solid foundation for the benchmark.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Data Source Limitations:**  The dataset is based on data from Taobao (Alibaba), which might introduce a bias towards that specific e-commerce ecosystem.  The generalizability of the benchmark to other e-commerce platforms or regions could be limited.
    *   **Chinese Specificity:** While the abstract claims are general, the examples and ECKG source point heavily to a Chinese e-commerce setting. The impact on LLMs with limited Chinese language skills is unknown.
    *   **Multiple-Choice Format:** While the multiple-choice format improves efficiency, it may not fully capture the complexities of real-world e-commerce scenarios where open-ended responses are often required. The risk is the evaluation is simplified beyond reality.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Evaluation Paradigm:** Using KG data with automatically generated multiple choice questions isn't particularly new (it is well established in the KG completion literature, for example); the main novel element is the construction and application to e-commerce.
    *   **Accessibility:** There are currently no plans to release the ECKG to the public.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper is relatively novel in its application of knowledge graph-based evaluation to the specific domain of e-commerce.  The combination of automated question generation, domain-specific knowledge incorporation, and knowledge boundary analysis represents a significant step forward in assessing LLMs' capabilities in this important area. However, the reliance on data from a single platform and the multiple-choice format somewhat limit the broader impact.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    ECKGBench has the potential to become a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners working on LLMs for e-commerce.  It can facilitate the development of more reliable and effective LLMs for various applications, such as product search, recommendation, and customer service. The knowledge boundary analysis framework can also inform the design of targeted fine-tuning strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution by creating a specialized, high-quality benchmark for evaluating LLMs in the e-commerce domain. The innovative use of a knowledge graph and automated question generation techniques improves the efficiency and reliability of the evaluation process. However, the limitations associated with the data source (Taobao specificity), reliance on the multiple-choice format, and a lack of novelty in the evaluation paradigm constrain the overall impact. For these reasons, the paper deserves a solid but not exceptional score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Animating the Uncaptured: Humanoid Mesh Animation with Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15996v1)
- **Authors**: Marc Benedí San Millán, Angela Dai, Matthias Nießner
- **Abstract**: Animation of humanoid characters is essential in various graphics applications, but requires significant time and cost to create realistic animations. We propose an approach to synthesize 4D animated sequences of input static 3D humanoid meshes, leveraging strong generalized motion priors from generative video models -- as such video models contain powerful motion information covering a wide variety of human motions. From an input static 3D humanoid mesh and a text prompt describing the desired animation, we synthesize a corresponding video conditioned on a rendered image of the 3D mesh. We then employ an underlying SMPL representation to animate the corresponding 3D mesh according to the video-generated motion, based on our motion optimization. This enables a cost-effective and accessible solution to enable the synthesis of diverse and realistic 4D animations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Animating the Uncaptured: Humanoid Mesh Animation with Video Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel method for animating static 3D humanoid meshes using text prompts. The core idea is to leverage the powerful motion priors learned by video diffusion models (VDMs). Given a 3D mesh and a text description of the desired motion, the method generates a video of the mesh performing the action using a text-to-video VDM.  The motion is then transferred to the original mesh by registering the SMPL body model, extracting 2D landmarks, silhouettes, and dense features from the generated video, and optimizing the SMPL parameters to match the video's motion. This approach provides a cost-effective way to create diverse and realistic 4D animations without requiring extensive motion capture data or manual animation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in combining video diffusion models with SMPL body parameterization and robust tracking to animate static 3D meshes. This is a clever approach to sidestepping the limitations of small 4D human motion datasets and leveraging the rich motion information captured in VDMs. The specific combination of techniques, including SMPL registration, multi-feature tracking (landmarks, silhouettes, dense features), and MLP-based parameter prediction, contributes to the robustness of the motion transfer. While individual components are not necessarily groundbreaking on their own, the specific orchestration of these elements to achieve text-to-3D-animation represents a significant contribution.

**Significance:**  The ability to easily animate 3D meshes from text has broad applications in computer graphics, including game development, virtual reality, and character creation. The method's accessibility, relying on text prompts and existing 3D meshes, makes it a valuable tool for both experienced animators and those with limited animation expertise. The paper tackles a practical problem and offers a potentially impactful solution.

**Strengths:**

*   **Effective use of VDMs:**  The paper demonstrates the power of VDMs in capturing realistic motion priors, which are then successfully transferred to 3D meshes.
*   **Robust tracking:** The combination of sparse and dense features for motion tracking enhances the accuracy and stability of the animation.
*   **SMPL parameterization:** Using the SMPL body model as a deformation proxy helps to regularize the animation and maintain realistic human poses.
*   **User Study:** Demonstrates significant perceptual preference over competing methods.
*   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the method and its components.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on VDMs:** The method's performance relies heavily on the quality of the underlying VDM. Artifacts or limitations of the VDM will be directly reflected in the resulting animation.
*   **SMPL Limitations:** SMPL, while effective, has inherent limitations in representing clothing, hair, and complex deformations.  The method, as described, will struggle with animations requiring significant deviations from the basic SMPL body model.
*   **Ethical Considerations:** While the paper briefly touches on the ethical implications of potential misuse, the discussion is relatively shallow. A more thorough analysis of potential biases or harms associated with generated human motion would strengthen the paper.
*   **Limited Quantitative Evaluation:** While the video tracking evaluation is solid, a broader quantitative evaluation of the overall animation quality (beyond tracking accuracy) would be beneficial.

**Potential Impact:**

The paper's approach is likely to stimulate further research in text-to-animation and the use of VDMs for 3D content creation. Its potential for practical applications makes it an important contribution to the field. This work encourages further exploration into incorporating more complex character models, fine-grained control of animation styles, and addressing the ethical challenges associated with generating realistic human motion.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**
The paper presents a novel and effective approach to animating 3D meshes from text, leveraging powerful motion priors from video diffusion models. The technical contributions, particularly the robust tracking pipeline and the orchestration of various components, are noteworthy. The method's potential impact on accessibility and efficiency in animation makes it significant. However, limitations associated with VDM reliance, SMPL model constraints, and ethical considerations prevent it from scoring higher. Further, whilst the proposed solution demonstrated robust performance, I would argue that the individual components are less novel, and the overall result does not represent a seismic shift in the field, hence I feel a '7.5' score is appropriately justified.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### SenseExpo: Efficient Autonomous Exploration with Prediction Information from Lightweight Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16000v1)
- **Authors**: Haojia Gao, Haohua Que, Hoiian Au, Weihao Shan, Mingkai Liu, Yusen Qin, Lei Mu, Rong Zhao, Xinghua Yang, Qi Wei, Fei Qiao
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes SenseExpo, an efficient autonomous exploration framework based on a lightweight prediction network, which addresses the limitations of traditional methods in computational overhead and environmental generalization. By integrating Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), Transformer, and Fast Fourier Convolution (FFC), we designed a lightweight prediction model with merely 709k parameters. Our smallest model achieves better performance on the KTH dataset than U-net (24.5M) and LaMa (51M), delivering PSNR 9.026 and SSIM 0.718, particularly representing a 38.7% PSNR improvement over the 51M-parameter LaMa model. Cross-domain testing demonstrates its strong generalization capability, with an FID score of 161.55 on the HouseExpo dataset, significantly outperforming comparable methods. Regarding exploration efficiency, on the KTH dataset,SenseExpo demonstrates approximately a 67.9% time reduction in exploration time compared to MapEx. On the MRPB 1.0 dataset, SenseExpo achieves 77.1% time reduction roughly compared to MapEx. Deployed as a plug-and-play ROS node, the framework seamlessly integrates with existing navigation systems, providing an efficient solution for resource-constrained devices.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper "SenseExpo: Efficient Autonomous Exploration with Prediction Information from Lightweight Neural Networks":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SenseExpo, a new autonomous exploration framework that uses a lightweight prediction network to enhance exploration efficiency, particularly for resource-constrained robots.  The core innovation lies in a compact prediction model built using GANs, Transformers, and Fast Fourier Convolutions (FFC). This model predicts local occupancy maps, which are then integrated to guide exploration. The framework is implemented as a ROS node and tested on standard datasets (KTH, HouseExpo, MRPB 1.0), demonstrating improved exploration speed and generalization compared to existing methods like MapEx and frontier-based approaches.  The lightweight nature of the model is emphasized, highlighting its suitability for robots with limited computational resources.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of GANs, Transformers, and FFC in a *local* map prediction network for autonomous exploration has merit. The emphasis on a lightweight model is a significant selling point, addressing a crucial constraint in robotics. However, the components themselves (GANs, Transformers, FFC) are not novel, but their *integrated* application within this *specific* problem domain and with a focus on *resource efficiency* represents a valuable contribution. Local map prediction is also a good approach, as it's much faster and lighter than global prediction. It's worth noting that similar approaches exist, like UPEN, but SenseExpo offers a compelling combination of efficiency and accuracy. The plug-and-play ROS node is useful, but a standard addition rather than a significant innovation.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is its potential to make prediction-based autonomous exploration more practical. The demonstration of improved exploration efficiency and generalization is promising. Addressing resource constraints opens the door for deploying these techniques on a wider range of robotic platforms. The improved performance compared to MapEx and Frontier-based methods is well-supported by experimental results on several datasets.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Lightweight Model:** A significant focus on reducing computational cost and model size.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** Thorough evaluation on multiple datasets demonstrates superior exploration efficiency and generalization.
    *   **ROS Integration:** Practical implementation as a ROS node for easy integration with existing robotic systems.
    *   **Local Prediction:** Addressing computational challenges and improving generalization, and much faster.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the integration of GANs, Transformers, and FFC is valuable, the individual components are well-established.
    *   **Dataset limitations:** While KTH, HouseExpo, and MRPB1.0 are widely used, the demonstration on larger, real-world datasets would significantly strengthen the paper.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies to justify and evaluate the individual contribution of GANs, Transformers and FFC would further strenghten the paper.
*   **Impact:** The paper can have a considerable impact on the robotics community. A lightweight prediction model can enable many robotic systems to use prediction information from neural networks.
*   **Clarity:** Paper is well written and well organized.
*   **Experimental Details:** More details on the robots used in the experiments, and the specific computation resources available is needed.

**Justification for Score:**

A score of 7 reflects the combination of novelty and significance. While the individual components of SenseExpo are not groundbreaking, the integrated approach to create a lightweight and efficient exploration framework is valuable. The strong experimental results and the ROS integration enhance the practical impact of the work. A higher score is not justified due to the incremental nature of the novelty and the need for validation on more complex, real-world datasets.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### "This could save us months of work" -- Use Cases of AI and Automation Support in Investigative Journalism
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16011v1)
- **Authors**: Besjon Cifliku, Hendrik Heuer
- **Abstract**: As the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) expand, more researchers are studying their adoption in newsrooms. However, much of the research focus remains broad and does not address the specific technical needs of investigative journalists. Therefore, this paper presents several applied use cases where automation and AI intersect with investigative journalism. We conducted a within-subjects user study with eight investigative journalists. In interviews, we elicited practical use cases using a speculative design approach by having journalists react to a prototype of a system that combines LLMs and Programming-by-Demonstration (PbD) to simplify data collection on numerous websites. Based on user reports, we classified the journalistic processes into data collecting and reporting. Participants indicated they utilize automation to handle repetitive tasks like content monitoring, web scraping, summarization, and preliminary data exploration. Following these insights, we provide guidelines on how investigative journalism can benefit from AI and automation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper explores the use of AI and automation to support investigative journalism. The authors conducted a within-subjects study with eight investigative journalists to elicit practical use cases. Participants reacted to a prototype system combining Large Language Models (LLMs) and Programming-by-Demonstration (PbD) to simplify data collection from websites.  The study identified key journalistic processes where automation is utilized: content monitoring, web scraping, summarization, and preliminary data exploration. Based on these insights, the paper offers guidelines for leveraging AI and automation in investigative journalism.  The study identifies user requirements and features for an automation framework, using a Human-in-the-Loop (HITL) approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific focus on the intersection of LLMs, PbD, and investigative journalism. While research on computational journalism and the use of AI in newsrooms exists, this study distinguishes itself by concentrating on *investigative* journalism and employing a tool-based, co-creation approach directly involving journalists in the design process using an LLM-integrated PbD prototype. Previous works have been more broad in scope, looking at overall trends, whereas this goes for a specific and actionable intervention. The use of speculative design with a functional prototype to elicit use cases is a strong aspect.

**Significance:** The paper has the potential to be significant for several reasons. First, it addresses a practical need in the field of investigative journalism by identifying specific tasks that can be automated to improve efficiency and allow journalists to focus on higher-level analysis and storytelling. Second, it contributes a taxonomy of automation scenarios grounded in real-world journalist workflows, which can serve as a foundation for developing new tools. Finally, the insights into the challenges of using automation in journalistic settings, such as concerns about reliability, accountability, and data literacy, are valuable for researchers and practitioners alike.

**Strengths:**

*   **User-Centered Approach:** The study adopts a strong user-centered approach, deeply involving investigative journalists in the co-creation process. This helps ensure that the proposed solutions are relevant and practical.
*   **Specific Focus:** The narrow focus on *investigative* journalism, rather than journalism in general, allows for a more targeted and actionable set of findings.
*   **Elicitation Prototype:** The use of a tangible prototype (JournalXRecorder) facilitated the elicitation of detailed use cases. The prototype provided a concrete basis for discussion, allowing journalists to better understand and articulate their needs.
*   **Detailed Use Cases:**  The paper provides a good selection of concrete use cases classified into data collection and reporting categories.
* HITL paradigm allows for feedback during the design process, this helps better tailor the solutions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Small Sample Size:**  The sample size of eight participants is relatively small, which could limit the generalizability of the findings.  The authors acknowledge this limitation.
*   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses primarily on data collection and analysis aspects. There are few insights of how the automation framework would effect reporting and dissemination of news.
*   **Lack of User Interaction with Prototype:** Although users reacted to a demonstration of the prototype, they did not have hands-on experience with it. Allowing users to interact directly with the tool could have elicited even more valuable insights.
* The paper primarily focuses on journalists based in Germany and primarily focuses on OSINT (Open Source Intelligence) specialists.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of new tools and technologies for investigative journalism. By providing a clear understanding of the specific needs and challenges faced by journalists, the paper can guide researchers and developers in creating solutions that are truly useful and impactful.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by focusing on a specific area within journalism, employs user-centric methodology, and creates a functional prototype to elicit user needs. The study is practical and yields actionable insights that help researchers develop better tools for journalist.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### GraspCoT: Integrating Physical Property Reasoning for 6-DoF Grasping under Flexible Language Instructions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16013v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaomeng Chu, Jiajun Deng, Guoliang You, Wei Liu, Xingchen Li, Jianmin Ji, Yanyong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Flexible instruction-guided 6-DoF grasping is a significant yet challenging task for real-world robotic systems. Existing methods utilize the contextual understanding capabilities of the large language models (LLMs) to establish mappings between expressions and targets, allowing robots to comprehend users' intentions in the instructions. However, the LLM's knowledge about objects' physical properties remains underexplored despite its tight relevance to grasping. In this work, we propose GraspCoT, a 6-DoF grasp detection framework that integrates a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning mechanism oriented to physical properties, guided by auxiliary question-answering (QA) tasks. Particularly, we design a set of QA templates to enable hierarchical reasoning that includes three stages: target parsing, physical property analysis, and grasp action selection. Moreover, GraspCoT presents a unified multimodal LLM architecture, which encodes multi-view observations of 3D scenes into 3D-aware visual tokens, and then jointly embeds these visual tokens with CoT-derived textual tokens within LLMs to generate grasp pose predictions. Furthermore, we present IntentGrasp, a large-scale benchmark that fills the gap in public datasets for multi-object grasp detection under diverse and indirect verbal commands. Extensive experiments on IntentGrasp demonstrate the superiority of our method, with additional validation in real-world robotic applications confirming its practicality. Codes and data will be released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GraspCoT: Integrating Physical Property Reasoning for 6-DoF Grasping under Flexible Language Instructions."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GraspCoT, a novel framework for 6-DoF grasp detection guided by flexible language instructions.  It addresses the limitations of existing methods that primarily focus on semantic understanding and user intent by explicitly incorporating reasoning about the physical properties of objects.  GraspCoT achieves this through a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning mechanism guided by auxiliary question-answering (QA) tasks. This CoT framework includes three stages: target parsing, physical property analysis, and grasp action selection. The architecture integrates visual information (multi-view 3D scene representations encoded into 3D-aware tokens) and textual information (flexible instructions and CoT-derived textual tokens) within a unified multimodal LLM. The paper also contributes IntentGrasp, a new large-scale benchmark designed to evaluate multi-object grasp detection under diverse and indirect verbal commands. Experimental results on IntentGrasp and real-world robot applications demonstrate the effectiveness of GraspCoT.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The integration of physical property reasoning into a flexible-instruction grasp detection framework is a genuine contribution. Previous work has touched upon semantic understanding and intention, but the explicit modeling of physical attributes using CoT is a relatively unexplored area.  The design of QA templates specifically tailored for physical property reasoning is also a novel element.  The IntentGrasp benchmark fills a significant gap in the evaluation of flexible instruction-based grasping.  The unified multimodal architecture, although inspired by recent advances in 3D MLLMs, is tailored to the specifics of the grasping task and the incorporation of the CoT reasoning results.
* **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the robustness and adaptability of robotic grasping systems in real-world scenarios. By considering physical properties, robots can make more informed decisions about how to grasp objects, reducing the risk of damage or failure. Flexible language instructions are also crucial for natural human-robot interaction. The IntentGrasp benchmark will likely become a valuable resource for the community, facilitating further research in this area.  The real-world experiments, while limited, provide a promising initial validation of GraspCoT's practical applicability.
* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem statement and motivation.
    * Well-defined and explained methodology (GraspCoT architecture, CoT reasoning, QA templates, IntentGrasp benchmark).
    * Comprehensive experimental evaluation on a new benchmark.
    * Demonstrates improved performance compared to existing methods.
    * Practical real-world validation.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The real-world experiments, while present, could be more extensive. A more detailed analysis of failure cases in the real-world deployment would be beneficial.
    *  While the CoT reasoning is structured, there is a limitation on how well it works in a dynamic or unpredictable environment. The LLM has the potential to misinterpret the situation or come to a wrong conclusion due to the complexity of the environment.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper offers a significant advance in flexible language-guided robotic grasping by integrating physical property reasoning. The novelty of the approach, the introduction of a new benchmark (IntentGrasp), and the demonstration of improved performance in both simulated and real-world settings justify a high score.  The weaknesses are relatively minor, and the potential impact of the work on the field is substantial.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 8/10

### Autonomous AI imitators increase diversity in homogeneous information ecosystems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16021v1)
- **Authors**: Emil Bakkensen Johansen, Oliver Baumann
- **Abstract**: Recent breakthroughs in large language models (LLMs) have facilitated autonomous AI agents capable of imitating human-generated content. This technological advancement raises fundamental questions about AI's potential impact on the diversity and democratic value of information ecosystems. Here, we introduce a large-scale simulation framework to examine AI-based imitation in news, a context critically influential for public discourse. By systematically testing two distinct imitation strategies across a range of information environments varying in initial diversity, we demonstrate that AI-generated articles do not uniformly homogenize content. Instead, AI's influence is strongly context-dependent: AI-generated articles can introduce valuable diversity in originally homogeneous news environments, while potentially diminishing diversity in contexts that initially display high heterogeneity. These results illustrate that the baseline diversity of an information space critically shapes AI's impact, challenging assumptions that AI-driven imitation uniformly threatens information diversity. Instead, when information is initially homogeneous, AI-driven imitation can expand perspectives, styles, and topics. This is especially important in news contexts, where information diversity fosters richer public debate by exposing citizens to alternative viewpoints, challenging biases, and preventing narrative monopolies, which is essential for a resilient democracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the impact of autonomous AI imitators on the diversity of news ecosystems using a large-scale simulation framework. The study models AI agents that generate news articles either by imitating a single source or by synthesizing multiple sources. The simulations are conducted across various information environments with differing initial levels of diversity. The key finding is that AI-driven imitation does not uniformly homogenize content. Instead, its effect is context-dependent: in initially homogeneous news environments, AI imitation can *increase* diversity, while in heterogeneous environments, it may *decrease* diversity by filtering out outlier perspectives. The paper argues that understanding the baseline diversity is crucial for predicting the impact of AI on information ecosystems and calls for policymakers to consider the status quo before making regulatory decisions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Question:** The paper addresses a crucial and timely question about the impact of AI on news diversity, moving beyond simple assumptions of homogenization.
*   **Rigorous Methodology:** The large-scale simulation framework, utilizing a comprehensive dataset of Danish news articles, provides a robust platform for exploring different scenarios. The multi-world simulation setup is particularly valuable for analyzing complex interactions.
*   **Nuanced Findings:** The discovery that AI's impact on diversity is context-dependent is a significant contribution. This finding challenges simplistic views about AI and encourages more nuanced discussions.
*   **Theoretical Contribution:** The "gravity well" metaphor offers a helpful way to conceptualize how LLMs interact with and reshape existing information distributions.
*   **Practical Implications:** The paper highlights the importance of considering the existing structure of information environments when evaluating the impact of AI and developing policies.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Context Specificity:** The study is based on the Danish news ecosystem, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other countries or cultural contexts. While Denmark provides a stable, easily measurable context, the findings may not hold in environments with different market structures or levels of regulatory oversight.
*   **Model Simplifications:** As with any simulation, the model simplifies reality. Factors such as the dynamic interaction between human journalists and AI, or the potential for evolving imitation strategies, are not fully captured.
*   **Metric Limitations:** Relying solely on cosine similarity to measure diversity has limitations. This approach may not fully capture semantic nuances or the importance of different types of diversity (e.g., viewpoint diversity, source diversity). Furthermore, the reliance on article features such as sentiment analysis may provide insight, but these features are easily gamed and do not provide strong evidence.
*   **Lack of Human Validation:** The study does not include a validation step involving human readers or expert assessments. This would strengthen the findings by confirming that the model's measure of diversity aligns with human perceptions.
*   **Limited exploration of the policy space:** While highlighting policy implications, the paper offers only high-level recommendations, rather than specific proposals, and the regulatory implications are under-explored.
*   **LLM Specifics:** Reliance on GPT-4o is a limiting factor, especially as more sophisticated and open-source LLMs become available. The results may be contingent on the specific pretraining of this model, and additional evaluation must be done.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper provides a novel and important counterpoint to the prevailing view that AI-driven imitation will uniformly homogenize content. The context-dependent findings are significant because they suggest that AI can be a tool for *promoting* diversity in certain circumstances. The study's strength lies in its rigorous methodology and the complexity of its simulation design. The paper makes a valuable contribution to the ongoing debate about AI's societal impact.

**Justification for Score:**

The study presents a well-executed analysis on an important and timely topic. It utilizes a solid methodological framework and presents thought-provoking findings. However, it also has certain limitations relating to the specificity of the data it is drawn from and potential simplifications it has to make regarding the complexities of the news ecosystem. The paper makes a strong contribution to ongoing debates about AI's impact on news, and the methodology is strong. However, the lack of human validation and limited exploration of policy implications also lead to a slightly lower score.

### Corrective In-Context Learning: Evaluating Self-Correction in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16022v1)
- **Authors**: Mario Sanz-Guerrero, Katharina von der Wense
- **Abstract**: In-context learning (ICL) has transformed the use of large language models (LLMs) for NLP tasks, enabling few-shot learning by conditioning on labeled examples without finetuning. Despite its effectiveness, ICL is prone to errors, especially for challenging examples. With the goal of improving the performance of ICL, we propose corrective in-context learning (CICL), an approach that incorporates a model's incorrect predictions alongside ground truth corrections into the prompt, aiming to enhance classification accuracy through self-correction. However, contrary to our hypothesis, extensive experiments on text classification tasks demonstrate that CICL consistently underperforms standard ICL, with performance degrading as the proportion of corrections in the prompt increases. Our findings indicate that CICL introduces confusion by disrupting the model's task understanding, rather than refining its predictions. Additionally, we observe that presenting harder examples in standard ICL does not improve performance, suggesting that example difficulty alone may not be a reliable criterion for effective selection. By presenting these negative results, we provide important insights into the limitations of self-corrective mechanisms in LLMs and offer directions for future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Corrective In-Context Learning: Evaluating Self-Correction in Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates a novel approach called Corrective In-Context Learning (CICL) to improve the performance of In-Context Learning (ICL) in large language models (LLMs).  The core idea is to provide the LLM with its own incorrect predictions alongside the ground truth corrections within the prompt. The authors hypothesized that this feedback mechanism would enable the model to refine its understanding and make more accurate subsequent predictions. However, the experimental results on text classification tasks revealed that CICL consistently *underperforms* standard ICL. Furthermore, the performance degrades as the proportion of corrections in the prompt increases. The authors also found that presenting harder examples in standard ICL does not consistently improve performance.  The paper concludes that CICL, contrary to initial intuition, introduces confusion and disrupts the model's task understanding, and that simply presenting harder examples is not a reliable strategy for improving ICL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using model errors as part of the prompt for self-correction in ICL is moderately novel.  While self-correction and iterative refinement are not entirely new concepts in LLMs, the *specific* implementation of incorporating predicted labels *alongside* correct labels *within the ICL framework* is a distinguishable approach. While prior work like Monea et al. (2024) explored binary reward signals, this paper investigates explicit ground truth corrections, adding a subtle but crucial distinction.

*   **Significance:** The *negative* results are significant. While it's common for publications to showcase successes, it's equally crucial to understand what *doesn't* work.  By rigorously demonstrating the failure of CICL, the authors provide valuable insights into the limitations of current LLMs' self-correction capabilities. This can prevent future research from pursuing similar unproductive avenues and guide the community towards more promising approaches. Furthermore, the finding that simply adding "harder" examples does not necessarily improve standard ICL is also a key contribution because it challenges a common intuition about ICL.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Methodology:** The CICL approach is clearly defined and explained, enabling reproducibility.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:**  The experiments are conducted across 17 diverse text classification datasets and four LLMs, providing robust evidence for the findings.
    *   **Statistical Analysis:** The use of statistical tests (Wilcoxon signed-rank test, Kruskal-Wallis test) adds rigor to the analysis and strengthens the claims.
    *   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is easy to read and logically structured, making it accessible to a broad audience.
    *   **Emphasis on Negative Results:** The authors don't shy away from presenting negative findings, which contribute more to the understanding of LLM limitations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Scale:**  The experiments are limited to relatively smaller models (up to 8B parameters, but preliminary tests also used 70B) due to computational constraints. While they found that results with 70B Llama-3.1 were similar to the smaller models, it would be useful to see if the trend holds for much larger models. It is possible that much larger models might be more capable of extracting information from the corrected examples.
    *   **Task Specificity:** The evaluation is focused solely on text classification. It's possible that CICL might be more effective for other tasks, such as tasks that involve multi-step reasoning or generation tasks where refining intermediate steps with corrective feedback might be more beneficial. The paper acknowledges this limitation.
    *   **Prompt Engineering:** The paper notes that LLMs are sensitive to prompt design and that different prompt formats could potentially yield better results for CICL.

*   **Impact:** The paper is impactful because it is valuable for informing the design of more effective LLM learning methods. Because researchers are aware of self-corrective limitations, they can focus on more reliable methods.

**Overall:**

This paper offers a solid contribution to the understanding of ICL and self-correction in LLMs. While the proposed CICL approach doesn't work as hypothesized, the negative results are valuable and well-supported by experimental evidence. The findings challenge intuition and provide a foundation for future research directions.

### BadToken: Token-level Backdoor Attacks to Multi-modal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16023v1)
- **Authors**: Zenghui Yuan, Jiawen Shi, Pan Zhou, Neil Zhenqiang Gong, Lichao Sun
- **Abstract**: Multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) extend large language models (LLMs) to process multi-modal information, enabling them to generate responses to image-text inputs. MLLMs have been incorporated into diverse multi-modal applications, such as autonomous driving and medical diagnosis, via plug-and-play without fine-tuning. This deployment paradigm increases the vulnerability of MLLMs to backdoor attacks. However, existing backdoor attacks against MLLMs achieve limited effectiveness and stealthiness. In this work, we propose BadToken, the first token-level backdoor attack to MLLMs. BadToken introduces two novel backdoor behaviors: Token-substitution and Token-addition, which enable flexible and stealthy attacks by making token-level modifications to the original output for backdoored inputs. We formulate a general optimization problem that considers the two backdoor behaviors to maximize the attack effectiveness. We evaluate BadToken on two open-source MLLMs and various tasks. Our results show that our attack maintains the model's utility while achieving high attack success rates and stealthiness. We also show the real-world threats of BadToken in two scenarios, i.e., autonomous driving and medical diagnosis. Furthermore, we consider defenses including fine-tuning and input purification. Our results highlight the threat of our attack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BadToken, a novel token-level backdoor attack targeting Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Unlike existing backdoor attacks on MLLMs that often generate fixed or easily detectable outputs, BadToken manipulates the MLLM's output at the token level, using two key behaviors: Token-substitution (replacing a source token with a target token) and Token-addition (appending a malicious token sequence).  The authors formulate an optimization problem to balance attack effectiveness (achieving desired backdoor behaviors) and utility (maintaining performance on clean inputs). They evaluate BadToken on open-source MLLMs across various tasks and real-world scenarios (autonomous driving and medical diagnosis), demonstrating its stealthiness and threat potential. Finally, the paper examines the resilience of BadToken against white-box and black-box defenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of token-level manipulation is a significant step forward in backdoor attacks on MLLMs. It moves beyond simple trigger-based output generation, allowing for more nuanced and subtle attacks. Existing attacks are limited to generate fixed sequences. Introducing token substitution and addition as attack vectors is novel and demonstrates a good understanding of the generative capabilities of MLLMs and how to leverage them for malicious purposes. The formulation of the optimization problem, balancing effectiveness and utility, is also a positive contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the growing reliance on MLLMs in real-world applications, often deployed as plug-and-play solutions or through APIs without extensive fine-tuning by the end-user. This creates a vulnerability, and BadToken highlights this vulnerability effectively. The demonstrated ability to manipulate outputs in autonomous driving and medical diagnosis scenarios raises serious ethical and safety concerns. The exploration of defenses, even if limited, is crucial for guiding future research in securing MLLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and well-defined attack model.
    *   Novel and flexible attack strategy.
    *   Extensive experimental evaluation on multiple MLLMs and tasks.
    *   Demonstration of real-world threat scenarios.
    *   Analysis of the effectiveness of potential defenses.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The defenses explored are somewhat basic (fine-tuning and zero-shot purification). The defense analysis is superficial. A more comprehensive investigation of other defense strategies (e.g., input sanitization, anomaly detection of outputs) would be beneficial.
    *   The paper acknowledges the need for stealthier triggers but primarily focuses on easily deployable triggers (logos, watermarks). Exploring less conspicuous triggers would strengthen the attack's real-world relevance.
    *   While the attack shows strong ASR, a deeper analysis of the semantic impact of token-level manipulations on the overall output's coherence and factual correctness is warranted. Does the token substitution subtly alter the intended meaning in unintended ways, even without activating the backdoor?

*   **Influence on the Field:** BadToken is likely to influence future research in several ways:

    *   It will encourage the development of more robust defense mechanisms specifically targeting token-level manipulations.
    *   It will inspire investigations into other types of subtle backdoor attacks that exploit the generative capabilities of MLLMs.
    *   It will raise awareness among MLLM developers and users about the importance of security considerations in plug-and-play deployments.

*   **Justification for the Score:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed attack that exposes a critical vulnerability in MLLMs. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the threat potential. The limitations in defense exploration and trigger stealthiness prevent a higher score. Nevertheless, BadToken represents a significant contribution to the field of MLLM security.

**Score: 7.5**
