### CaKE: Circuit-aware Editing Enables Generalizable Knowledge Learners
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16356v1)
- **Authors**: Yunzhi Yao, Jizhan Fang, Jia-Chen Gu, Ningyu Zhang, Shumin Deng, Huajun Chen, Nanyun Peng
- **Abstract**: Knowledge Editing (KE) enables the modification of outdated or incorrect information in large language models (LLMs). While existing KE methods can update isolated facts, they struggle to generalize these updates to multi-hop reasoning tasks that depend on the modified knowledge. Through an analysis of reasoning circuits -- the neural pathways LLMs use for knowledge-based inference, we observe that current layer-localized KE approaches, such as MEMIT and WISE, which edit only single or a few model layers, struggle to effectively incorporate updated information into these reasoning pathways. To address this limitation, we propose CaKE (Circuit-aware Knowledge Editing), a novel method that enables more effective integration of updated knowledge in LLMs. CaKE leverages strategically curated data, guided by our circuits-based analysis, that enforces the model to utilize the modified knowledge, stimulating the model to develop appropriate reasoning circuits for newly integrated knowledge. Experimental results show that CaKE enables more accurate and consistent use of updated knowledge across related reasoning tasks, leading to an average of 20% improvement in multi-hop reasoning accuracy on MQuAKE dataset compared to existing KE methods. We release the code and data in https://github.com/zjunlp/CaKE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CaKE: Circuit-aware Editing Enables Generalizable Knowledge Learners":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CaKE (Circuit-aware Knowledge Editing), a novel method for improving the generalizability of knowledge editing (KE) in large language models (LLMs). The core idea is that existing KE methods, which primarily focus on local parameter adjustments, often fail to effectively propagate updated knowledge through the LLM's reasoning circuits, hindering performance on multi-hop reasoning tasks. CaKE addresses this limitation by: (1) analyzing LLM reasoning circuits to understand how knowledge is used in multi-hop inference; (2) generating circuit-aware training data that forces the model to leverage modified knowledge and develop appropriate reasoning circuits; and (3) fine-tuning the LLM using this data. Experiments on the MQuAKE dataset demonstrate that CaKE significantly improves multi-hop reasoning accuracy compared to existing KE methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its circuit-aware approach to knowledge editing. While previous work has explored reasoning circuits in LLMs, this paper is among the first to explicitly design a KE method that accounts for and leverages these circuits. The idea of using circuit-aware training data to force the model to integrate updated knowledge into its reasoning process is also a novel contribution.  The creation and strategic use of circuit-aware data, guided by a thorough circuit analysis, distinguishes this paper from other KE methods which often target parameter adjustments.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical limitation of existing KE methods – their lack of generalizability to multi-hop reasoning. This is significant because real-world applications of KE often require models to reason with updated knowledge across multiple steps.  The empirical results demonstrate a substantial improvement in multi-hop reasoning accuracy, suggesting that CaKE has the potential to make KE more practical and useful. The paper offers valuable insights into the interplay between KE strategies and LLM reasoning architectures. By addressing this disconnect, the paper paves the way for more effective and generalizable knowledge integration.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-Motivated:** The paper clearly identifies a key problem with existing KE methods and provides a strong rationale for its circuit-aware approach, supported by experimental analysis of LLM reasoning circuits. The visual representations, especially of the reasoning circuit failures, are effective in communicating the problem being addressed.
    *   **Technically Sound:** The CaKE method is well-defined and the data generation strategy is clearly explained. The approach to mitigating potential data leakage is a thoughtful touch.
    *   **Empirically Validated:** The experiments on MQuAKE are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of CaKE across different LLMs and dataset versions. The locality experiments help to show that improvements are not coming at the cost of forgetting unrelated knowledge.
    *   **Release of Code and Data:** This enhances reproducibility and allows others to build upon the work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** While MQuAKE is a standard benchmark, it may not fully capture the complexity of real-world multi-hop reasoning. The experiments and analysis could be further strengthened by including a wider variety of tasks and knowledge domains.
    *   **Scalability:** While results are shown for a 70B model, a more thorough exploration of the method's scaling properties would be valuable.  The data generation process might become computationally expensive for larger models or datasets.
    *   **Limited comparison with recent works:** More recently there have been several works improving knowledge editing using different techniques, some of which tackle multi-hop reasoning to some extent. More comparison to these methods could strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in KE by shifting the focus from isolated parameter adjustments to more holistic, circuit-aware approaches.  It could also inspire new techniques for generating training data that better aligns with LLM reasoning architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

CaKE represents a significant advance in the field of knowledge editing, particularly in addressing the challenge of generalizable knowledge integration for multi-hop reasoning.  The circuit-aware approach, data generation strategy, and empirical validation demonstrate a clear and well-executed contribution. While certain limitations exist, as outlined above, the core ideas presented are both novel and impactful. The paper opens up new avenues for future research and development in KE. For these reasons, a high score is justified.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LaPIG: Cross-Modal Generation of Paired Thermal and Visible Facial Images
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16376v1)
- **Authors**: Leyang Wang, Joice Lin
- **Abstract**: The success of modern machine learning, particularly in facial translation networks, is highly dependent on the availability of high-quality, paired, large-scale datasets. However, acquiring sufficient data is often challenging and costly. Inspired by the recent success of diffusion models in high-quality image synthesis and advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs), we propose a novel framework called LLM-assisted Paired Image Generation (LaPIG). This framework enables the construction of comprehensive, high-quality paired visible and thermal images using captions generated by LLMs. Our method encompasses three parts: visible image synthesis with ArcFace embedding, thermal image translation using Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs), and caption generation with LLMs. Our approach not only generates multi-view paired visible and thermal images to increase data diversity but also produces high-quality paired data while maintaining their identity information. We evaluate our method on public datasets by comparing it with existing methods, demonstrating the superiority of LaPIG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LaPIG: Cross-Modal Generation of Paired Thermal and Visible Facial Images" presents a novel framework for generating paired visible and thermal facial images using Large Language Model (LLM) assistance. The proposed method, called LLM-assisted Paired Image Generation (LaPIG), leverages LLMs to generate captions from visible images, enhancing control over identity preservation. The framework consists of three components: visible image synthesis with ArcFace embedding, thermal image translation using Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs), and caption generation with LLMs. The paper demonstrates that LaPIG can generate high-quality paired data while maintaining identity information, and it achieves superior performance compared to existing methods on public datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the integration of LLM-generated captions to improve identity preservation in cross-modal (visible-to-thermal) facial image generation. The use of LLMs to enrich textual descriptions with comprehensive identity-specific information and subsequently incorporate this into a diffusion-based model is a relatively new approach.  While individual components like ArcFace embeddings and LDMs are established, their combination with LLM-derived context specifically for this cross-modal generation task distinguishes the work. The training-free nature of the method also adds to its novelty.

* **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in addressing the scarcity of high-quality, paired visible and thermal facial datasets. Generating synthetic data with good fidelity and identity consistency is crucial for various downstream tasks, like facial recognition and security applications, especially in scenarios where thermal imaging is essential (e.g., low-light conditions).  The paper's ability to synthesize multi-view paired images with preserved identity could significantly impact the development and performance of models in these areas.

* **Strengths:**
    * **High-Quality Results:** The paper demonstrates superior quantitative and qualitative results compared to existing methods, indicating effective identity preservation and realistic image synthesis.
    * **Efficient Data Generation:** LaPIG offers a time- and cost-effective alternative to manual data collection, which is a significant advantage for research and development in this field.
    * **Modular Design:** The framework's modularity allows for potential improvements by swapping out individual components (e.g., using different ID encoders or diffusion models).
    * **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a well-structured and detailed description of the proposed method, making it easier to understand and reproduce.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dataset Dependency:** While the framework uses LLMs to augment identity information, the base performance still depends on the quality and diversity of the initial ID images used as input. The paper could explore limitations related to pose and expression variations in the input set V.
    * **Limited Scope of Applications:** The paper primarily focuses on facial recognition as a downstream application. Exploring other potential applications (e.g., medical diagnosis, human behavior analysis) could further strengthen its impact.
    * **Computation Cost:** While it's more efficient than manual data collection, the computational cost of LDM-based image synthesis and LLM inference is still a consideration, particularly for large-scale dataset generation. The paper could delve further into strategies for reducing computational overhead.
    * **Generalization:** The experiments are conducted on two datasets, and it would be beneficial to evaluate the method's performance on a broader range of datasets to assess its generalization capability.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust and accurate facial recognition systems, especially those operating in challenging conditions.  The LLM-assisted synthetic data generation approach could be extended to other cross-modal image synthesis tasks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed framework for generating paired visible and thermal facial images. The integration of LLMs to enhance identity preservation is a significant contribution. The paper demonstrates its superior performance through comprehensive experiments and addresses the limitations of existing methods. However, there is room for further improvement by exploring dataset limitations, expanding the scope of applications, and assessing computational efficiency. The score reflects its solid contribution with the potential for broader impact with further development. The method is novel enough, yet builds off of existing tech in a meaningful way and the empirical findings are sound.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deconstructing Long Chain-of-Thought: A Structured Reasoning Optimization Framework for Long CoT Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16385v1)
- **Authors**: Yijia Luo, Yulin Song, Xingyao Zhang, Jiaheng Liu, Weixun Wang, GengRu Chen, Wenbo Su, Bo Zheng
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable reasoning capabilities through long chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning. The R1 distillation scheme has emerged as a promising approach for training cost-effective models with enhanced reasoning abilities. However, the underlying mechanisms driving its effectiveness remain unclear. This study examines the universality of distillation data and identifies key components that enable the efficient transfer of long-chain reasoning capabilities in LLM distillation. Our findings reveal that the effectiveness of long CoT reasoning distillation from teacher models like Qwen-QwQ degrades significantly on nonhomologous models, challenging the assumed universality of current distillation methods. To gain deeper insights into the structure and patterns of long CoT reasoning, we propose DLCoT (Deconstructing Long Chain-of-Thought), a distillation data enhancement framework. DLCoT consists of three key steps: (1) data segmentation to decompose complex long CoT structures, (2) simplification by eliminating unsolvable and redundant solutions, and (3) optimization of intermediate error states. Our approach significantly improves model performance and token efficiency, facilitating the development of high-performance LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Deconstructing Long Chain-of-Thought: A Structured Reasoning Optimization Framework for Long CoT Distillation" addresses the problem of efficiently distilling long chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning capabilities from large language models (LLMs) into smaller, more cost-effective models.  The authors identify that simply distilling from models like DeepSeek's R1 or QwQ is not universally effective, particularly when the student model differs significantly from the teacher. They propose DLCoT (Deconstructing Long Chain-of-Thought), a three-step framework that enhances distillation data by: (1) segmenting CoT structures, (2) simplifying them by removing unsolvable and redundant solutions, and (3) optimizing intermediate error states. The experimental results show that DLCoT improves model performance and token efficiency. The key finding is that the richness of approaches within the reasoning trunk is crucial for effective long CoT distillation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has some novelty in its analysis of long CoT structures and the proposal of DLCoT. While the idea of distillation is not new, the specific deconstruction and optimization of CoT data for distillation purposes, focusing on the "reasoning trunk" is a worthwhile contribution. The observation that the "richness of approaches" is more important than the absolute correctness of every step is also a non-trivial insight. Prior works focus on fixing errors but this paper proposes to enhance diversity.

*   **Significance:** The problem of distilling reasoning capabilities into smaller models is highly relevant. DLCoT provides a practical approach to this challenge, potentially enabling broader adoption of LLMs in resource-constrained environments. The token efficiency improvement is also a significant practical benefit.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear explanation of the DLCoT framework.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of DLCoT across different models and benchmarks.
    *   The ablation studies offer valuable insights into the relative importance of different components of the framework.
    *   The structured analysis of CoT data, leading to the "reasoning trunk" concept, is a strong contribution.
    *  The paper identifies the key to effective long CoT distillation: the richness of approaches within the reasoning trunk.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The descriptions of the algorithms and experimental setup can be a bit vague (e.g., the specifics of similarity clustering in the Redundancy Analysis stage). The appendix helps, but more detail in the main paper would be useful.
    *   The "minimal-edit rewriting" in the Coherence Reconstruction step could benefit from more detail and justification. How is this done, and why is it effective?
    *   While the results are promising, the long CoT distillation is still a relatively new field. It would be great to compare DLCoT to more of recent and diverse baseline to demonstrate it is a more efficient way of distillation.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical framework for improving the efficiency of CoT distillation. The insights into the structure of long CoTs could also inform future research on reasoning in LLMs. The finding on the reasoning trunk highlights new research directions.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The value of DLCoT comes from its structured approach to optimize long CoT, which addresses the limitations of current universal distillation methods.
Overall, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM distillation. DLCoT addresses a practical problem, and the experimental results validate its effectiveness. The identified need to balance the diversity of the trunk solution also has positive implications on future research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Do Visual Imaginations Improve Vision-and-Language Navigation Agents?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16394v1)
- **Authors**: Akhil Perincherry, Jacob Krantz, Stefan Lee
- **Abstract**: Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) agents are tasked with navigating an unseen environment using natural language instructions. In this work, we study if visual representations of sub-goals implied by the instructions can serve as navigational cues and lead to increased navigation performance. To synthesize these visual representations or imaginations, we leverage a text-to-image diffusion model on landmark references contained in segmented instructions. These imaginations are provided to VLN agents as an added modality to act as landmark cues and an auxiliary loss is added to explicitly encourage relating these with their corresponding referring expressions. Our findings reveal an increase in success rate (SR) of around 1 point and up to 0.5 points in success scaled by inverse path length (SPL) across agents. These results suggest that the proposed approach reinforces visual understanding compared to relying on language instructions alone. Code and data for our work can be found at https://www.akhilperincherry.com/VLN-Imagine-website/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates whether incorporating visual representations ("imaginations") of sub-goals derived from natural language navigation instructions can improve the performance of vision-and-language navigation (VLN) agents. The authors leverage a text-to-image diffusion model to generate these visual imaginations from landmark references within segmented instructions.  The generated images are then provided as an additional modality to VLN agents, alongside an auxiliary loss that encourages the agents to relate the imaginations to their corresponding textual expressions. Experiments on the Room-2-Room (R2R) and REVERIE datasets demonstrate improvements in success rate (SR) and success scaled by inverse path length (SPL) for existing VLN models, HAMT and DUET. The results suggest that this approach enhances visual understanding beyond relying solely on language instructions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its explicit integration of visual imaginations, synthesized using a text-to-image diffusion model, into the VLN pipeline. While using external knowledge sources and auxiliary tasks to improve VLN is not entirely new (e.g., ADAPT), the specific combination of leveraging modern text-to-image models for generating task-relevant visual landmarks and then explicitly encouraging alignment via a loss function represents a worthwhile contribution. The generation of a dataset of text-to-image imaginations also adds value. However, similar ideas have been explored in other embodiment settings like tabletop manipulation. This dilutes novelty to some extent.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to enhance VLN agents' ability to ground language in visual environments. By explicitly providing visual cues that correspond to landmark references in the instructions, the approach potentially addresses weaknesses in cross-modal alignment mechanisms within standard VLN architectures. The performance gains on R2R and REVERIE, while modest (~1 SR and ~0.5 SPL), support the validity of the approach. The qualitative results and ablation studies further provide insight into the benefits of visual imagination. However, the quantitative improvement margins are not that substantial.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined methodology.
    *   The use of a modern text-to-image diffusion model (SDXL) is relevant and appropriate.
    *   The agent-agnostic approach allows for integration into different VLN architectures (HAMT, DUET).
    *   The introduction of the auxiliary loss helps align the imaginations with the instructions.
    *   Experimental results on R2R and REVERIE demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   Ablation studies provide valuable insights into the design choices.
    * Qualitative examples strengthen the understanding of the method

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The improvements in SR and SPL are relatively modest, especially for REVERIE. The gains may not be impactful enough to justify the additional computational cost associated with generating and processing visual imaginations.
    *   The reliance on a text-to-image model introduces a dependency on its performance and biases. The generated imaginations may not always accurately reflect the actual visual characteristics of the environment.
    *   The paper does not fully explore the limitations of the approach in out-of-distribution scenarios where the text-to-image model struggles to generate accurate or relevant imaginations.
    * Limited generalization to REVERIE
    * Cost of generating images is significant.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could inspire further research on leveraging generative models to enhance embodied AI agents. The approach could be extended to other tasks that involve language grounding and visual reasoning. The R2R-Imagine dataset also has value in itself. Also, the work highlights the potential benefits of incorporating cognitive science insights, such as mental imagery, into AI systems.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and well-executed approach with promising results, the relatively modest performance gains and limitations related to the reliability of the text-to-image model prevent a higher score. The impact, while potentially significant, is not yet fully demonstrated due to the small performance improvements. The novelty is diminished by similar ideas in other embodiment contexts. Therefore, a score of 6 reflects the value of the work while acknowledging its limitations and the need for further research to fully realize its potential.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### SV4D 2.0: Enhancing Spatio-Temporal Consistency in Multi-View Video Diffusion for High-Quality 4D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16396v1)
- **Authors**: Chun-Han Yao, Yiming Xie, Vikram Voleti, Huaizu Jiang, Varun Jampani
- **Abstract**: We present Stable Video 4D 2.0 (SV4D 2.0), a multi-view video diffusion model for dynamic 3D asset generation. Compared to its predecessor SV4D, SV4D 2.0 is more robust to occlusions and large motion, generalizes better to real-world videos, and produces higher-quality outputs in terms of detail sharpness and spatio-temporal consistency. We achieve this by introducing key improvements in multiple aspects: 1) network architecture: eliminating the dependency of reference multi-views and designing blending mechanism for 3D and frame attention, 2) data: enhancing quality and quantity of training data, 3) training strategy: adopting progressive 3D-4D training for better generalization, and 4) 4D optimization: handling 3D inconsistency and large motion via 2-stage refinement and progressive frame sampling. Extensive experiments demonstrate significant performance gain by SV4D 2.0 both visually and quantitatively, achieving better detail (-14\% LPIPS) and 4D consistency (-44\% FV4D) in novel-view video synthesis and 4D optimization (-12\% LPIPS and -24\% FV4D) compared to SV4D. Project page: https://sv4d2.0.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SV4D 2.0 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents Stable Video 4D 2.0 (SV4D 2.0), an improved multi-view video diffusion model for generating dynamic 3D assets from monocular videos.  Building upon its predecessor, SV4D, the new model introduces several key improvements: a modified network architecture (removing reference multi-view dependency, incorporating 3D attention), enhanced and curated training data, a progressive 3D-to-4D training strategy, and a two-stage 4D optimization scheme. These improvements lead to higher-quality outputs, with better detail sharpness, spatio-temporal consistency, robustness to occlusions and large motion, and improved generalization to real-world videos. The model achieves state-of-the-art performance on novel-view video synthesis and 4D optimization tasks compared to SV4D and other baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents several important modifications to the original SV4D framework, each addressing specific limitations. Removing the dependency on reference multi-views is a significant architectural improvement that enhances robustness and practicality, enabling more flexible inference and avoiding issues with conflicting initial views. The introduction of 3D attention and α-blending further improves spatial consistency.  Data curation is also important and results in better performance. A progressive 3D-to-4D training strategy and the two-stage optimization scheme are also sensible improvements. However, the individual components (3D attention, progressive training) are not entirely new in the broader field. The novelty lies primarily in their combination and adaptation within the SV4D framework to address the specific challenges of dynamic 3D asset generation from monocular video.
*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates substantial improvements in the quality and consistency of dynamic 3D asset generation.  The ability to generate high-fidelity, temporally coherent multi-view videos from monocular input has significant implications for various applications, including content creation, video games, and AR/VR.  Generalizing to real-world videos is crucial, extending the applicability of the technology beyond purely synthetic data. It sets a new state-of-the-art for this task, providing a strong foundation for future research and development.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed and executed experiments with quantitative and qualitative results demonstrating the effectiveness of each component of SV4D 2.0.
    *   Comprehensive ablative study justifying design choices.
    *   Good generalization performance on real-world datasets, demonstrating its practical relevance.
    *   The improved ability to handle occlusions and large motions compared to prior works.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The camera model is limited to azimuth and elevation changes, restricting the applicability of the model to more complex camera motions.
    *   Focus is primarily on object-centric videos; extending it to scene-level dynamic 3D generation is still a challenge.
    *   Some individual components of the approach (e.g., 3D attention, progressive training) are not entirely novel on their own but their novel combination within the given task is original.
    *   Computational cost is still relatively high (21 minutes for 4D optimization) although generation is very fast.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7**. While the individual components are not groundbreaking in isolation, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field by effectively combining and adapting existing techniques within a novel framework for dynamic 3D asset generation. The architectural improvements (removal of reference dependency, incorporation of 3D attention), the enhanced data curation, and the progressive training strategy all contribute to a significant improvement in output quality, robustness, and generalizability. It pushes the state-of-the-art, enabling better performance than other frameworks and demonstrates that the system actually works on difficult video datasets. The system is practical, enabling very fast generation. The remaining limitations related to the camera model and scene-level generalization offer directions for future research.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Scale-wise Distillation of Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16397v1)
- **Authors**: Nikita Starodubcev, Denis Kuznedelev, Artem Babenko, Dmitry Baranchuk
- **Abstract**: We present SwD, a scale-wise distillation framework for diffusion models (DMs), which effectively employs next-scale prediction ideas for diffusion-based few-step generators. In more detail, SwD is inspired by the recent insights relating diffusion processes to the implicit spectral autoregression. We suppose that DMs can initiate generation at lower data resolutions and gradually upscale the samples at each denoising step without loss in performance while significantly reducing computational costs. SwD naturally integrates this idea into existing diffusion distillation methods based on distribution matching. Also, we enrich the family of distribution matching approaches by introducing a novel patch loss enforcing finer-grained similarity to the target distribution. When applied to state-of-the-art text-to-image diffusion models, SwD approaches the inference times of two full resolution steps and significantly outperforms the counterparts under the same computation budget, as evidenced by automated metrics and human preference studies.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces Scale-wise Distillation (SWD), a novel training framework for accelerating text-to-image diffusion models. SWD generates images at gradually increasing resolutions during the denoising process, capitalizing on the observation that high frequencies are masked by noise at early timesteps, allowing for computation at lower resolutions. The authors propose a patch distribution matching (PDM) loss to better align the distribution of generated and target image patches, and integrate SWD with DMD2, a state-of-the-art diffusion distillation method. Experiments show SWD outperforms comparable distilled models under a similar computation budget and achieves competitive or even superior results compared to more computationally intensive approaches. They validate the effectiveness of SWD through automated metrics and human evaluations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of scale-wise processing in diffusion models is somewhat inspired by previous work on next-scale prediction and the observation of spectral autoregression properties of diffusion models. However, the explicit integration of scale-wise generation *within* the distillation process, rather than as a pre- or post-processing step, is a significant point of novelty. The PDM loss also contributes a new element to the distribution matching strategies for distillation. While MMD has been used in GANs and generative modeling before, its application specifically to patch distributions on intermediate features of pretrained DMs in a distillation context is innovative.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical bottleneck in diffusion models: computational cost, particularly for high-resolution image generation. The results demonstrate substantial speedups in generation and training while maintaining or improving image quality, indicating a genuine practical impact. The fact that the authors can use a "poor" teacher like SDXL, one that struggles to meaningfully generate low-resolution images, and still yield plausible distilled results underscores the robustness and practical value of SWD.  However, the improvement in quality is not uniformly observed across all evaluation metrics. There are some metrics where the full resolution distilled model does as well or better than SWD.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a well-motivated approach with clear connections to existing research.
    *   The experimental setup is comprehensive, covering various models, metrics, and ablation studies.
    *   The human evaluation provides strong evidence for the effectiveness of SWD.
    *   The qualitative results visually demonstrate the benefits of the approach.
    *   The PDM loss provides a simple, easy to implement, and effective regularization mechanism.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The gains depend significantly on the quality of the low-resolution teacher model.
    *   While the paper explores various configurations and ablation studies, further analysis of the optimal scale schedule could be beneficial.  The paper provides some justification for the 256x256 starting resolution but doesn't rigorously explore different scale schedules or dynamic approaches to scale determination.
    *   The paper primarily uses synthetic data for the distillation process. Performance on real-world data may differ.
    *   The novelty, while valuable, builds on existing ideas (next-scale prediction, spectral properties). This lowers the overall impact relative to a truly groundbreaking innovation.

*   **Potential Influence:** SWD has the potential to influence the development of more efficient diffusion models, particularly in resource-constrained environments. The idea of scaling resolution during generation could be further explored in other generative models and potentially lead to new architectural designs. The PDM loss is a valuable addition to the distillation toolbox and could find broader applications.  The method is fairly straightforward to implement, a quality that will increase its adoption.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and practical technique for accelerating diffusion model training and inference. The results are compelling, and the approach is well-motivated. The novelty is good, but not exceptional, as it builds on existing ideas. The paper has clear potential to influence the field, but the limitations of the approach (dependence on teacher model, potential for further optimization) prevent it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ScalingNoise: Scaling Inference-Time Search for Generating Infinite Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16400v1)
- **Authors**: Haolin Yang, Feilong Tang, Ming Hu, Yulong Li, Junjie Guo, Yexin Liu, Zelin Peng, Junjun He, Zongyuan Ge, Imran Razzak
- **Abstract**: Video diffusion models (VDMs) facilitate the generation of high-quality videos, with current research predominantly concentrated on scaling efforts during training through improvements in data quality, computational resources, and model complexity. However, inference-time scaling has received less attention, with most approaches restricting models to a single generation attempt. Recent studies have uncovered the existence of "golden noises" that can enhance video quality during generation. Building on this, we find that guiding the scaling inference-time search of VDMs to identify better noise candidates not only evaluates the quality of the frames generated in the current step but also preserves the high-level object features by referencing the anchor frame from previous multi-chunks, thereby delivering long-term value. Our analysis reveals that diffusion models inherently possess flexible adjustments of computation by varying denoising steps, and even a one-step denoising approach, when guided by a reward signal, yields significant long-term benefits. Based on the observation, we proposeScalingNoise, a plug-and-play inference-time search strategy that identifies golden initial noises for the diffusion sampling process to improve global content consistency and visual diversity. Specifically, we perform one-step denoising to convert initial noises into a clip and subsequently evaluate its long-term value, leveraging a reward model anchored by previously generated content. Moreover, to preserve diversity, we sample candidates from a tilted noise distribution that up-weights promising noises. In this way, ScalingNoise significantly reduces noise-induced errors, ensuring more coherent and spatiotemporally consistent video generation. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate that the proposed ScalingNoise effectively improves long video generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ScalingNoise: Scaling Inference-Time Search for Generating Infinite Videos":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ScalingNoise, a novel inference-time search strategy to enhance the consistency and quality of long video generation using Video Diffusion Models (VDMs).  The method addresses the problem of accumulated errors and visual degradation that often occur in long video sequences generated chunk-by-chunk or using FIFO-Diffusion methods. ScalingNoise performs a step-by-step beam search for suitable initial noises, guided by a reward model that incorporates an anchor frame from previous chunks.  This allows for both the evaluation of frame quality in the current step *and* the preservation of high-level object features across long sequences.  A key component is a one-step denoising approach, guided by a reward signal, to efficiently evaluate candidate noises, thereby reducing computational cost. The paper proposes a tilted noise distribution to sample candidates that up-weight promising noises, further improving content consistency and visual diversity. Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate improvements in content fidelity and subject consistency, particularly for resource-constrained long video generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper proposes a potentially novel inference-time search strategy. While inference-time scaling and search have been explored in LLMs and, to some extent, in image diffusion models, the application to long video generation with explicit long-term consistency supervision is a distinct contribution. The one-step denoising evaluation method and the integration of an anchor frame reward model contribute to the novelty. The tilted noise distribution sampling adds another layer of refinement.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in long video generation: the accumulation of errors and inconsistencies. The proposed method offers a practical approach to mitigate these issues, especially within limited computational resources. The improvement in subject consistency and content fidelity, as claimed by the authors, can improve the visual appeal and quality of long videos. If proven effective, could influence subsequent research in long video generation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of long-term consistency in long video generation.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** ScalingNoise is presented in a structured and understandable manner, with clear descriptions of its components (one-step denoising, reward model, tilted sampling).
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper reports quantitative results on benchmark datasets (VBench and UCF-101), comparing against relevant baselines.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** Example videos give insight into the visual enhancements.
    *   **Ablation studies:** Ablation studies provides insights on the contribution of individual method components, leading to a better understating of the proposed approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Technical Depth/Innovation:**  While the proposed approach combines existing components (beam search, DDIM, reward models), it may lack significant deep technical innovation. The main contribution appears to be in carefully combining and adapting these components for the specific task of long video generation.
    *   **Dependence on Reward Model:** The performance hinges on the quality of the reward model. The paper primarily uses DINO, but further exploration of different reward models and their impact would strengthen the work.
    *   **One-Step Denoising Approximation:** The one-step denoising shortcut, while efficient, is an approximation that could potentially discard some better candidate noises. There needs to be an in-depth study on the pros and cons of using this approach.
    *   **Quantitative Metric Reliance:** The primary quantitative results rely on VBench, a learned video evaluation metric. There are valid concerns about the trustworthiness and bias embedded within. Human subjective assessment of longer videos could provide a more solid validation.
    *   **Clarity needed for Ablation studies:** More rigorous ablations and associated explanations are needed, especially related to the tilted sampling method, which includes several components (A1, A2, A3 and A4), to better demonstrate the importance of each element.
    *   **Lack of code release**: The absence of publicly available code hinder the impact of the research by making reproduction of the work challenging.

*   **Impact:** The paper's impact will depend on how well ScalingNoise generalizes to other VDM architectures and datasets. If the method proves to be a reliable and efficient way to improve consistency in long video generation, it could be adopted in various applications and inspire further research in inference-time optimization for generative models.

*   **Overall Assessment:** While the paper introduces a practical and potentially useful technique for improving long video generation, the degree of technical innovation seems incremental. The novelty resides in the *integration* of known methods to specifically address the long-term consistency challenge, rather than a breakthrough in diffusion modeling itself.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid, well-executed engineering contribution, but the degree of novelty is not exceptionally high. The strengths lie in the problem definition, clear method description, and empirical validation. The weaknesses include the dependence on reward model quality, the potential limitations of one-step denoising, and relying heavily on automated metrics. ScalingNoise addresses a pertinent problem and could have a reasonable impact on the field, but the relatively low level of groundbreaking technical insight warrants a score in the mid-range.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Exploring the Hidden Reasoning Process of Large Language Models by Misleading Them
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16401v1)
- **Authors**: Guanyu Chen, Peiyang Wang, Tianren Zhang, Feng Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) and Vision language models (VLMs) have been able to perform various forms of reasoning tasks in a wide range of scenarios, but are they truly engaging in task abstraction and rule-based reasoning beyond mere memorization and pattern matching? To answer this question, we propose a novel experimental approach, Misleading Fine-Tuning (MisFT), to examine whether LLMs/VLMs perform abstract reasoning by altering their original understanding of fundamental rules. In particular, by constructing a dataset with math expressions that contradict correct operation principles, we fine-tune the model to learn those contradictory rules and assess its generalization ability on different test domains. Through a series of experiments, we find that current LLMs/VLMs are capable of effectively applying contradictory rules to solve practical math word problems and math expressions represented by images, implying the presence of an internal mechanism that abstracts before reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel evaluation paradigm, Misleading Fine-Tuning (MisFT), to probe the reasoning abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision Language Models (VLMs). The key idea is to fine-tune LLMs/VLMs on datasets that contain intentionally incorrect or contradictory rules (e.g., "4 + 6 = 12").  The researchers then evaluate the model's ability to generalize these misleading rules to other tasks, such as solving math word problems or interpreting math expressions in images. By observing how well LLMs/VLMs apply the incorrect rules in new contexts, the authors aim to determine if the models are truly engaging in abstract reasoning or merely memorizing patterns from pre-training data. The experiments demonstrate that LLMs/VLMs can effectively apply these contradictory rules, suggesting the presence of an internal mechanism that abstracts before reasoning. The paper explores variations of MisFT, including number overloading, operator overloading, and partial parameter freezing during fine-tuning to further analyze the model's reasoning process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The MisFT paradigm is genuinely novel.  Existing approaches to evaluate LLM reasoning often struggle with data contamination. MisFT cleverly addresses this by fine-tuning on contradictory rules, making it highly unlikely the behavior originates from pre-training data. The concept of misleading the LLM to see how it adapts and generalizes is a fresh perspective.
*   **Significance:** The findings have significant implications for our understanding of LLM reasoning. The paper presents compelling evidence that LLMs might possess a basic form of abstract reasoning, going beyond mere memorization.  This challenges the skepticism surrounding LLMs' true understanding. The study explores the location of abstraction in the model, and the ability to reason with vision demonstrates a broader understanding.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses Data Contamination:** The central strength is MisFT's inherent defense against data contamination.
    *   **Careful Experiment Design:** The various experiments (number overloading, operator overloading, VLM application) are well-designed to dissect different facets of LLM reasoning. The negative control where models are not able to generalize when shallow layers are frozen is a helpful confirmation of the importance of the deep layers for abstract reasoning.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The exploration of different fine-tuning strategies, model sizes, and modalities provides a relatively comprehensive view.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Scope of Reasoning:** While the paper shows LLMs abstract and generalize within the domain of math, the extent of this ability to different forms of knowledge and reasoning is limited. More specifically, the ability to generalize rules only tested math capabilities and not others, such as knowledge graphs.
    *   **Over-interpretation:** The paper sometimes leans towards over-interpreting the results, suggesting a "two-stage abstraction-reasoning mechanism" as a definite fact when the evidence is still suggestive. Further investigation needs to focus on more types of knowledge and applications.
    *   **Limited Dataset Size:** The dataset size for multimodal finetuning (~1.5k) is pretty limited and can be a limiting factor for complex learnings.

*   **Potential Influence:** The MisFT paradigm has the potential to become a standard tool for evaluating LLM reasoning. The paper's findings will likely stimulate further research into the internal mechanisms of LLMs and how abstract knowledge is represented. The result offers a potentially important message for LLM development. The paper also opens the possibility for LLMs to generalize into more complex rule-based systems or knowledge-based systems.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the above points, I assign a score of 8.

*   **Rationale:** The paper presents a highly novel and methodologically sound approach to addressing a fundamental question about LLM reasoning. The MisFT paradigm is a significant contribution, and the results provide compelling evidence that LLMs can abstract and generalize beyond memorization. While the scope is currently limited to mathematical reasoning, the potential for broader application is evident. I reduced the score from a higher level due to the potentially overly-strong interpretations and relatively small dataset size. Despite these limitations, the strengths far outweigh the weaknesses, making this a high-impact contribution to the field.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### VerbDiff: Text-Only Diffusion Models with Enhanced Interaction Awareness
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16406v1)
- **Authors**: SeungJu Cha, Kwanyoung Lee, Ye-Chan Kim, Hyunwoo Oh, Dong-Jin Kim
- **Abstract**: Recent large-scale text-to-image diffusion models generate photorealistic images but often struggle to accurately depict interactions between humans and objects due to their limited ability to differentiate various interaction words. In this work, we propose VerbDiff to address the challenge of capturing nuanced interactions within text-to-image diffusion models. VerbDiff is a novel text-to-image generation model that weakens the bias between interaction words and objects, enhancing the understanding of interactions. Specifically, we disentangle various interaction words from frequency-based anchor words and leverage localized interaction regions from generated images to help the model better capture semantics in distinctive words without extra conditions. Our approach enables the model to accurately understand the intended interaction between humans and objects, producing high-quality images with accurate interactions aligned with specified verbs. Extensive experiments on the HICO-DET dataset demonstrate the effectiveness of our method compared to previous approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the VerbDiff paper:

**Summary:**

The VerbDiff paper addresses the challenge of accurately depicting human-object interactions in text-to-image diffusion models. These models often struggle to differentiate between similar interaction verbs and tend to generate biased interactions based on the frequency of verbs in the training data. VerbDiff proposes two main innovations: Relation Disentanglement Guidance (RDG) and Interaction Direction Guidance (IDG). RDG reduces the bias towards frequent interaction verbs by aligning features of generated images with real images while separating them from features associated with anchor texts (frequent verbs). IDG focuses the model's attention on the interaction region by extracting this region using cross-attention maps and guiding the feature modification within this region. The paper demonstrates through qualitative and quantitative evaluations on the HICO-DET dataset that VerbDiff generates images with more accurate and nuanced human-object interactions compared to existing methods, without requiring additional input conditions such as bounding boxes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel approach to improving interaction awareness in text-to-image diffusion models. The combination of RDG and IDG is unique and offers a plausible solution to the identified problems. Specifically, disentangling the interaction words with frequency-based anchor texts, leveraging localized interaction regions from generated images through cross-attention mechanisms, and then using these interaction regions to guide the generation process shows innovation. These features are not commonly addressed in similar existing work. The creation of a new evaluation metric Sentence-BERT is another strong point, as it better captures the subtle meanings of the sentences.

*   **Significance:** The paper contributes to the field by providing a method that enhances the semantic understanding of text-to-image models, leading to more realistic and accurate image generation. The ability to generate images with nuanced human-object interactions has broad applications in areas such as content creation, virtual reality, and robotics. By not requiring extra conditions such as bounding boxes, VerbDiff offers a more user-friendly approach. The VQA-Score is important for visual understanding of the scene with complex compositional prompts.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem of interaction bias and the limitations of existing methods.
    *   The proposed approach is well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive and provide compelling evidence of the effectiveness of VerbDiff. The qualitative results demonstrate improved visual realism and interaction accuracy.
    *   The quantitative results on the HICO-DET dataset show significant improvements in HOI classification accuracy compared to existing methods. The Ablation study helps provide the effectiveness of each module.
    *   The presentation of the paper is clear and well-organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The computational cost and efficiency are not adequately discussed. Training and inference times are important considerations for practical applications.
    *   While the HICO-DET dataset is a good choice, it might be beneficial to show results on more diverse datasets to check for generalizability.
    *   The improvements in the VQA score appear relatively modest. The reliance on cross attention maps could be problematic if the model struggles with object recognition in the first place. Also, the paper mentions the number of humans within an image being miscounted at times, making the paper not being robust across multiple scenes.

*   **Potential Influence:** VerbDiff has the potential to influence future research on text-to-image generation by highlighting the importance of interaction awareness and providing a practical approach to address this challenge. Other researcher could use this for diverse applications, especially on visual and robotic perception.

**Justification for the score:**

Based on the novelty and significance of the contributions, along with the strengths and weaknesses discussed above, I assign the paper a score of 7.

The paper presents a solid contribution by addressing a significant problem in text-to-image generation and proposing a novel solution with convincing experimental results. The relative high novelty and significance are countered by the somewhat moderate improvements on existing methods and certain lack of analysis on complex scenarios.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### DreamTexture: Shape from Virtual Texture with Analysis by Augmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16412v1)
- **Authors**: Ananta R. Bhattarai, Xingzhe He, Alla Sheffer, Helge Rhodin
- **Abstract**: DreamFusion established a new paradigm for unsupervised 3D reconstruction from virtual views by combining advances in generative models and differentiable rendering. However, the underlying multi-view rendering, along with supervision from large-scale generative models, is computationally expensive and under-constrained. We propose DreamTexture, a novel Shape-from-Virtual-Texture approach that leverages monocular depth cues to reconstruct 3D objects. Our method textures an input image by aligning a virtual texture with the real depth cues in the input, exploiting the inherent understanding of monocular geometry encoded in modern diffusion models. We then reconstruct depth from the virtual texture deformation with a new conformal map optimization, which alleviates memory-intensive volumetric representations. Our experiments reveal that generative models possess an understanding of monocular shape cues, which can be extracted by augmenting and aligning texture cues -- a novel monocular reconstruction paradigm that we call Analysis by Augmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DreamTexture paper:

**Summary:**

The DreamTexture paper presents a novel approach to unsupervised 3D reconstruction from a single image. It leverages the inherent geometric understanding encoded in pre-trained generative models by introducing "Analysis by Augmentation" (AbA). The core idea involves augmenting a virtual texture onto the input image and optimizing the depth map by aligning the real depth cues with the virtual texture using Score Distillation Sampling (SDS).  It achieves this via a two-stage process: first, aligning texture coordinates using SDS and then optimizing depth via a conformal map optimization based on Least Squares Conformal Mapping (LSCM).  The method avoids costly volumetric rendering (NeRF, Gaussian Splatting) and does not require 3D supervision. Experiments show that DreamTexture excels where multi-view methods struggle due to a lack of image features and can handle textured and textureless objects.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Analysis by Augmentation (AbA):** The central concept of using virtual texture augmentation to extract shape cues from pre-trained image priors *is* novel.  Existing methods rely on multi-view consistency or explicit shape priors. AbA presents a different reconstruction paradigm.
*   **Shape from Virtual Texture (SVT):** Inverting shape-from-texture is a clever idea.  Traditionally, SfT requires a *real*, regularly textured object, which is limiting.  DreamTexture creates the texture and uses it to infer shape, expanding the applicability to textureless or irregularly textured objects.
*   **Conformal Map Optimization:** While LSCM itself isn't new, its *re-purposing* for depth optimization, where it's used to minimize 2D-to-3D deformation derived from aligned texture coordinates, adds to the novelty.

**Significance:**

*   **Efficiency:** Avoiding NeRFs or Gaussian Splatting *is* significant. The method promises more efficient 3D reconstruction. Results show a much smaller number of trainable parameters and shorter training times than DreamFusion or RealFusion.
*   **Single-View Reconstruction:** The potential to reconstruct 3D shapes from single images *without* 3D supervision *is* a valuable contribution. This opens new avenues for downstream tasks.
*   **Addressing Limitations of Multi-View Methods:** The paper correctly identifies that multi-view methods can struggle with featureless regions. The ability to work when multi-view methods fail makes it valuable and potentially significant.
*   **Generative Model Understanding:** By showcasing how generative models understand monocular cues and how they can be extracted, the paper contributes to a deeper understanding of how generative models encode the 3D world.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Detail:** As pointed out by the authors, relying solely on virtual texture cues can result in a lack of finer details in the reconstruction, particularly for complex objects.  This limits the fidelity achievable.
*   **Concave/Convex Ambiguity:** The method struggles with concave surfaces. This is a well-known limitation in single-view geometry inference and shape from shading.
*   **Reliance on Generative Priors:**  Performance *is* heavily dependent on the quality and bias of the pre-trained generative model.  If the prior is weak or biased against certain shapes, the reconstruction will suffer. The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of how different priors influence the results.  It doesn't address the potential for hallucinations that can arise from generative priors.
*   **Qualitative Evaluation Predominance:**  The quantitative evaluation is somewhat limited, focusing on synthetic data. More in-depth quantitative analysis on real-world datasets, even with imperfect ground truth, would strengthen the paper. The uncurated samples evaluation is helpful, but more rigorous statistical analysis of the results would be beneficial.
*   **Lack of Detailed Comparison:** While the paper compares against DreamFusion and RealFusion, a deeper analysis of *why* DreamTexture performs better in specific scenarios (e.g., textureless objects) is missing.
*   **Sensitivity to Hyperparameters:** The paper mentions specific hyperparameter choices but lacks a detailed analysis of how these impact the results. A sensitivity analysis would improve reproducibility and provide insights into the robustness of the method.

**Potential Influence:**

DreamTexture offers a fresh perspective on unsupervised 3D reconstruction. AbA represents a general framework that can be extended beyond textures. If proven robust and scalable, it could influence future research in 3D reconstruction, generative modeling, and augmented reality.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper introduces a novel and promising approach to a challenging problem.  It offers clear advantages in efficiency and applicability compared to existing methods.  While the method has limitations (especially detail and concavity), its core concept and efficient implementation provide valuable contributions to the field. However, the limited detail and the dependence on generative priors somewhat lower the score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Tokenize Image as a Set
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16425v1)
- **Authors**: Zigang Geng, Mengde Xu, Han Hu, Shuyang Gu
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes a fundamentally new paradigm for image generation through set-based tokenization and distribution modeling. Unlike conventional methods that serialize images into fixed-position latent codes with a uniform compression ratio, we introduce an unordered token set representation to dynamically allocate coding capacity based on regional semantic complexity. This TokenSet enhances global context aggregation and improves robustness against local perturbations. To address the critical challenge of modeling discrete sets, we devise a dual transformation mechanism that bijectively converts sets into fixed-length integer sequences with summation constraints. Further, we propose Fixed-Sum Discrete Diffusion--the first framework to simultaneously handle discrete values, fixed sequence length, and summation invariance--enabling effective set distribution modeling. Experiments demonstrate our method's superiority in semantic-aware representation and generation quality. Our innovations, spanning novel representation and modeling strategies, advance visual generation beyond traditional sequential token paradigms. Our code and models are publicly available at https://github.com/Gengzigang/TokenSet.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new paradigm for image generation called "TokenSet," which moves away from the traditional approach of serializing images into fixed-position latent codes. Instead, it proposes representing images as unordered sets of tokens, dynamically allocating coding capacity based on the semantic complexity of different regions. To model these discrete sets, the authors develop a "dual transformation" mechanism that converts sets into fixed-length integer sequences with summation constraints.  They also introduce "Fixed-Sum Discrete Diffusion," a diffusion-based framework designed to handle discrete values, fixed sequence length, and summation invariance simultaneously.  Experiments are conducted to demonstrate the method's superior semantic representation and generation quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of representing images as unordered sets of tokens is indeed novel. It addresses a fundamental limitation of existing tokenization methods that uniformly allocate coding capacity regardless of semantic complexity. The dual transformation and Fixed-Sum Discrete Diffusion are also innovative technical contributions aimed at tackling the challenges posed by set-based image representation.
*   **Semantic Awareness:** The TokenSet approach demonstrates the capacity to dynamically allocate tokens based on semantic importance, potentially leading to more efficient image representations. This is a significant advantage over methods with fixed spatial correspondence.
*   **Robustness:** The inherent permutation invariance offers robustness to local perturbations, which is a desirable property for image generation.
*   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper provides a well-defined pipeline, from tokenization to distribution modeling, with clear technical details.  The experiments are well-designed and address key aspects of the proposed method.
* **Thorough experimentation:** The paper presents ablations and visualizations that convincingly demonstrate the advantages of their set-based representation. Quantitative evaluation using rFID and gFID provides evidence of the superior performance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:**  The dual transformation and Fixed-Sum Discrete Diffusion introduce added complexity. While justified, it is important to consider the computational overhead compared to simpler tokenization and modeling methods.
*   **Scalability:** The paper's experimentation is primarily focused on ImageNet. It would be useful to see the results on higher resolution images, especially regarding the ability of the "Fixed-Sum Discrete Diffusion" method to scale to larger token sets and codebook sizes.
*   **Limited Baselines for Set Modeling:** While the paper adequately covers image tokenization, the discussion and comparison to existing set modeling approaches is somewhat limited. A more in-depth analysis of the strengths and weaknesses of alternative set modeling techniques would strengthen the paper.
*   **Lack of Code Availability:** Though the paper claims that code and models will be available, there are numerous typos in the URL link. This needs to be fixed.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of image generation by providing a new direction for image representation and distribution modeling. The concept of dynamically allocating coding capacity based on semantic complexity could inspire new architectures and algorithms that are more efficient and robust. The proposed techniques are not limited to image generation and could be potentially applied to other domains involving set-structured data, such as point clouds or graphs. However, more evidence of broader generalizability is needed.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents novel concepts and encouraging experimental results, some limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score. The complexity of the framework, the potential scalability issues, and the need for more in-depth comparisons to other set modeling methods restrain its impact. The approach marks a notable shift in thinking and offers compelling avenues for future research, the lack of publicly available code holds the paper back.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### XAttention: Block Sparse Attention with Antidiagonal Scoring
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16428v1)
- **Authors**: Ruyi Xu, Guangxuan Xiao, Haofeng Huang, Junxian Guo, Song Han
- **Abstract**: Long-Context Transformer Models (LCTMs) are vital for real-world applications but suffer high computational costs due to attention's quadratic complexity. Block-sparse attention mitigates this by focusing computation on critical regions, yet existing methods struggle with balancing accuracy and efficiency due to costly block importance measurements. In this paper, we introduce XAttention, a plug-and-play framework that dramatically accelerates long-context inference in Transformers models using sparse attention. XAttention's key innovation is the insight that the sum of antidiagonal values (i.e., from the lower-left to upper-right) in the attention matrix provides a powerful proxy for block importance. This allows for precise identification and pruning of non-essential blocks, resulting in high sparsity and dramatically accelerated inference. Across comprehensive evaluations on demanding long-context benchmarks-including RULER and LongBench for language, VideoMME for video understanding, and VBench for video generation. XAttention achieves accuracy comparable to full attention while delivering substantial computational gains. We demonstrate up to 13.5x acceleration in attention computation. These results underscore XAttention's ability to unlock the practical potential of block sparse attention, paving the way for scalable and efficient deployment of LCTMs in real-world applications. Code is available at https://github.com/mit-han-lab/x-attention.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces XAttention, a novel block-sparse attention mechanism designed to accelerate long-context Transformers (LCTMs). XAttention leverages the sum of antidiagonal values within attention matrix blocks as a proxy for block importance. This allows for efficient identification and pruning of non-essential blocks, leading to increased sparsity and faster inference. The method is evaluated on various long-context benchmarks across language and video domains, showing comparable accuracy to full attention with significant computational gains (up to 13.5x speedup in attention computation). The authors also introduce dynamic threshold prediction to further optimize sparsity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using antidiagonal sums as a proxy for block importance is reasonably novel. While other block-sparse attention methods exist, this approach presents a simpler and potentially more efficient way to identify important blocks compared to methods that rely on more complex token pooling or search algorithms.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the practical acceleration of LCTMs. The quadratic complexity of attention is a bottleneck in deploying these models. XAttention aims to address this by providing a method that reduces computation without substantial accuracy loss. The reported speedups on various benchmarks are compelling. If it generalizes well and can be easily integrated into existing transformer architectures, it has the potential to impact the deployment of LCTMs in real-world applications, especially in resource-constrained environments.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Simplicity and Efficiency:** The antidiagonal scoring method is simple to implement and computationally efficient, reducing the overhead associated with block importance measurement. This is a key advantage over some existing approaches.

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper demonstrates strong empirical results across diverse tasks (language, video understanding, video generation) and models (Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, Qwen2-VL-7B, Hunyuan-Video).

    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the importance of different components of XAttention, particularly the antidiagonal pattern.

    *   **Plug-and-Play:** The authors describe XAttention as a "plug-and-play" framework, suggesting ease of integration into existing transformer architectures.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides an intuitive explanation for why antidiagonal sums work, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of the approximation would strengthen the claims.

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of XAttention might be sensitive to the choice of stride (S) and threshold (T).  While the paper introduces dynamic threshold prediction, further investigation into the robustness of these parameters across different tasks and model sizes is needed.

    *   **Generalizability:** The experiments are limited to the specific models and datasets used in the paper.  More evaluations across different transformer architectures (e.g., encoder-only models, decoder-only models) and broader range of tasks are needed to confirm its generalizability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The method's simplicity and speed make it promising for practical applications where long context and computational efficiency are critical. If the performance holds across different architectures and the 'plug-and-play' integration is straightforward, this could become a widely used technique. The clear experiments in the video generation task also highlight the significance to multimodal areas that demand long contexts.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practically useful method for accelerating long-context Transformers. The strength lies in the simplicity of the antidiagonal scoring approach and the compelling empirical results. However, the lack of deeper theoretical analysis and limited generalizability across different architectures and tasks prevent it from achieving a higher score.  The practical implications are significant, but more rigorous and broader validation is necessary to fully establish its impact on the field. Thus, a score of 7 reflects a solid contribution with strong potential, tempered by the need for more comprehensive analysis and validation.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10
